# FlexPay business architecture and the payout-device catalog

In the week after the FlexSMIB architecture ruling ([[wiki/decisions/2025-04-07-flexsmib-modern-flexport-legacy.md]]), Val opened a deliberate "FlexPay Evolution" strategy thread: he now believes the kiosk-centred business model may be rejected by the Georgia market, and wants JVL to **obsolete its own model before someone else does**, offering a catalog of cheaper payout devices instead of one kiosk. Sessions: Apr 8 strategy ([[wiki/meetings/2025-04-08-flexpay-evolution-strategy.md]]), Apr 8 GAMOA demo recap ([[wiki/meetings/2025-04-08-flexport-gamoa-demo-recap.md]]), Apr 11 "FlexPay Evolution 1" ([[wiki/meetings/2025-04-11-flexpay-evolution-1.md]]). The engineering/dev thread stays on [[wiki/topics/flexpay-mobile-app-and-service-advocates.md]].

## Val's market math (Apr 8) — why the kiosk model may fail

- Georgia has "in access of 6,000 locations"; under the upcoming legislation each would need a payment kiosk "at $12,000 … it's an investment of close to $70 million," plus ~35,000 machines needing printer retrofits "at a cost of, I don't know, $500 per retrofit that's additional something like $17 million." [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]] On Apr 11 Val rounds the market retrofit burden to "closer to a hundred million dollars" and calls a business architecture that forces a $12,000 kiosk on a five-machine location "flawed and possibly will fail." [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
- Operators see no profit in complying — "they will try to do as little as possible to comply." Technically a cashier could pair a ticket number to a card manually with "a primitive application on a PC" using the Incom API — "a much, much, much … cheaper solution … and then our business model is completely ruined." [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]]
- Constraint: the Incom agreement "puts the onus on the individual or the company who signed … to ensure that there is no fraud" — so tickets must be validated against a legitimate machine win (a mini TITO/SAS server), not typed in by hand. [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]] For Mother-Goose-only operators, Val refuses to be the proxy signer: "I will not sign with them. I tell you one hundred percent now that I will not sign." [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]]
- Strategic frame (Apr 11): the big competitors (Light and Wonder, IGT, Aristocrat) "are not making their own kiosks … we're not redesigning our machines"; Val wants JVL to capture the market as a technology leader with "three, four, five variations of the products, many levels of pricing" — "You cannot create a market with one style … A market is created by a catalog of products." Revenue thesis: "the bulk of our revenue will derive not from the sales of hardware, but from reoccurring revenue of processing transactions." [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]] [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]]

## The device variants on the table (Apr 8 + Apr 11)

1. **Cage POS (cheapest, half-automated):** attendant-facing touchscreen + scanner + magnetic card reader/loader in the cage, plus a customer-facing second display; ticket scanned, value moved to a card manually but validated against the mini SAS/TITO server. Rough component talk: "$250 altogether? Yes. Well, maybe even less" (Apr 8); Val's Apr-11 sizing: bill of material "probably … under $1,000 … a unit like this could be sold to the market at about $5,000." (The two figures describe different sketches of the same cage-POS idea — recorded as said, not reconciled.) [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]] [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
2. **Automated dispensing unit / "payout tower":** self-service card-dispensing unit next to the machines — scanner, small customer screen, large-capacity dispenser, mini TITO server inside; Val's Apr-8 cost vision "under 700 dollars, 600 dollars" (component talk: dispenser ~$350, FlexPort ~$100, enclosure ~$60 → "about 500 dollars"), vs FlexPay "considerably more." Marketing model: rent it "at a … dollar per day" on a three-year contract. On Apr 11 it becomes a slim **tower on a pedestal that fits between machines**; two towers per location give master/slave failover — "we already have this technology implemented in our embedded progressive" (Pavel). [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]] [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
3. **In-machine dispenser retrofit:** card dispenser inside each machine, routed through FlexPort — raised for completeness; Val: "Personally, I don't like it"; Juan adds many locations can't even open the master's machine door. [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
4. **Cheap dual-dispenser kiosk:** Pavel's variant — strip bill acceptor and printer from the FlexPay kiosk and mount two large card dispensers ("that's how we planned the kiosk initially" — Val). [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
- **Architecture dispute (open):** Val leaned on FlexPort as the embedded brain of the cheap devices ("it has all the input output … a dedicated team that could deliver the product in isolation"); Pavel dislikes the embedded route — "MBED is a difficult solution … not predictable in terms of development time" — preferring a cheap x86 platform with a 10-inch touchscreen at "the same money." Val: "whatever architectural solution … should really be dictated by you, not by me, Pavel." [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]]
- **Direction picked (Apr 11, not a locked decision):** "I heard what Gennady said. Let's concentrate on the cheapest solution" (the cage POS), enabled by the TITO mini-server: "by the end of the month, we will have a Tito solution … The moment you have mini-server on a location, everything is open to you." New-device delivery talk: "we're talking about September of this year." Goal: "nail the business architecture by the end of this month," with a follow-up Wednesday 11 am ET and use cases written by everyone. [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
- Applicability beyond Georgia (Juan/Val): the POS+TITO solution "is something we could also use in Puerto Rico … And in Nebraska? Yes … and I know for a fact that we could do it in Pennsylvania." [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]

## The GAMOA pulse-security objection (Apr 11)

- Juan relayed via Felipe: **Mike Patel** — rendered "the director of Gomorrah"/"Gomorrah's president" (the GAMOA operators' association; transcript garble) — "will not be buying our kiosk solution because if we offer pulse capture, it is not secure." [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
- Rebuttal assembled on the call: Georgia's legacy **Mother Goose** payout system is itself pulse-based with no GLC mandate to remove it ("your regulator is saying pulses are OK"), and JVL's pulses "live only within a cabinet up to Flexport. We do not move the signal outside the cabinet" (Pavel) — arguably safer than the status quo. Juan to raise it with Patel on his Georgia trip the following week; Val: ask the masters directly "what is your dream solution here? 100%." [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
- (On Apr 8 the engineering side — Pavel by adjacent context, inference — had also floated sniffing the Mother-Goose–era "Gaggle"/"yellow board" RS-485 protocol to validate printed wins on old locations; Val was skeptical — reverse-engineering a third-party protocol JVL doesn't control. Names as transcribed; unclear. [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]])

## GAMOA demo configuration (Apr 8 recap)

- The event is "not a trade show. This is just a Gamoa meeting, two hour thing," maybe ~10 masters attending. Demo: **one JVL machine (Flex-S) with FlexPort and its bill acceptor kept**, connected via a separate router (Felipe's existing stock TP-Link, DHCP defaults) to one FlexPay; show **Diamond Link** software (Juan: "I need to push Diamond Link"); do **not** show a competitor machine, the scanner-only bracket, or TITO. [[raw/flex_port_recap_felipe_juan_pavel-april-8-2025.md]] The Apr-11 call adds: demonstration "next Thursday at 4 p.m." on "the 17th," showing "one V43 machine. With Diamond Link"; a device may need FedEx-ing for port reprogramming. [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
- Hardware state: Felipe's current 3D-printed-plastic unit "needs to go into the garbage" (strip and keep reusable parts); he receives "two brand new units, production units, molded"; the design has capability for two bill-acceptor styles (back load and front load) though no cutouts were made. Ship plan on the call: units ship **Thursday**, arriving **Monday** (an evolution of Apr 7's "tomorrow we must ship"). [[raw/flex_port_recap_felipe_juan_pavel-april-8-2025.md]]

## Temporary-wallet product rules (Apr 8 recap)

- The ticket is a **temporary e-wallet**, not a ticket — Val: "use it as a temporary wallet instead of a ticket. The ticket is just the wrong connotation." **No value is printed on the ticket** ("we do not print any value … It's hidden from the player") because the balance changes as the player moves between machines; the ticket carries **two counters** — playable credits and cashed/winnings value — and **only winnings can be redeemed to the prepaid MasterCard** ("by legislation, you can only convert credits won"). FlexPay shows both values; FlexPort shows only the machine-chargeable value. [[raw/flex_port_recap_felipe_juan_pavel-april-8-2025.md]]
- **Scope gap found — the no-value wallet:** Felipe's use case — a player walks in with "his last and only $20," feeds the machine directly, wins, and has no wallet to carry the winnings. Fix direction: let FlexPay "print a no-value temporary wallet"; Pavel's variant: a "generate a new ticket" button at the machine with a PIN, printing at FlexPay. Open problems: blank-ticket spam at the kiosk, and collisions when the FlexPay is occupied. "That's a scope [gap] that we identified right now … implement it for the beta version testing," not for the show. [[raw/flex_port_recap_felipe_juan_pavel-april-8-2025.md]]
- **Wallet validity period is unsettled:** Pavel mentioned tickets reusable "within 30 days"; Juan pushed back that a prior conversation had said as low as 24 hours (small operators' book-balancing; database size) — "we need feedback from the masters on that one." [[raw/flex_port_recap_felipe_juan_pavel-april-8-2025.md]]
- Player-anonymity stance (Apr 8 strategy): most route players are anonymous and won't give a phone number, so a phone/e-wallet-registration model "I don't see it being a business model, at least from the start" (Val); the missing ingredient is a card usable for betting inside the machines, which "Incom promised" later this year (see [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]]). [[raw/flex_pay_evolution-april-8-2025.md]]

## Incom threads picked up in this series

- **Card-back-into-machine / Visa:** the Georgia COAM market complains that loading winnings on a prepaid MasterCard "takes all this winnings out of the immediate circulation of the gaming business"; Incom "is in talks with Visa" for a card the machine can validate, where Visa's ~3% fee could be made manageable if Incom runs the banking cycle internally — making JVL "a reoccurring partner." [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
- **PCI-compliant optical scanner:** per the last Incom call, the optical card scanner must be PCI-compliant for a future Incom technology; Juan to re-read the notes so the hardware dimensions allow a swap-out. [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]
- "John from Income" reported masters want "the automated solution so that no clerk is involved" — one of the conflicting master-preference signals (locations reportedly want the human hustle). [[raw/evolution_flex_pay-april-11-2025.md]]

## The Apr-16 Genadi one-on-one — tower/POS design & the priority pick

On Wednesday Apr 16, Val worked the device design directly with [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] ([[wiki/meetings/2025-04-16-flexpay-evolution-dispensing-tower.md]]); the headline calls are on [[wiki/decisions/2025-04-16-dispensing-tower-first-magnetic-stripe.md]]. Design detail captured:

- **Two devices to design:** a **POS** (a cage unit + an attendant-facing device on the desk) and the **card-dispensing tower**. The tower can be a cage-internal customer-facing unit driven by the POS, or a **free-standing self-serve tower** — powered by mains on one side, CAT6 from the router on the other. It dispenses **cards only** (TITO format), reading the card's magnetic stripe; each machine already has SMIB or FlexPort to the router. [[raw/fkex_pay_evolution-apr-16-2025.md]]
- **Dispense flow:** read the TITO ticket (e.g. "$100"), dispense a blank card, read its number, pair number + value via InComm, get confirmation, re-read the stripe/QR, confirm the value, dispense. [[raw/fkex_pay_evolution-apr-16-2025.md]]
- **LED vs screen cost debate (resolved toward a small screen):** a FlexPortal touchscreen display "costs $20"; a larger 12–14-inch touchscreen "sandwich … about $60" plus a GUI to write; LED bulbs are "cents … maybe $2" but need a controller that erases the saving. Val settled on the small **7-inch FlexPort screen** in front, on a cheap embedded platform (one serial port + CAT5). Pavel had resisted embedded for the **POS** (many screens, heavy support) but the tower has only 3–5 screens, so embedded fits. [[raw/fkex_pay_evolution-apr-16-2025.md]]
- **Enclosure:** ~10–11 in wide, height of a computer tower, ~500-card capacity in one width (no parallel stacking), gray/milky plastic (no lighting), three-part shell (two cheeks + a front mold, no metal frame), front-loading extractable mechanism as on FlexPay. [[raw/fkex_pay_evolution-apr-16-2025.md]]
- **Optical-scanner question closed:** the TITO-ticket barcode can be read after the manufacturer reprograms a setting; Pavel tested a black/white inversion and "then he reads everything." Val: "I'm taking it off the agenda." (This is separate from the card reader, which stays magnetic-stripe-only.) [[raw/fkex_pay_evolution-apr-16-2025.md]]
- **Roadmap & market:** priority order = finish JVL business model → TITO architecture (SMIB + FlexPort + Local TITO Server) → dispensing tower → FlexPOS; tower work goes to [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]] + [[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Yavorski]]. Targeted at South Dakota, Nebraska, Puerto Rico; JVL follows InComm (Alabama, Texas). Legacy non-FlexPort machines (~3,000 of ~35,000) deprioritized. Rent-not-sell / recurring-revenue model (see decision). [[raw/fkex_pay_evolution-apr-16-2025.md]]
- Val floated handing the whole tower project to someone else (perhaps Evgeny's ex-cash-handling mechanics) to free Genadi for the Flex-S machines, but "отдать на сторону проект немножко скерри мне" — with control, not abandonment. [[raw/fkex_pay_evolution-apr-16-2025.md]]

## Apr 23 — architecture evolution (Juan/Pavel/Gennady/Val)

A long English architecture meeting ([[wiki/meetings/2025-04-23-flexport-smib-tito-architecture-evolution.md]]) worked the device architecture into a clearer shape; the decision-grade rulings (SMIB scope, the standalone TITO-server product) are on [[wiki/decisions/2025-04-07-flexsmib-modern-flexport-legacy.md]]. Design/strategy detail: [[raw/evolution_flex_port-apr-23-2025.md]]

- **Barcode readability dispute (action item, not resolved on the call):** Pavel thinks the optical scanner needs **~15–20 cm** reading distance for the long central barcode a third-party printer produces; Gennady measured **~10–12 cm** available and says 10 cm suffices. The scanner was chosen at "**above 50 degrees**" wide angle (the maximum). Undertakings: **Pavel** to verify the actual minimum distance and send Gennady the QR settings to reconfigure the reader; **Gennady** to check readability on **FlexPay** (its scanner-to-glass distance is a fixed 10 cm, sized for a card not a long ticket — may need a bigger opening). A lens/prism cap to widen the angle was floated as a fallback. [[raw/evolution_flex_port-apr-23-2025.md]]
- **One experience per location (reaffirmed, with a consequence):** a location runs **either** the digital/temporary-wallet model **or** TITO, never both — "you can't have digital wallet and Tito in the same building." The consequence Val drew out: in the **digital-wallet** model, since a modern IGT machine's cash-out prints only a plain central barcode (not JVL's scannable retain-and-reuse ticket), **every machine in the location needs a FlexPort with a scanner** so the player scans-and-keeps the wallet at each game — otherwise the wallet is lost. Which answered the standing SMIB question for that case: "do we actually need a SMIB or is it just a flex port that does it all? — **Just a flex port.**" [[raw/evolution_flex_port-apr-23-2025.md]]
- **Kiosk-not-first (evolution of thinking):** Val explicitly revised the original model — "one of the drawbacks… was, you needed to **start your play at the kiosk**. And that was a negative. We want to **start a player at the machine**," keeping the kiosk as an alternative, not the primary touchpoint. [[raw/evolution_flex_port-apr-23-2025.md]]
- **Two POS products:** **POS #1** = the **FlexPay top panel** driven by the regular FlexPay CPU (the CPU stays in the base unit; the top just becomes a point-of-sale surface). **POS #2** = a **FlexPort-based** unit — the 7-inch screen/touchscreen/board "sandwich" turned **vertical** in a plastic enclosure, mounted on a **$30 Amazon suction-cup arm** or an L-bracket. Val's worry: the FlexPay-derived POS is "the world's largest POS device" versus the **$350 tiny store POS** JVL competes with on a crowded gas-station counter; the interim fix is the L-bracket to stand it vertically while a smaller FlexPort POS is designed. The bill acceptor uses the **ID003** protocol (transcribed "ID03"); the FlexPort printer is driven over **RS232** (USB reserved for logs/firmware — "USB in… industrial application is not such a good interface"). [[raw/evolution_flex_port-apr-23-2025.md]]
- **Where the local TITO server lives (the long debate → a decision):** Val floated a standalone/cloud server; Pavel argued firmly for **Linux over the STM32/embedded route** (STM has "a lot of issues related to network communications… size of memory to write logs"; embedded code is "one monolithic software block file" vs modular, well-tested Linux; the Java/Linux code is reusable; more Linux specialists exist — Toronto, Minsk, Pavel himself). Reliability framing: the server is the **single point of failure for the whole location** ("when we have a CPU failure, one machine goes down… all nine machines go down" — max **9 FlexPort devices** per location), so a **99.5%-uptime + auto-recover** requirement, wired (not Wi-Fi) connection, minimal cooling (it only does "100 or 500 transactions per day"). The current cheap amusement board ("Kopini/Kardini", a "low-grade Chinese manufacturer") was judged unreliable; prefer a fanless industrial board (Gennady suggested **Tatang**) with NVMe + memory (~$120), "not… placing price tag as a criteria." **Resolution:** move the TITO server onto **its own standalone industrial-Linux hardware, presented as a separate product called "TITO server"** (see decision). [[raw/evolution_flex_port-apr-23-2025.md]]
- **Dispenser hardware:** the card-dispensing tower will use **embedded (STM32-based) hardware similar to FlexPort**; when a location has a dispenser but no FlexPay, the TITO-server CPU box can be placed inside the dispenser enclosure (a separate CPU from the dispenser's own). [[raw/evolution_flex_port-apr-23-2025.md]]

## Apr 24 2025 — server "in a box" locked; dispenser/POS design converges

The next-day continuation ([[wiki/meetings/2025-04-24-flexpay-server-dispenser-pos-evolution.md]]) turned the Apr-23 direction into concrete decisions. [[raw/evolution_flex-pay_pos_server-apr-24-2025.md]]

- **Standalone "server in a box" — Val's final verdict** (see [[wiki/decisions/2025-04-24-standalone-server-in-a-box.md]]): a Linux industrial board (3.5"×5", fanless, ~$120–130, ~10-yr life, "like our v1000 board, low performance"), mounted outside the machine like a router, **Ethernet-only** (never hardwired to FlexPay), in a deliberately oversized **Cisco-looking** enclosure. It runs **three** server apps — **JVL accounting host, Incom API, TITO server** (evolving the Apr-23 single-TITO-server framing). FlexPay stays on the machine CPU and just needs a **slave mode**. Samples: 4 to Toronto, 2 to Minsk. [[raw/evolution_flex-pay_pos_server-apr-24-2025.md]]
- **Dispensing tower:** plastic enclosure on a metal pedestal, driven by FlexPort hardware + an embedded **STM** solution; scans a presented ticket → local server → Incom validate → dispenses a card, shown on a player-facing screen at ~45°, ~8" wide × ~10–11" deep. **Stacker capacity raised to 450 cards** (from 300/320). A cheaper/better dispenser brand chosen (~**$320 vs $375**, more capacity). A **vertical magnetic swipe reader** is built in; the optical scanner reads only tickets, not cards. [[raw/evolution_flex-pay_pos_server-apr-24-2025.md]]
- **Detachable POS:** the top of the dispensing tower detaches to become the POS — a **mirror-image/symmetrical** design so a screen can face cashier or player (or the whole unit swivels 180°), mounted on an Amazon swivel/movable stand (legs ≥10–15 cm). A player-facing screen is required because the player must acknowledge the **$2 service fee**. [[raw/evolution_flex-pay_pos_server-apr-24-2025.md]]
- **FlexPort main-board revision for production:** one main board common to FlexPort / dispenser / POS — **three RS-232 COM ports** (dispenser, scanner, reader), Ethernet, USB, and a **wide-range adjustable-voltage power connector on the main board** (dispensers run 24V, FlexPort today 12V), dropping the bill-acceptor interface board. [[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Eugene Yavorski]] does the main-port production update. A future maximal FlexPort at the machine could scan/encode cards and act as its own dispenser; **reloadable cards** become allowed "a year from now." [[raw/evolution_flex-pay_pos_server-apr-24-2025.md]]
- Next meeting May 2. [[raw/evolution_flex-pay_pos_server-apr-24-2025.md]]

## May 1–2 2025 — tower/POS industrial design converges on "no detachable head"

Two [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] ↔ [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] design calls reviewing [[wiki/people/igor-starovoytov.md|Igor Starovoytov]]'s industrial-design proposal — the [[wiki/meetings/2025-05-01-flex-tower-spatial-design-review.md|May-1 spatial review]] and the [[wiki/meetings/2025-05-02-flex-tower-pos-form-fit-design.md|May-2 POS form/fit]] (the "next meeting May 2" from Apr-24). Both transcripts internally duplicate (§12) — facts taken once.

- **Tower anatomy (as proposed):** a **housing** containing only the dispenser (available in two sizes), a **removable head** as the main unit (holding the FlexPort electronics — the screen + touchscreen "sandwich" — plus an **optical scanner** to read any-shape barcodes), and an **optional pedestal** for the floor. Width discussion: currently **8 inches**, Val comfortable widening to **~10 inches** ("no strategic benefit" to saving width) with the credit-card swipe moved beside the screen inside the head; Genadi thought 10" felt like "a suitcase" (expected 6–7"). [[raw/flex_tower_spacial_design_review-may-1-2025.md]]
- **Unify FlexPort to drive both tower and POS.** Val wants the FlexPort brought to a state where the **identical** electronics/screen/insert serve both the tower head and a standalone POS — the extra cost to make FlexPort do both is minimal, and he doesn't want a separate hardware revision. FlexPort is "the main brains/engine." Only the **main board** goes in the tower head; the **third interface board** (bill-acceptor + extras) is left out there. [[raw/flex_tower_spacial_design_review-may-1-2025.md]]
- **The design resolution — drop the detachable head.** A head that detaches to become a convertible POS needs special **"floating" (self-mating) connectors**, guides and mounting — complex, costly, slow. Genadi warned that path means redesigning **three Flex-S machines + the tower dispenser + a separate POS**, which "we'd only come out with by the end of next year." Val's fix: **do not make a detachable head.** Build the **POS separately** from the same electronics/screen/insert, just wrapped in **"two hoods"** (top + bottom plastic covers), skipping the adapter boards and sliding connectors entirely — cheaper and faster. Mounting: the **back cover** carries the stand/leg; connectors (Ethernet, power, maybe USB for service) exit the side or back/bottom. [[raw/flex_tower_spacial_design_review-may-1-2025.md]]
- **Screen: rotate, don't flip (May 2).** For a POS that must serve both attendant and player, the screen should **rotate ~180° horizontally in the plane parallel to the table** (a "twist"), **not flip** — a flip would need an **accelerometer** and an ugly mid-redraw. Keep the device **thin** ("rotate a piece of paper, not a brick"). Orientation rule: **horizontal** reads better when the unit sits **on a stand**; **vertical** suits a unit held **in the hand**. Initial builds can ship with **one screen + a pivot**; full POS-by-rules needs two screens (attendant-facing + a player info screen). [[raw/flex_tower_pos_form_fit_design-may-2-2025.md]]
- **Next steps:** Val to hand Igor Starovoytov all the models so he draws over the weekend; Val had a **6-hour design session with Igor** that evening (May 2). A candidate off-the-shelf POS **bracket at $77** was noted as interesting. [[raw/flex_tower_spacial_design_review-may-1-2025.md]] [[raw/flex_tower_pos_form_fit_design-may-2-2025.md]]

## May 6 2025 — tower hardware "make it a product," speed over polish

A [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] ↔ [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] hardware working call ([[wiki/meetings/2025-05-06-flex-tower-hardware-design.md]]; transcript internally duplicates — facts taken once). Genadi is picking components (power supply, filter, **card reader still not received**) and finishing **speaker/acoustics** selection for the Flex (needs a rapid prototype so the acoustician can take a final volume measurement). [[wiki/people/igor-starovoytov.md|Igor Starovoytov]] did little this round at Val's request. Genadi's job now: "make it a product" — needs ~**4 days** to immerse and push it fast; **Tower first, POS second** (Tower can work standalone; the POS needs more work); **mass-production readiness** for ~six months of orders. [[raw/flex_port_hardware_design_evolution-may-6-2025.md]]

- **Enclosure/industrial-design calls:** body + back wall in **metal**, front **door in plastic** (cheaper, complex shape); a **removable head** holding the electronics that assembles separately then **slides in and fixes under the door** (two dispenser interface wires run down the back wall); make the machine **fully symmetric top/bottom** and slightly narrower/streamlined — **drop the reverse-angle undercut** that complicates the mold; the scanner becomes a **separate small plastic part** pointing down. **Speed is paramount** — "делайте самые простые формы… делайте это быстро"; dimensions unimportant (±2 cm "никого это не убьет"). [[raw/flex_port_hardware_design_evolution-may-6-2025.md]]
- **Lighting over labels:** replace the printed "swipe card" label with an **embossed icon**; **backlight the card-slot ribs** (green indicator) so it's usable in a dark location; light the card-exit bezel (self-lit by the dispensing device); a scan-sign area with an **embossed, blinking barcode** rather than a sticker. This requires adding **RGB support / an interface provision to the FlexPort electronics** — waiting on [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]] to make the change fit **all devices (port, tower, POS)**. [[raw/flex_port_hardware_design_evolution-may-6-2025.md]]
- **Card tray:** much thicker/robust with a semicircular grip. **Front (frontal) service** because the unit stands between machines. **Tilt angle is fixed** (scanner mounted parallel) — no vertical orientation in this first release; fine for now. [[raw/flex_port_hardware_design_evolution-may-6-2025.md]]
- **Pedestal/footing:** the pedestal is a plain "black box" (not fancy); a flat metal **footing plate** (like the Countertop pedestal's) usable under both the machine and the pedestal, or two perpendicular metal strips as a cross-foot; the foot extends slightly forward (and back if free-standing) for stability when the dispenser slides out. A **lazy-susan/carousel** with **stepped (clicking) ~100° rotation** was floated for the foot. [[raw/flex_port_hardware_design_evolution-may-6-2025.md]]
- **Dispenser order + supply:** the dispensing devices weren't shipped on time (returned after a 3-day location trial); order split **2 to Minsk, 3 to Toronto** ([[wiki/people/mikhail-perelman.md|Misha]] placing it, payment in) — the **cheaper ~450-card** unit; one extra for Alekseev's development ("довольно большой" payment on **5 units**). A **standalone server** still needs building separately, with electronics changes for it; the industrial boards were ordered but not shipped (vendor went on the **May holidays**) — expected this week. [[raw/flex_port_hardware_design_evolution-may-6-2025.md]]
- **Staffing:** "**Sasha**" to help Genadi; **Flex Curve** work paused to prioritize the tower. [[raw/flex_port_hardware_design_evolution-may-6-2025.md]]

## May 12 2025 — FlexPay decomposed as a capability (product-discovery worked example)

On the May-12 product-discovery teaching session ([[wiki/meetings/2025-05-12-product-discovery-double-diamond-aaron.md]]) [[wiki/people/aaron.md|Aaron]] and [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] used **FlexPay** as the running example to demonstrate breaking a product idea into capabilities → use cases → requirements → features. Durable product content (the process theory itself is on [[wiki/decisions/2025-04-03-jira-capability-level-and-github-mapping.md]]): [[raw/theory_product_discovery_process-may-12-2025.md]]

- **The core capability** was refined from "flexible payment processing **system**" to a verb-phrased capability: **"able to send and receive funds easily and without disruption"** (Val: "system is not a capability"). Supporting/adjacent capabilities mapped out: **the machine transacts multiple payment formats**; **pays out in the player-preferred format**; **accepts funds** (two-way); **the player can view credits easily and without disruption**; and machine-side hardware capabilities — **receive/accept physical currency bills via a bill recycler** and **receive/accept voucher (TITO) tickets**. Aaron split these into **player-oriented** vs **machine-layer** capabilities. [[raw/theory_product_discovery_process-may-12-2025.md]]
- **Payment methods in scope:** cash, coins, **AFT** (Val corrected "EFT" to "AFT" — covers most electronic methods), **Zelle** (kept only to show a unique transfer), and vouchers; credit-card/PayPal/coupons were removed as redundant. "Without disruption" = ergonomic, player-facing dashboard; the player never leaves their chair; everything shows as **credits** on the machine. [[raw/theory_product_discovery_process-may-12-2025.md]]
- **Bill recycler = Cash Code.** For physically sending/receiving currency, Val referenced the **bill-to-bill (B2B) recycler** — a device that accepts bills and dispenses bills in multiple denominations via recycling cassettes — and noted he **once owned a company called Cash Code** that developed exactly this device; **JCM** makes a similar one. [[raw/theory_product_discovery_process-may-12-2025.md]]
- **Sample user story** (feature level): "As a player, I want to insert cash into a machine so I can start playing games immediately." [[raw/theory_product_discovery_process-may-12-2025.md]]

## May 15 2025 — tower electromechanical detailing; three new sensors; universal main board

A [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] ↔ [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] design review ([[wiki/meetings/2025-05-15-flex-tower-hardware-design-review.md]]; transcript internally duplicates — §12, facts taken once). The dispensing tower is now at **electromechanical detailing** (concept and spatial planning done; parts split agreed with "Sasha"; FlexPort interfaces broken out for [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]]); Genadi is waiting on **swipe-reader and dispenser samples** to finalize interfaces/dimensions. The §3 continuation of the May-1/2/6 tower thread. [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]

- **Surface finishes:** the top surface's **inner field textured/rough**, the outer material **smooth/polished** (concave). The black table part will be **textured**, not **piano black** (piano black shows fingerprints/scratches). [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]
- **The ticket-slot handle:** [[wiki/people/igor-starovoytov.md|Igor]] wants it trapezoidal and prominent; make it **clear plastic with a texture underneath** so the scanner's red laser refracts and glows (no printed text or sticker needed — just an **embossed bi-directional arrow**); use an **ABS mix (not PC)** so it doesn't crack; JVL logo embossed on top. The internal swipe slot is **backlit two-colour red/green** (prior decision); the card-exit slot illumination is still **unresolved pending the reader sample** (it must blink when a card exits). [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]
- **Dispenser drawer:** pulls fully out on rails like the countertops; whole assembly pre-assembled and screw-mounted. Add a **mechanical click/detent** so it can't sit misaligned, plus a **sensor that reports "dispenser is misaligned"** back to FlexPort (Val: "mature people don't [design] like that… I want people to say nobody does it like JVL"). [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]
- **Three new sensor requirements captured:** a **door-open sensor**, a **dispenser-position sensor**, and a **card-level sensor** (at least two sensors then count dispensed cards, with a low-card **Tilt** indication; plus counting **rejection-tray** cards) — "we must always know the card level." [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]
- **Metal enclosure for volume manufacturing:** a single **U-shape part** (mirrored), front plastic screwed on, back wall attached with **tapping screws or rivets**; **source it ready-made from China** (split the back sheet down the middle with an overlapped seam to save material) and go **straight to stamps/molds** — because the unit is a **requirement to build in high volume, fast** ("no time for assembly… it has to come off the stamp"), unlike the machines JVL assembles over years. [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]
- **New FlexPort main-board revision for [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]]:** add **scanner and dispenser interfaces on the main board**, put the power supply on it; dispensers run **24V**, so FlexPort takes 24V in and **steps down to 5V on the main board** → one universal board. Make a **test bed + test points with a harness to a laptop** for programming/checking (written as a requirement; Alekseev already lays test points). [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]
- **Priority order (Val):** the FlexPort faceplate is **not** the priority — the **POS** is, then the **Flex-Server / TITO-server box** ("things we can't operate without"). Genadi hands the plastics work to Sasha tomorrow and switches to the **Flex Curve acoustics** (has the acoustician's data; needs to source speakers and build a rapid model to finish the audio-box design — currently blocked). [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]
- **Industrial board to order:** the board Genadi found is a **3.5-inch** complete computer (heat-sink ready) that suits FlexPay, countertops and the server; **Pavel to receive four units — three low-cost (Celeron) + one ~$100 higher-performance** unit that can run graphics. Genadi to design an **enclosure symbiotic with the Flex Tower** (circular JVL-logo design). This extends the Apr-24 "server in a box" (see [[wiki/decisions/2025-04-24-standalone-server-in-a-box.md]]) and the Apr-23 industrial-board discussion. [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]
- **Scheduling:** next tower/POS/server talk **next week** (meeting Tuesday); Genadi available Mon/Tue/Wed only; Alekseev reachable **weekends or 7 am** (7 am slots the 19th or 21st — prefer the 19th; Monday the 19th is a Belarus holiday, "Intellectual day"). [[raw/Flex_tower_hardware_design_review-may-15-2025.md]]

## May 16 2025 — FlexPay commissioning plan (in-house + location life tests)

A [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] ↔ [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] session (Irina named as an information recipient) laying out how JVL will **commission FlexPay** — take the live card-payout system from lab to a real location. Two ordered milestones. ("Коммишининг" = commissioning/bring-up, not operator commission economics.) [[raw/commission_flex-pay-may-16-2025.md]]

- **Milestone 1 — In-House (life) Commissioning** at the **Georgia office:** connect one FlexPay machine plus a couple of machines with FlexPort (one with a single SAS port, one with two SAS ports) and run a real card-issue test. Val insists a genuine **In-House Full/Life Test** means money actually moving **through the Incom bank into the JVL account** and reflected on all reports — i.e. using **Incom's production environment** with an assigned location ID, opening a location, running a transaction, and confirming the dollar lands ("better to suffer over $1 or $10 than $10,000"). [[raw/commission_flex-pay-may-16-2025.md]]
- **Milestone 2 — Location (Outside) Commissioning:** the same type of live test, but installed **on an actual location**. Only after In-House passes. [[raw/commission_flex-pay-may-16-2025.md]]
- **Pavel owns the Incom relationship now.** Val is stepping out as intermediary — **Pavel** must drive all Incom emails/calls/face-to-face sessions and report status **proactively** to Val and Irina. Incom is "a $6-billion company that moves at the speed of the slowest turtle" (replies ~a week later), so every message needs a **deadline + strong follow-up**. [[raw/commission_flex-pay-may-16-2025.md]]
- **Bill-validator requirements (into the spec):** FlexPay must work with **JCM UBA, JCM iVision, and MEI** validators; on the **machine** side any bill validator works (connected in the machine, not JVL's problem if it breaks), but the **FlexPay** unit itself needs only **two** validators for now — **JCM UBA or MEI** (no iVision on FlexPay). iVision has no JVL **receptacle/connector to the bill acceptor** yet (Gena was preparing wires; an Evgeny must verify software) — but operators will often just splice wires, so what matters is offering the **choice**. **MEI** support is scheduled for ~**June**. [[raw/commission_flex-pay-may-16-2025.md]]
- **The cross-border tooling problem (Val's own theme):** an engineer crossing the Canada→US border **cannot carry** a dev station/tools/laptop/wires (he'd be a tourist, not allowed to work). So JVL must **ship a broad kit by truck** to Philippe in advance (dev station left there permanently, connectors, spare boards, maybe a soldering iron/monitor/power supply) — allow ~**8 working days / 2 weeks** end-to-end (send 2 days, transit 3 days, test 3 days). If **Yavorski** (or Alekseev) is to go to Georgia, plan the ticket/hotel and arrive a day or two before Incom is ready. [[raw/commission_flex-pay-may-16-2025.md]]
- **Who does what at the Georgia office:** Philippe and **Elizabeth** (described as a competent engineer) can set up the hardware/software environment; but the card-dispensing FlexPay work was built by **Sasha and Dima** (backend), not the Evgenys — so remote access is needed so Sasha/Dima can log in and fix card issues; Yavorski's FlexPort involvement is machine-port/link only. Val wants tests scheduled **early morning** to avoid weekend/night work. [[raw/commission_flex-pay-may-16-2025.md]]
- **Feature-granularity aside (repeat of Val's Omni doctrine):** the same call restated "don't describe all features up front — a feature should be a small demonstrable deliverable that moves the product one step," and told Irina to write tests in small achievable fragments. (Durable version on [[wiki/topics/omni-core-advancement-and-game-templating.md]].) [[raw/commission_flex-pay-may-16-2025.md]]

## May 20 2025 — industrial-design review: Linux tower, drop the FlexPort swipe

A [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] ↔ [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] industrial-design call ([[wiki/meetings/2025-05-20-flex-tower-industrial-design.md]]; [[wiki/people/igor-starovoytov.md|Igor Starovoytov]] does the CAD, referenced third-person; "Noah" greeting at the open is unclear). The §3 continuation of the May-1/2/6/15 tower thread. [[raw/industrial_design_update-may-20-2025.md]]

- **FlexPort dashboard bracket — "intended design," not a "conversion."** The first-revision bracket (three borders + scanner, plastic housing with backlight) is acceptable only as a **temporary** solution to reach the market for beta tests; as a permanent solution it "looks temporary." Val wants a **solid, integrated, forward-looking (slightly aggressive) protruding design** — a single **intended specific-purpose** part, not an assembly of separate pieces (the way the **FlexTower** already reads as intended). He preferred the third/bottom design variant as most harmonized with the FlexTower and wants the same language carried onto the next-cabinet panel. [[raw/industrial_design_update-may-20-2025.md]]
- **Drop the credit-card swipe from the FlexPort dashboard.** The key resolution: the FlexPort dashboard needs **only the optical scanner + monitor/touchscreen + FlexPort behind** — **no card swipe**, because the credit-card swipe **moves to the FlexTower**. The scanner is needed only in the **electronic-wallet** mode (not for TITO), but it's cheap and easy to implement, so keep it **permanent** under a small canopy. (Partial-insert card readers don't read the full track; full-insert readers are gas-station-size and too big — reinforcing the move to the tower.) [[raw/industrial_design_update-may-20-2025.md]]
- **Linux platform for the tower (~90%).** Val leans strongly to putting the **industrial board Genadi found** (a Linux platform) in the tower — "**bullseye**," marginally bigger but designed well; "don't fight for millimeters… make your life comfortable — we don't need the thinnest/smallest/lightest; we need it to switch on, work, and never break." Open item: how to drive the LCD **monitor + touchscreen** under Linux (touchscreen isn't USB; the monitor interface is **RGB**, needing a controller/adapter or a different LCD type) — Val to work it out with [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Frolov]]. Port plan: USB for scanner, serial for dispenser, serial for the swipe reader. The **POS** will likely also be Linux. [[raw/industrial_design_update-may-20-2025.md]]
- **Priority order (Val):** Tower (nearly done) → POS → amplifier → **TITO server** → the Flex-S machines (unified design) → the dashboard plastics. Sasha helped with the tower plastics this week; Genadi expects a near-orderable variant next week (awaiting samples). Design guidance: simplest forms, fast, top-cover + bottom-cover assembly, dimensions unimportant. [[raw/industrial_design_update-may-20-2025.md]]
- **FR7 board supply:** [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] launched two samples of the powerful **FR7** boards; the supplier (transcribed "до танка" — likely the **Batang/Datang** board vendor, flagged) will start the **first production batch mid-June** and needs JVL's desired quantity by **early June** (an allocation of ~10 was floated). [[raw/industrial_design_update-may-20-2025.md]]

## May 20 2025 — JCM bill-recycler evaluation for the payout-device line

Val evaluated **[[wiki/organizations/jcm.md|JCM]]** bill recyclers with JCM's [[wiki/people/cliff.md|Cliff]] ([[wiki/meetings/2025-05-20-jcm-bill-recycler-eval.md]]) as a cash-in/cash-out device candidate — the follow-through on the May-12 note that JVL's payout capability needs a **bill-to-bill recycler** (the "Cash Code"/JCM device). The end-of-life **iPro-RC** is out; the friction-feed **UBA Pro-RC** (70–80 notes, doesn't denominate out) and the twin-drum **UBA Pro RT** (Mylar-tape, 30 notes/drum, guaranteed dispense) are the live options, on the JCM ID-003/ID-008 protocols; Val flagged the ~250,000-cycle MTBF as low and asked JCM to verify. Full device catalog on [[wiki/organizations/jcm.md]]. [[raw/JCM_meeting_Cliff__bill_recycler-may-20-2025.md]]

## May 21 2025 — POS/Tower tech-stack selection deferred (embedded STM vs industrial-Linux)

A Russian architecture call ([[wiki/meetings/2025-05-21-tech-stack-flex-pos-tower-tito.md]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]) explicitly convened "to discuss the tech stack for use in POS and FlexTower." Two paths: (a) the **embedded (STM32) "sandwich"** — the embedded CPU + monitor + touchscreen bundle already built for FlexPort; (b) the **industrial PC board Genadi found, written for Linux**. The §3 continuation of the [[wiki/meetings/2025-05-20-flex-tower-industrial-design.md|May-20 industrial-design call]] (where Val leaned ~90% Linux for the *tower*); here he weighs the stack for **both POS and Tower** and ends more ambivalent. **No decision was locked.** [[raw/selection_tech_stack_hardware-flex-pos_flex-tower_flex-tito-may-21-2025.md]]

- **Val's stated lean (early): industrial-Linux.** "**99% я уверен**" the industrial board is more flexible, more convenient and easier to support. His main argument is the **software stack**: the company is moving GUIs to a **browser-based framework**, and he wants all company interfaces on browser solutions so **any** JVL programmer can work on them — versus embedded/STM, which needs scarce **low-level C programmers** and slow development. Both Evgenys already write for Linux and could be harnessed onto the work, shipping the products faster. [[raw/selection_tech_stack_hardware-flex-pos_flex-tower_flex-tito-may-21-2025.md]]
- **The cost penalty.** The industrial solution is "**в три раза дороже**" (~3× the price): the platform is **~$100 + storage ≈ $130**, versus **~$30** for STM. It also needs a way to **drive the cheap RGB touchscreen monitor** (no built-in interface — needs an LCD/LED controller/adapter, ~$10–20; the screen itself would cost a bit more than the STM variant). Net device difference put at roughly **$100–200 each**. For the **Tower** (dominated by the ~**$300 card dispenser**), STM ≈ **$400** vs industrial ≈ **$550**; the delta is more visible on the cheap **POS** than on the Tower. Genadi to price monitor/touchscreen adapter options overnight with the Chinese suppliers. [[raw/selection_tech_stack_hardware-flex-pos_flex-tower_flex-tito-may-21-2025.md]]
- **Embedded's merits (pull the other way).** Once an STM design is debugged it "**practically never breaks**," draws **less power**, runs cooler, and is a true embedded solution; smaller boards are easier to produce and ship. Downside: debugging is slow/tedious, and on STM32 you can't easily add a **second screen** (Evgeny said no) — the industrial board could drive two screens for the POS. Val wants a **NailBed** (bed-of-nails test rig) for fast embedded-board testing either way. [[raw/selection_tech_stack_hardware-flex-pos_flex-tower_flex-tito-may-21-2025.md]]
- **TITO server is Linux regardless.** The simple devices (Tower, POS) are the choice in play; the **TITO server** "надо писать уже под Linux" (must be written for Linux) — the open worry is whether a Linux-based stack and an embedded stack can communicate, judged fine since they already talk over the **WSS protocol** on current projects. [[raw/selection_tech_stack_hardware-flex-pos_flex-tower_flex-tito-may-21-2025.md]]
- **Volume/tooling pressure.** If the devices take, JVL must produce a **large quantity Jan–June 2026** using full tooling (**stamp + mold injection**) — "time is of the essence." Val is compressing decisions because "I'm going to be out of commission for a couple of days." [[raw/selection_tech_stack_hardware-flex-pos_flex-tower_flex-tito-may-21-2025.md]]
- **The (non-)decision.** By the end Val flips — "**я как-то склоняюсь в Embedded сторону**" (I'm somehow leaning toward the embedded side) — but declines to rule: "let's not decide now… this is a Monday/Tuesday decision, I'd at least gather the numbers." He'll **tentatively meet Monday ~10am**, and see Pavel the next day to prepare. So the embedded-vs-industrial-Linux stack for POS/Tower is **still open** as of May 21 (mild continuation/tension with the May-20 ~90%-Linux tower lean — recorded as ongoing deliberation, not a contradiction). [[raw/selection_tech_stack_hardware-flex-pos_flex-tower_flex-tito-may-21-2025.md]]

## May 26 2025 — tech-stack resolved: a cheap ARM-Linux board (the "third path")

The [[wiki/meetings/2025-05-26-hardware-selection-arm-flex-tower-pos.md|May-26 hardware-selection call]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]]) **closed the embedded-STM-vs-industrial-Linux dilemma** the May-20/21 calls left open — a weekend investigation found a way to get Linux flexibility at nearly the STM price. The decision-grade content is on [[wiki/decisions/2025-05-26-arm-linux-platform-flex-devices.md]]. [[raw/hardware_selection_flex-tower_flex-pos-may-26-2025.md]]

- **The board:** an **ARM-based, fully embedded Linux board — a Rockchip RK-series SoC** (the "линейка RK … 30, 31, 28, 33, 28, 35"; a quad-core "68-й" ≈ RK3568 cited), ~**10× more powerful than STM**, with **2 GB RAM + 16 GB storage onboard**, watchdog, battery, two full RS-232 serials, two I2C (dual-screen), several USB, Ethernet, 12 V, GPIO + spare UARTs. **~$60 ±$1** — only "чуть-чуть дороже" than the ~$30 STM [[raw/hardware_selection_flex-tower_flex-pos-may-26-2025.md]], versus the ~$130 (≈3×) industrial-Linux board from May-21 [[raw/selection_tech_stack_hardware-flex-pos_flex-tower_flex-tito-may-21-2025.md]]. Off-the-shelf; samples immediately. [[raw/hardware_selection_flex-tower_flex-pos-may-26-2025.md]]
- **Why it wins:** it runs **Linux (Ubuntu) or Android**, programmable in **C/Java/JavaScript** so **any JVL programmer** (not just scarce low-level C coders) can work on it — the exact flexibility Val wanted on May-21, now without the 3× cost penalty. One board serves **Flex Tower, Flex POS and the standalone server** (server logs on a swappable external SD card). [[raw/hardware_selection_flex-tower_flex-pos-may-26-2025.md]]
- **The enabling work:** the cheap touchscreens are **LVDS**, so JVL builds a simple FlexPort-style **adapter board** and [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]] writes the **LVDS video driver** (~2 days; a controller would otherwise add ~$20) — net device penalty vs STM only ~$20. **Firm sub-ruling: no extra I/O boards** — sensors go straight to GPIO/UART. [[raw/hardware_selection_flex-tower_flex-pos-may-26-2025.md]]
- **Ruled, with a validation contingency:** chosen direction pending Alekseev confirming the LVDS interface and sample validation; Val wanted to close it at a 7 am meeting the next day. (This resolves the May-21 mild tension — the earlier deferral flipped toward *embedded*; the ARM board reconciles both sides, being an embedded board that runs Linux.) [[raw/hardware_selection_flex-tower_flex-pos-may-26-2025.md]]

## May 27 2025 — full-SAS ruling and the e-Wallet-vs-TITO split

The May-27 dev review ([[wiki/meetings/2025-05-27-flexpay-flexport-dev-review.md]]) produced two strategic rulings now on [[wiki/decisions/2025-05-27-release-ewallet-mvp-first.md]]:

- **Two architectures are formally separated and mutually exclusive.** The **e-Wallet** architecture (FlexPort + FlexKiosk, no SMIB) is **development-complete and going to beta first**; the **TITO** architecture (FlexPort + SMIB + future **FlexTower / FlexPOS / FlexTitoServer**) is developed in parallel but still "very raw." A location runs one or the other — "две архитектуры в одной существовать не могут." [[raw/dev_review_flex-pay_flex-port-may-27-2025.md]]
- **Implement the FULL SAS protocol, no truncation.** Val corrected a prior "artificial scope gap": JVL had assumed it could ship a *truncated* SAS, but every major Class III manufacturer now on the Georgia COAM market — **Light & Wonder, IGT, Bally, Novomatic, Konami** — ships **full SAS including AFT** (Advanced Funds Transfer) and does not truncate. So FlexPort's architecture/hardware must assume full SAS (an "AFT display" is, to a non-engineer, much like FlexPort); only the MVP commands are *implemented* now. [[raw/dev_review_flex-pay_flex-port-may-27-2025.md]] [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] dissented (no player-tracking/switchboard vendor ever needs full SAS; AFT only matters if JVL wants card-based money-in/out) — Val's ruling stands over the objection. [[raw/dev_review_flex-pay_flex-port-may-27-2025.md]]
- **Why e-Wallet first even though Val expects it to fail in the market.** The e-wallet architecture "скорее всего, на рынке не проживется," but the **financial-transaction cycle** built for it (money through the Incom Bank and the JVL Bank) is reusable for TITO, so running a short e-wallet feature through the full money cycle now de-risks the later TITO in-house test — against a **November 2025** hard deadline. See the decision page for the MVP scope (JCM UBA only, version freeze, twice-weekly cadence, Georgia hardware list). [[raw/release_e-wallet_flex-pay_flex-port-may-27-2025.md]]

## May 29 2025 — e-Wallet/TITO implementation action-list review

A point-by-point action-list review ([[wiki/meetings/2025-05-29-ewallet-tito-flexpay-implementation.md]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] chairing, with [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Chudinov]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]], [[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Yavorski]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], and "Irina Shishko"), two days after the [[wiki/decisions/2025-05-27-release-ewallet-mvp-first.md|e-wallet-MVP-first ruling]]. The two-architecture split was restated: **E-Wallet vs TITO; the SMIB board belongs to the TITO architecture.** (§12: this source is a byte-identical download pair — the "(1)" copy was skipped.) [[raw/e-wallet_tito_flex-pay_flex-port_implementation_update-may-29-2025.md]]

- **Remote-update packet size: 64KB → 8KB.** On the E-Wallet architecture the software-update transfer (FlexPay→FlexPort over WSS) needs 64KB, at which the receive buffer "hangs very stably"; [[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Yavorski]] found **8KB transfers work without issue**, so [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Chudinov]] will change the packet size to 8KB (one 8KB fragment now; ~8000 fragments will make the full image, plus a frame/assembly + start-update mechanism). Logging is a non-issue on TITO (only ~60 bytes). Yavorski to add the protocol command Sunday so Minsk can test Monday. [[raw/e-wallet_tito_flex-pay_flex-port_implementation_update-may-29-2025.md]]
- **SMIB boards: 10 delivered (May 26); porting to a cheaper processor.** [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]] received **10 SMIB boards**; his weekend job is bringing them up and **porting the SMIB firmware from the expensive 176-pin "mock-up" processor to a cheaper fewer-pin permanent processor** — a firmware-project change (pin/peripheral config), the hardware/layout already done. USB firmware-update, SAS-command packet prep (≤60 byte for WSS), and the LED-status doc (on GitHub, 21st) are done; **MEI bill-acceptor compatibility testing moved to backlog.** [[raw/e-wallet_tito_flex-pay_flex-port_implementation_update-may-29-2025.md]]
- **Yavorski money-in/out corrections:** use the **minor-units** field (not the amount field) to issue credits; return the **real device number from the SAS protocol** (not a stub); implement real command logging (Alekseev supplies the internal command queue). Money-in/out was tested and an update sent to Minsk ~May 20. [[raw/e-wallet_tito_flex-pay_flex-port_implementation_update-may-29-2025.md]]
- **Partial redemption done.** Chudinov's partial-redemption implementation is complete and in beta testing, including the **API for [[wiki/people/ihar-hrybko.md|Ihar Hrybko]]** to build the FlexPay partial-redemption UI. [[raw/e-wallet_tito_flex-pay_flex-port_implementation_update-may-29-2025.md]]
- **GitHub repos:** SMIB and FlexPort repos are done (firmware incrementing; Alekseev commits artifacts); the **FlexPay Bitbucket→GitHub migration** is now targeted **~June 7** (was mid-June). Documentation (including SAS-protocol PDFs) is to live **in the repos** in Markdown; Val offered his 140-page-PDF→Markdown converter. **Full SAS** implementation is deferred to future versions ("could take a year — a five-year-plan in two days"), but the architecture must not preclude it. Irina raised correct firmware-version naming, **test-vs-production folders** on GitHub, and using a sub-version number instead of a build number. [[raw/e-wallet_tito_flex-pay_flex-port_implementation_update-may-29-2025.md]]
- **Future hardware deferred:** [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] is waiting on Alekseev to free up to discuss the next hardware platform (**FlexTower and POS** / massive-FlexPort updates) — pushed ~2 weeks, not touched now. Next meeting Monday June 2, 7am. [[raw/e-wallet_tito_flex-pay_flex-port_implementation_update-may-29-2025.md]]

## May 30 2025 — the commissioning/rollout plan and "Temporary Wallet is not the MVP"

On the [[wiki/meetings/2025-05-30-qa-flexport-flexpay-plan.md|May-30 QA/rollout meeting]] Val laid out the concrete go-to-market path for the e-Wallet architecture and restated where it sits in the product strategy (extends the [[wiki/decisions/2025-05-27-release-ewallet-mvp-first.md|e-Wallet-MVP-first]] and [[wiki/meetings/2025-05-16-flexpay-commissioning-plan.md|May-16 commissioning]] plans). [[raw/qa_flex-port_flex-pay_plan-may-30-2025.md]]

- **Completed vs pending (Temporary Wallet architecture).** Done: money transaction to Temporary Wallet on **FlexPort** and **FlexPay** (both directions), money acceptance, Temporary Wallet issuance + calculations + validations, **Incom card issuance**, USB firmware update. Pending: firmware update via **remote device / download**, **WSS-protocol logging** FlexPort↔FlexPay, all **TITO-architecture** functionality, and bill validators **beyond JCM UBA**. [[raw/qa_flex-port_flex-pay_plan-may-30-2025.md]]
- **~30-day commissioning path.** (1) debug-version cards at Felipe's (cards already arrived); (2) **Incom creates a fictitious location** inside Felipe's Georgia office (an exception — usually not done in-office) with a real location number, on Incom's **production** environment; (3) run the full **positive money cycle** — insert cash → Temporary Wallet → play across machines → validate full/partial at FlexPay → money to Incom → card → **JVL Bank** commission → Incom invoices JVL. Testing **Temporary-Wallet-only first, then TITO** (a second test at JVL's own warehouse "location," possibly a third), then live. Purpose is proving the **money-transfer cycle with Incom**, not device verification. [[raw/qa_flex-port_flex-pay_plan-may-30-2025.md]]
- **Scale + next devices.** Grow to **~10 locations by November**; then **Sept–Nov** begin testing the next hardware — **FlexPOS** (a mini point-of-sale computer, like a cash register), the **Flex Self-Dispensing Tower** (scans a Tito ticket between machines and dispenses a card), and the **Tito Server** (standalone box running a local server that validates ticket transactions) — each first at JVL's warehouse facility. [[raw/qa_flex-port_flex-pay_plan-may-30-2025.md]]
- **Executive caveat — Temporary Wallet is not the market MVP.** Val: the Temporary Wallet architecture "далеко не пойдёт" — he's using it as a **vehicle to prove the Incom↔JVL bank money cycle**, not to roll out; and he isn't sure the **kiosk** is the product that will go to market either. This aligns with the May-27 "e-Wallet first even though it likely fails in-market" ruling. Val put **Pavel** in charge of verifying the full cycle **remotely** (won't delegate), and would send "one of the Evgenys" to Georgia only if an on-site engineer is needed; **Juan** receives the Incom reports by email (mailboxes made by Andrei). See [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]]. [[raw/qa_flex-port_flex-pay_plan-may-30-2025.md]]
- **Full FlexPort with TITO / two client device types.** A closing exchange restated that JVL supports **two client device types — SMIB and FlexPort**; running FlexPort at full capability (with screen) in a TITO config on a machine with two full SAS ports would need the Evgenys to build a **third firmware variant**. [[raw/qa_flex-port_flex-pay_plan-may-30-2025.md]]

## June 2 2025 — e-Wallet live-test planning (the "rudderless" test)

The ~10-minute segment after the progress review ([[wiki/meetings/2025-06-02-incomm-ewallet-live-test-planning.md]]) focused on getting the first live e-Wallet beta test right at the **beta location = JVL Georgia warehouse** (connected to Incom's production server, real money). [[raw/incomm_testing_progress_report-june-2-2025.md]]

- **"Rudderless."** Val judged the prior week's attempt rudderless — [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]] loaded Pavel's file, "it hung and nothing worked," with no time/version/detail. Val called [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] (more descriptive than Felipe) who confirmed only that the test failed. [[raw/incomm_testing_progress_report-june-2-2025.md]]
- **Val's process coaching (to Pavel, durable working rule).** Before handing anyone a build, give it a **reference number**, state the **results you expect**, describe the **steps**, and ask for a report **in a defined style** — verbal-only instructions leave a ~50/50 chance of being understood; an accompanying written description raises it to ~75%. Val told Pavel to change how he works with Felipe (or anyone). A ChatGPT tip: copy the useful/useless chat into a document, then ask the AI to write the expectations for one person — "works like a charm." [[raw/incomm_testing_progress_report-june-2-2025.md]]
- **The primary objective = a clean, uninterrupted Incom connection.** Val: negative/positive test detail matters less than proving a **positive, uninterrupted connection** to the Incom server with steady communication. This needs a **visible connection-status indicator** (a green/red blinking icon, showing connected *and* disconnected, plus packet counts) — **accessible to the location owner**, not only a technician, and understandable by a non-engineer; in player mode a "connection lost, call the administrator" message. Prioritise this in development. [[raw/incomm_testing_progress_report-june-2-2025.md]]
- **Incom-outage policy.** Block the money-out path if Incom is unreachable ("no Incom, no money" — you can't take a player's money and fail to return it); keep retrying and auto-recover when the link returns; treat the Incom link as a "greenhouse"/hothouse resource kept always healthy. Card issuance requires **three requests to Incom**: register via **OAuth**, provide authentication credentials, then request card **activation** (with the required data set). [[raw/incomm_testing_progress_report-june-2-2025.md]]
- **Val to sit through it.** He wants to be present from **start to finish** of the first beta test (even 5 hours). Pavel will first talk to Felipe to understand what went wrong; the first beta test is left "hanging in the air" pending that clarification. [[raw/incomm_testing_progress_report-june-2-2025.md]]

## June 11 2025 — the manual "POS 2" model for Mother-Goose operators

A [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] ↔ [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]] session (with the new engineer [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan]] onboarding; [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]] referenced) worked through a **new, deliberately minimal business model** Val had messaged the team about — a **fully manual point-of-sale** that lets the cheapest Georgia street operators comply without TITO. ([[wiki/meetings/2025-06-11-pos-mother-goose-manual-model.md]]) [[raw/POS_mutha_goose_architecture-june-11-2025.md]]

- **"Mother Goose"** is the Georgia **legacy pulse-based ticketing/redemption system** many street locations already run (per Felipe, **RKS** makes the original; two or three other systems now print similar tickets). Its dot-matrix ticket carries a **confidence number** (sample "5-665-255-941"), a serial ("GM-RU-06-13-599"), a voucher number, date/time and value. Mother Goose has **no validation** and JVL has **no access to its database**. [[raw/POS_mutha_goose_architecture-june-11-2025.md]]
- **The model (Val's operator persona).** Playing "Val the operator" with ~200–300 marginal, capital-intensive locations facing ~**$10,000/location** to convert to TITO, Val proposes a **standalone POS device placed next to the existing Mother Goose printer**: the clerk **manually keys in** the ticket's confidence/serial/voucher number **and amount**, the POS connects to **[[wiki/organizations/incom.md|Incom]]**, and it **pairs a prepaid MasterCard** with that number and win value (charging the customer a **$2 fee**). A **completely manual process** — no TITO, no SAS, no printers-in-machines, no server, no cabinet re-work. It adds **no security and no technical improvement** — it simply achieves **full legislative compliance for almost no money**. [[raw/POS_mutha_goose_architecture-june-11-2025.md]]
- **Fraud is out of scope here.** Whether the Mother Goose ticket is still open in its own system is "**between the operation and GLC**," not JVL's concern — a reversal of the [[#the-gamoa-pulse-security-objection-apr-11|earlier Incom "onus is on the signer" stance]] because this is a **street business** where operators know their players by first name; fraud isn't a practical problem ("it would never work in a casino environment… but street business is street business"). [[raw/POS_mutha_goose_architecture-june-11-2025.md]]
- **Two POS forms now.** JVL will build **"POS 2"** (this manual Mother-Goose model) **and** a **"POS TITO" / ticket-related POS** (connects to the TITO server + FlexPort). **Physically the same device**, but internally different — the TITO version needs a Tito server, FlexPort, printers; POS 2 needs none of that. The **architecture underneath POS 2 must still be expandable into TITO / ticket validation** later, but the **user interface stays dead simple**. [[raw/POS_mutha_goose_architecture-june-11-2025.md]]
- **Go-to-market thinking.** Val's read: it's the **least-expensive compliance solution** he's seen (at the shows every POS was a sub-component of a bigger kiosk ecosystem, never standalone), so if JVL is first with it before the **June-1-2026** deadline it could win "majority of the market." Idea floated: **give the POS away free in exchange for a 3–5-year exclusive agreement**, monetising through **recurring transaction processing** and any future upgrades. Operators can later add a Tito server + printers to graduate to TITO. [[raw/POS_mutha_goose_architecture-june-11-2025.md]]
- Juan understood the ask; deep-dive with Nathan (FlexPay/FlexPort background) pushed to the **Friday 6 a.m.** session. [[raw/POS_mutha_goose_architecture-june-11-2025.md]]

## June 13 2025 — FlexPay system architecture walkthrough; the offline "POS non-SAS"

An architecture review ([[wiki/meetings/2025-06-13-flex-tower-pos-architecture.md]]; [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]] chairing, [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]] presenting; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda Giorgi]], [[wiki/people/ihar-hrybko.md|Ihar Hrybko]]). [[raw/Flex-Tower _POS - Architecture.md]]

- **The Flex Tito server is the central brain.** Its purpose is cashless redemption of tickets across EGMs; it holds all the API logic and talks over **secure WebSockets** to every component — SMIB boards, Flex Tower, Flex POS, technician laptops (used for setup because the Tito server has no monitor/keyboard) — and to the cloud (config, firmware, reports, logs) and to the third-party card provider (**Incom**). Tower/POS/SMIB are deliberately kept **"dumb"** so several towers/POS can share one Tito server's identifications; Pavel's team chose central logic over each device calling Incom directly because it makes the architecture simpler. [[raw/Flex-Tower _POS - Architecture.md]]
- **Device catalog restated.** **Flex SMIB** (per-EGM board, RS-232/SAS to the machine, WSS to the Tito server, translates SAS↔cheater/Tito). **Flex Tower** (optional kiosk: card dispenser, QR scanner, 5–6" touch screen, magnetic swipe reader; auto-dispenses a ready-to-use gift card when a ticket is scanned; the side swipe reader is future expansion for next-year reloadable cards; needs a **low-card / level sensor** in the stacker and a **secure back-door** to refill cards). **Flex POS** (cashier desk: 10" touch, optional 2nd screen, QR scanner, swipe reader; service/setup mode via **PIN or card swipe — not a mechanical audit key**, which "always gets lost"). All components must be **swappable by non-technical location staff** with automatic passport re-registration on power-up (no manual re-pairing); Val stresses these are **operations-critical** — if they fail the whole location stops. [[raw/Flex-Tower _POS - Architecture.md]]
- **New idea: the offline "POS non-SAS" for Mother-Goose street operators.** Val's pitch (told at length for Felipe): ~35,000 machines across ~6,000–6,500 Georgia locations already work fine and installing TITO won't earn the operator more money — so offer a **self-contained POS** that needs **no connection to Mother Goose, no server, no printers-in-machines**. The cashier manually keys the Mother-Goose ticket value + a few fields, pairs a stacked prepaid card, done; **lease it ~$1/day over 3 years for an exclusive contract**; graduate to full TITO later without throwing the POS away. Juan named the two variants **"POS SAS" and "POS non-SAS."** Whatever logic runs on the Tito server can run on the same ARM-Linux POS hardware. [[raw/Flex-Tower _POS - Architecture.md]]
- **Pavel's fraud concern (Val "loved the argument").** With the offline POS a cashier could self-issue many cards with no cross-check to the Mother-Goose cash-out; since they'd transact through JVL's merchant account, JVL pays Incom immediately then has to investigate. Mitigations discussed: Incom **per-location throttling limits** (e.g. ~$5,000/day) and JVL-side limits on large/multiple activations; and **serial-sniffing the Mother-Goose printer** (wedge the POS between machine and printer via dual RS-232 to validate the entered amount against the print stream). Felipe's counter-risk: legacy counter boards print bad values ($39/$3999999/$1 tickets) and there's no dedicated Mother-Goose transaction history, so sniffing trusts 30-year-old serial data — which may itself justify converting to TITO. **RKS** (a Georgia husband-and-wife "dying company") makes the original Mother Goose; **Omega** makes the "fledgling." [[raw/Flex-Tower _POS - Architecture.md]]
- **Hardware line drawn.** FlexPort stays **STM** ("a done product"); the **POS** leans **ARM + Linux** on a compelling China board from a core supplier ("JVL is more of a Linux house than an STM embedded house"); **Tower** is an open choice Val declined to settle in this meeting. Undertaking: [[wiki/people/ihar-hrybko.md|Hrybko]] builds an **interactive Figma player-side Flex Tower prototype** to test whether the player UI is legible on a **5-inch** screen (the legal service-fee disclaimer must be readable — the US is litigious) for a combined **Friday 6 a.m.** sizing review; Juan/Felipe expedite persona use cases; Nathan bridges use-cases→capabilities→features into GitHub. [[raw/Flex-Tower _POS - Architecture.md]]

## June 16 2025 (date inferred) — dev standup: FlexPort idle-screen timeouts, statistics logging, the ARM dev-kit & a microcontroller-certification scare

A weekly developer standup ([[wiki/meetings/2025-06-16-flexpay-flexport-smib-progress.md]]; Russian; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Yavorski]], [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]], [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Chudinov]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Shishko]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]]). **Date inferred** (undated): postdates the June-13 architecture meeting (the Linux dev-kit is already ordered); placed mid-June, exact day unconfirmed. [[raw/Flex-Pay_Flex-Port_Flex-SMIB_progress_update.md]]

- **Kiosk/FlexPort idle-screen timeouts.** After scanning, screens hold ~1 minute; Chudinov's agreed design splits it — a **30-second confirmation screen**, then a **30-second countdown** ("you still here?") before returning to the idle screen — so a slow player isn't cut off but the terminal doesn't sit open. Val to have the requirement written up (it's a spec item, "not a bug"). [[raw/Flex-Pay_Flex-Port_Flex-SMIB_progress_update.md]]
- **FlexPort statistics logging.** Alekseev is adding **lifetime statistics** to FlexPort external memory (no filesystem, hand-written records, machine-readable only; ~2,400-record circular buffer of bills accepted/rejected/faults) plus a separate **on-request** statistics read for a FlexPort connected to a kiosk; he'll ship a **USB dump utility** and a decode document (Chudinov can write the interpreter). Distinct from FlexPay, which knows nothing about the money until a transfer starts. [[raw/Flex-Pay_Flex-Port_Flex-SMIB_progress_update.md]]
- **ARM-Linux dev-kit ordered + a certification scare.** Yavorski **ordered the ARM Linux dev-kit** for the Tower/POS; next step is simply to boot Linux and confirm it meets requirements. He also relayed a **certification warning**: a lab told him passing is "unrealistic" because of a near-ban / sanctions on **Chinese microcontrollers** (a sanctioned component must be swapped or the UL/GLI certificate is voided). Val is sceptical the lab even inspects the microcontroller ("ARM is a company; made-in-China doesn't matter"); **Genadi to ask the lab**. (Recorded as an open question on the meeting stub.) [[raw/Flex-Pay_Flex-Port_Flex-SMIB_progress_update.md]]

## June 18 2025 — GA-COAM ticket-redemption workflow; Tito vs Mother-Goose; ARM hardware

A ~2-hour workflow-design session ([[wiki/meetings/2025-06-18-gcoam-ticket-redemption-workflow.md]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]] — rendered both "Pablo" and "Pavel" — and [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]]) walking the ticket-redemption use case end-to-end on a **Confluence** page (use-case containers + workflow diagrams, replacing the earlier Miro). Engineers still track work in **Jira**, not GitHub; GitHub has empty **FlexTower** and **FlexPOS** repos. (§12: transcript internally duplicates — facts taken once.) [[raw/about_tickets_redemption-gcoam-june-18–2025.md]]

- **Two distinct business architectures.** **Tito business architecture** (barcode ticket validated against a Tito server) vs **"Move-the-Goose" / Mother-Goose (Mutagoose) business architecture** (legacy pulse ticket, no server, no validation). They share hardware but differ in validation logic. [[raw/about_tickets_redemption-gcoam-june-18–2025.md]]
- **Tito redemption flow (Flex Tower).** Idle screen when powered + connected to Incom and the internal Tito server; the player either **presents the ticket to the scanner or presses a start button** — both start the process, treated equally (Val's ruling: satisfy both preferences, "either present the ticket or press a button… either or starts an operation"), plus a **recurring light gesture** on the tray so the machine never looks dead. The tower reads the **barcode only** (the barcode carries **no value** — the server knows the value); one request/response to the Tito server returns valid/invalid + value. A ticket **cannot be partially redeemed** (SAS protocol); validity is set by the back-office system (15 days / 48–72h / up to ~30 days). This is the **first time a Tito ticket stays in the player's hands** (normally consumed inside a bill acceptor's stacking cassette) — so **clarity of player communication matters** to avoid costly on-site conflicts. Invalid/redeemed tickets must show **when + at which device** it was redeemed. [[raw/about_tickets_redemption-gcoam-june-18–2025.md]]
- **Fees / card limits.** A **$2 processing fee** is deducted ($100 win → **$98** on the card); amounts over a per-location card limit (e.g. max $1,000) **split into several cards**; card min/max is a property of the **Incom card set** (last batch had a **$20 minimum**), set per location on FlexPay. Redemption requires a **terms/agreement page** ($2 fee acknowledgement) only after the player chooses to redeem, then an optional receipt (SMS/email or a QR link to the server). [[raw/about_tickets_redemption-gcoam-june-18–2025.md]]
- **RIS = Remote Initial Setup.** Val re-branded the per-location setup file (location ID, limits, reporting, operating restrictions, min card value…) as **RIS** — a setup file pushed remotely, not tied to a specific server or "Tito Serga." [[raw/about_tickets_redemption-gcoam-june-18–2025.md]]
- **SMIB / FlexPort retrofit for the Tito path.** Newer machines with **two SAS ports** get a **JVL SMIB** (a ~$10 slot-machine-interface board on the second SAS port; Cat6 to the Tito server over **WSS protocol**). Older machines (**~30–50% of the market, ~10–17k of ~35,000**) have only **one SAS port dedicated to the Georgia Lottery Corporation** (the primary SAS port with the enable/disable function) — these get a **FlexPort** instead (an SMTP-based embedded "sandwich" board + 5-inch player-facing touchscreen driving validator + printer, connecting to the Tito server over WSS). [[raw/about_tickets_redemption-gcoam-june-18–2025.md]]
- **Mother-Goose (Mutagoose) path — the primitive POS.** The legacy system is machine-specific with **no server, no validation**; the dot-matrix ticket has **no barcode, just alphanumeric numbers**. JVL's minimal solution: a **POS connected only to Incom**, where the cashier manually keys the ticket value and pairs a MasterCard. An evolution: **wedge the POS in parallel with the printer** (one serial port capturing the print traffic) so the POS captures each win's value and the cashier selects the right record — "no Tito application" needed. [[raw/about_tickets_redemption-gcoam-june-18–2025.md]]
- **Hardware direction — SMTP → ARM.** JVL is "**more of a Linux house than an embedded house**"; Genadi found an **inexpensive ARM board** whose price is close enough to SMTP to justify moving to **ARM + Linux + a HAL abstraction** and standard libraries to drive monitors/printer. A **price-sensitive ~8-inch, ~3×4-ratio monitor** was chosen for the POS; samples shipping **Monday**. Industrial design ~2 weeks; a **dedicated plastics expert** lays out two shells; tooling ~**30 days** with no sampling. (Continues the [[#may-26-2025--tech-stack-resolved-a-cheap-arm-linux-board-the-third-path|May-26 ARM-Linux decision]].) [[raw/about_tickets_redemption-gcoam-june-18–2025.md]]
- **FlexPOS is now priority #1, FlexTower #2** — Val redirected Nathan from Tower to POS (rollout schedule on [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]]). A future **mobile app** to scan a barcode for validation info was floated. Nathan to build a JIRA **requirements ticket** for the "scan & validate" work with the rules documented in it; [[wiki/people/aaron.md|Aaron]] to help organize Jira (Nathan + Pavel to meet him).
