Communication to InComm is moving to a **cloud server** by January for security (InComm advised this). [[raw/CMS- Block Diagram_Arch.md]] [[raw/back-office_architecture-august-20-2025.md]]

## Aug 22 2025 — Flex-POS release requirements (written note) + reconciliation/fund-flow deep-dive + Georgia-show device signals

**The Flex-POS release requirements as a standalone note.** Val's written requirements doc [[raw/First course of business POS 082225.md]] ("First course of business: Release the Flex-POS + dispenser into the market") is the authoritative source for the four release capabilities the Aug-20 follow-up first sketched — here with more detail: [[raw/First course of business POS 082225.md]]
1. **Install + auto-configure out of the box** — define **network requirements**, the **minimum Internet requirement** (does InComm need a dedicated line? — resolved toward **cloud**), and InComm's **bare-minimum API over HTTPS**.
2. **Establish an ACH agreement with a location** — define the **ACH contract language** (carrying the client's ACH details) and decide **existing JVL bank account vs a new dedicated account**.
3. **Debit locations' accounts by ACH within 24 hours** — an automated process to **debit each client ACH account daily** (business days or every day) and an **automated ACH credit to InComm**.
4. **Present a reconcilable accounting detail** to locations and master operators — a **cloud-based client login** for reconciliation reporting, **JVL tools to track pending ACH transactions**, **location reports** (transaction detail + service-fee breakout), and **master reports** (scope still a "?"). [[raw/First course of business POS 082225.md]]

**The Aug-22 CMS block-diagram session ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-22-cms-block-diagram-reconciliation.md|arc session 2]])** worked these requirements plus the device fund-flows for Will's benefit. Device/engineering facts new or sharpened here (the CMS/cloud direction is on [[wiki/topics/persona-driven-design-and-orchestration-platform.md]]): [[raw/CMS- Block Diagram_Arch 082225.md]]
- **Reconciliation is three flows that must reconcile** (Will): EGM in/out, funds-in-transit, and the InComm side. Pavel simplified it — you **cannot insert money into a terminal** (cash goes into the machine; the kiosk only redeems), so there is no kiosk-money reconciliation; Flex-POS only knows a ticket arrived from the Mother Goose, so JVL reconciles that ticket value against what it pays InComm and debits the location. [[raw/CMS- Block Diagram_Arch 082225.md]]
- **The e-wallet must function fully locally** (a player moves between machines even with the Internet down; only card issuance needs InComm), so funds must persist durably on-site, not just in RAM. Will pushed for an **ATM-grade write-only durable storage device** so transactions survive a destroyed/stolen kiosk; Pavel's plan is to **share ticket/accounting data back to the JVL terminal-communication server after each operation** so a broken terminal can be replaced (recoverable if it didn't fail mid-transaction, not 100% loss-proof). Local storage on the **ARM board's EMMC + SD-card slot** was floated; Genadi Peari's spec didn't cover memory, so it must be confirmed with him. [[raw/CMS- Block Diagram_Arch 082225.md]]
- **The e-wallet ticket carries two counters** (playable-only + redeemable); **only winnings redeem to the MasterCard**; **partial transfers** are allowed. For older single-SAS machines (state system Intralot occupies SAS 1) JVL **mimics the bill-acceptor input pulses** and uses the **electromagnetic counter** for cash-out; the TITO solution uses **full secure-enhanced SAS**. The **Flex-POS alone (no kiosk)** is the Q4 target; the FlexPay kiosk exists only in advanced prototype for shows. The Georgia-show SAS pilot "worked flawlessly" (the only fault was Juan's own human error — inserting cash into the TITO kiosk instead of the machine, locking the printer). [[raw/CMS- Block Diagram_Arch 082225.md]]

**Georgia-show (GACOAM) device signals** from the [[wiki/meetings/2025-08-22-gacoam-trade-show-recap.md|Aug-22 7:09 PM recap]] (market/strategy detail on [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]]): [[raw/GACOAM_trade_show_recap.md]]
- **e-wallet was preferred over TITO** by operators once reminded a printer isn't needed ("e-wallet all day long"); at least 5–6 rival digital-wallet solutions were shown, Wind Systems' getting the most notice (no ticket/kiosk; claims it moves money to the player's Apple/Android wallet). [[raw/GACOAM_trade_show_recap.md]]
- **Printer retrofit is a market non-starter** — installing a printer on ~500 games across ~700 locations is work masters won't do (Val: refilling paper / breaking print heads is "a point of failure"), and converting the ~20–30% of single-SAS inventory to TITO is impractical. Val's strategic answer: the **Flex-POS wedges into the existing Mother Goose infrastructure** with **no out-of-pocket cost and no machine retrofit** — his "top secret" to unveil aggressively; the business unfolds starting **July 2026**. **Flexport will support legacy products** (e.g. IGT Prodigy) that get no other upgrade path. [[raw/GACOAM_trade_show_recap.md]]
- **Remote initial setup (RIS)** remains the acknowledged inconvenience; Val told Felipe not to expect a radical RIS improvement before **end of 2026** — full RIS benefit comes only with the **new framework** entering the market in 2026. A quick UI toggle for "printer present or not" was floated to engineers as a possible near-term fix. [[raw/GACOAM_trade_show_recap.md]]
- **Third product stage = redemption centers.** Val framed the product evolution as incomplete until it reaches an InComm-backed **Redemption Center** (cash-to-cash, non-TITO, fewer peripheral devices) — confidential, not for customer discussion. [[raw/GACOAM_trade_show_recap.md]]

## Sept 3 2025 — Flex Capabilities weekly: near-final POS industrial design + cashier administration

The [[wiki/meetings/2025-09-03-flex-capabilities-weekly-ach-dispute-pos-design.md|Sept-3 weekly]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]], 12:03 PM) advanced the physical POS design and cashier administration; the ACH-provider, once-daily batch-swipe, reconciliation and dispute-resolution substance is on [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]] (Sept-3 section). [[raw/Flex Capabilities Weekly Update-september-3-2025.md]]

- **POS = a double-screen device, near-final direction (Genadi).** Main **cashier screen 8 inch**; whole-device width **7.5 inch** (Juan sensitive to size); a smaller, less-tall **customer screen (~7 inch)** on the back; **scanner in the middle** so a ticket is scannable by cashier and customer both; **magnetic-stripe reader on top, sunk into the enclosure** with a side-to-side slide cover, available to both sides. A further round of modifications was promised for the following Monday. [[raw/Flex Capabilities Weekly Update-september-3-2025.md]]
- **Dispenser stays a separate "dummy" product.** The enclosure carries interfaces for all external connections incl. a dedicated dispenser connector, so the dispenser is a **standalone, freestanding device** (one wire, no screen/features of its own — the POS screens cover it) mounted on the counter or a pedestal; also interfaces for the printer and Mother Goose, plus an optional extra-screen interface. [[raw/Flex Capabilities Weekly Update-september-3-2025.md]]
- **Optional floating third screen.** The POS can drive a **third autonomous screen** extended on a wire and laid flat near the player (limited graphical performance, "mostly for static data" / accepting terms — "like Flex-Port"); it must still be touch. In that mode the built-in second screen is removed and the POS becomes single-screen. Val: it is for the **player**, positioned by the location where players can see it. [[raw/Flex Capabilities Weekly Update-september-3-2025.md]]
- **Cashier login / PIN administration (Pavel), staged.** JVL doesn't want to be in the password-administration business (cashiers turn over ~monthly). Stage 1 (**by January 1**): create PIN codes **centrally on the server side** and assign a PIN to a POS device; a location must first be **created on the server side by JVL**, then a location admin PIN lets a third party log in, enter InComm credentials + terminal parameters, and create cashier PINs. Later stages: a location admin **magnetic card**, or an **admin QR/barcode generated from the operator's mobile** and presented to the POS scanner to enter a special mode (add employee, set PIN, **hot-swap a device** — settings retrieved from the server since credentials attach to the **location, not the device**). Device swap can't be fully automatic (needs a confirmation step). [[raw/Flex Capabilities Weekly Update-september-3-2025.md]]

## Sept 9 2025 — POS spatial/hardware planning: one-board vs interface-board (unresolved), HDMI-connector swap

The [[wiki/meetings/2025-09-09-pos-spatial-hardware-board-planning.md|Sept-9 3:18 PM session]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]]; Russian) took the Aug-19 single-board direction into the actual PCB partitioning and lost the argument for full consolidation — for now. [[raw/POS_spatial_planning-september-9-2025.md]]

- **Block scheme (from Genadi + Alekseev).** Motherboard mounted **vertically in the top section**; a **main interface board** below multiplexes all external connections (Mother Goose, printer/Motagus, dispenser, power/Ethernet, optional extra monitor) into **one connector/harness** so the unit disassembles fast; power step-down (~5 V) and minimal logic on the interface board. Cashier screen **~800×608**, customer screen smaller/lower-res, centred dual-side scanner, top magnetic-stripe reader. [[raw/POS_spatial_planning-september-9-2025.md]]
- **The debate: one board vs main-board + interface-board.** Val pushed to consolidate onto **one board** (as done on Flex-Port) — fast assembly, "sandwich" mount behind the screen, connectors up off the tabletop (spills/water/vibration are the real failure and cost drivers: "our money is in assembly and development, not in buying the board"). Genadi + Pavel defended the **two-board split** — the ready-made cheap Chinese **Rockchip** main board (multi-layer, expensive to custom-make) + a cheap **2-layer interface board** that's trivial to change later; a custom processor board wouldn't be cheaper, would risk the **January** target, and likely wouldn't work first try. **Outcome: keep the existing scheme now; consider a custom board as a future exercise.** [[raw/POS_spatial_planning-september-9-2025.md]]
- **Connectors / details.** Only real weak point is the **second monitor's HDMI-micro connector** (not shielded, not latching); plan is to ask the China supplier to **swap it for a more industrial connector** (or offer a different board). Touchscreens connect via **USB** (standard, software-transparent). Card-reader **status LED** driven from the interface board, lit only when awaiting a card; built-in beeper kept; monochrome embossed **JVL** on the reader slot rather than a separate lit part. Pavel floated **LVDS daisy-chain / dual-mode** to drive both monitors off one connector (depends on Rockchip dual-mode support) — an Alekseev question; a meeting with Alekseev booked next day. [[raw/POS_spatial_planning-september-9-2025.md]]

A Val↔[[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] one-on-one ([[wiki/meetings/2025-09-22-electro-mechanical-alignment-genadi.md]], 4:28 PM, Russian) on the embedded-hardware side of the January POS. [[raw/alignment_elctro-mechanical-september-22-2025.md]]

- **POS RockChip board can't be USB-flashed.** The cheap Chinese "RockChip" (rock-chip) processor board needs the vendor's proprietary PC-side softload procedure, not JVL's simple plug-a-USB-stick image; JVL can't get the source files. Genadi's three parallel paths: extract the vendor source files, find a better-supported Chinese vendor on the same **ARM industrial platform**, or have **[[wiki/people/dima-pikulev.md|Dima Pikulev]]** rebuild the Linux so a USB image works. Genadi holds that an ARM industrial platform is the right embedded direction; the RockChip is cheap but poorly supported. [[raw/alignment_elctro-mechanical-september-22-2025.md]]

A **bigger flash chip** was added because the firmware didn't fit, so [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] must re-solder one; files to Pavel/Minsk by end of week. [[raw/alignment_elctro-mechanical-september-22-2025.md]]
- **Alekseev's universal interface board.** [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]] (opening 33 Jira tasks) builds the **monitor interface board** first, then a single **main interface board** universal to any ARM platform later dropped in — carrying a **USB hub/multiplier**, "no processor, no firmware, purely electronic," one wire to avoid confusion. Val queried USB reliability; Genadi: it adds no new bus lines, just multiplies the same USB (a converter). [[raw/alignment_elctro-mechanical-september-22-2025.md]]
- **FlexPort board waits on the mechanical form.** Genadi wants to lock the FlexPort plastic/mechanical form first because **"Evgeny" will have to adjust the board file** (mount, size, perimeter lighting, scanner reach) — "we must be first, he comes after us." Per the updated §4 registry ruling, hardware-adjacent board work on Genadi's projects resolves to [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Evgeniy Alekseev]] (no fourth "Zhenya") — the board-file side of his FlexPort/interface-board work above. Sequencing: clear the Canadian warehouse + clean Georgia first, reuse the freed shelving + lift in Georgia (buy a small local forklift), then set up overflow. Current ~$7.40/sq-ft vs a taller ~$13/sq-ft option — undecided. Genadi's rule: untouched 2 years → discard. [[raw/alignment_elctro-mechanical-september-22-2025.md]]

[[raw/POS_and_PORT-schedule_review.md]]

## Nov 28 2025 — FlexPort/SMIB sync: cashout bill-return resolved, validator diagnostics, Jira-as-truth discipline

The [[wiki/meetings/2025-11-28-flexport-smib-sync-jira-review.md|Nov-28 FlexPort/FlexSMIB sync]] (Val, [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Eugine Yavorski]], [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Evgeniy Alekseev]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], 1h7m) is the weekly Jira task review continuing the Nov-11/12/20 arc. The Rockchip Linux-image/board-source substance is on the [[wiki/decisions/2025-05-26-arm-linux-platform-flex-devices.md|ARM/Rockchip decision]] (Nov-28); the FlexPay/transactional points here: [[raw/Flex-Port_Smib_alignment-November-28-2025.md]]

- **Cashout "bill-return" resolved into a two-phase, uncompleted command.** On a low-credit-cap machine (won't hold more than ~$200), an over-limit load triggers a bill-acceptor **bill return**; returning that as cash mis-converted when player/redeemable credit and cash were mixed. Rather than add a new FlexPay command or an extra cashout field (Yavorski's first instinct, to keep backward compatibility), Pavel and Yavorski settled on running cashout as a **two-phase command that simply does not complete phase 2** when the machine rejects the bill — simpler and more robust against a mid-transaction hang. Yavorski to rework and test (HFM-255). [[raw/Flex-Port_Smib_alignment-November-28-2025.md]]
- **New validator/cash-box diagnostics (CPI-477, on [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dima Chudinov]]).** There was no diagnostic for the bill-validator/cash-box state — a removed cash box or disconnected validator was silently ignored while the system kept running (~2–3 weeks lost to it). Pavel opened CPI-477 to analyse and report the bill-acceptor state. The FlexPay-side negative-scenario suite (TF-256, also Chudinov) is ~1.5 weeks out. [[raw/Flex-Port_Smib_alignment-November-28-2025.md]]
- **Anti-tamper design vs the "eco" test machines.** Alekseev's two small home "eco" machines (used for lack of space) — one won't run (needs a progressive) despite being identical to the working one. Val's durable point: JVL machines carry deliberate **hardware/BIOS anti-tamper traps** (one-screen software can't drive two screens; can't assemble from market parts), so cobbling eco machines into a test rig fights JVL's own design — "the wrong approach" (ICF-258 on hold). [[raw/Flex-Port_Smib_alignment-November-28-2025.md]]
- **Jira-as-truth cadence.** Val enforced "**if it isn't in Jira, it doesn't exist**," a weekly Monday Jira-status pull he sends each engineer to confirm, weekly sketch/scrum planning, and (bluntly, to Yavorski) breaking big tasks into properly-statused subtasks. Board tasks 274/275 due this weekend (274 critical — "Minsk can't work" without it). [[raw/Flex-Port_Smib_alignment-November-28-2025.md]]

## Dec 1 2025 — the cashout bill-return worked into a multi-denomination emulation (weekly)

The [[wiki/meetings/2025-12-01-flexport-smib-weekly-multidenomination-cashout.md|Dec-1 weekly]] (Val, [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Eugine Yavorski]], [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Evgeniy Alekseev]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], 59m) **advanced** (did not supersede) the Nov-28 bill-return resolution: what Nov-28 framed as a simple "two-phase command that doesn't complete phase 2" is really a **per-bill, multi-denomination emulation**. [[raw/Flex-Port_Flex-Smib_Weekly-December-1-2025.md]]

- **How FlexPort loads a ticket into a machine.** FlexPort **emulates a bill acceptor**, splitting the ticket value into individual bills (e.g. $95 → $50 + $20 + $20 + $5) and sending them one at a time over **protocol 1003** (the DCC3 bill-acceptor protocol; FlexPort talks to the machine directly — there is no machine-level "maximum I'll accept" command). When the machine's credit cap is reached it **returns** the next bill. Resolution: behave like a real acceptor — **send bill-by-bill, confirm each via the 1003 status, deduct it on FlexPay only on confirmation**; whatever is rejected simply **stays on the ticket**; show a neutral "**$70 of $90 credited**" message. One cashout becomes 5–6 transactions and FlexPay reporting shows a row per bill (accepted as improved transparency). Bills split into the **highest denominations** the acceptor's note-table allows. This is **HFM-339** (protocol modification). [[raw/Flex-Port_Flex-Smib_Weekly-December-1-2025.md]]
- **Process ruling.** Rather than argue estimates, JVL will **feed the recording to AI to decompose HFM-339 into subtasks**, then **Pavel meets Yavorski one-on-one** to agree scope/timing. More bugs captured: a **double-click double-withdrawal** (fix with a button lock/semaphore); the "**cash out $0**" spurious screen (HFM-344); a validator-restart-after-machine re-attach issue (HFM-345); and **long-run stability testing** (HFM-346, kept under FM-191). [[raw/Flex-Port_Flex-Smib_Weekly-December-1-2025.md]]
- **Board work + January deferral.** Alekseev fixed cable/interface boards (HFM-270/274/275), Genadi to send the Minsk address + order quantity; a stray duplicate (HFM-301) closed; Flex Sport production boards arriving for check/connect/test. The firmware-update and negative-scenario protocol changes needing **[[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dima Chudinov]]**'s FlexPay side are **deferred to January** — Dima is prioritised on **Flex-POS and the payment hub**. Val's discipline: keep only 3–4 tasks in progress; everything for next year goes to the backlog. [[raw/Flex-Port_Flex-Smib_Weekly-December-1-2025.md]]

## Dec 8 2025 — HFM-339 becomes a new WSS "cancel-transaction" command; user screens; year-end scope

The [[wiki/meetings/2025-12-08-flexport-smib-pos-sync-cancel-command-user-screens.md|Dec-8 sync + planning]] (same attendees, 59m) turned the Dec-1 approach into a concrete protocol change. [[raw/Flex-Port, Flex-Smib, Flex-POS sync and planing meeting.md]]

- **Add a "cancel-transaction" message to the WSS protocol.** The FlexPort↔FlexPay **WSS** protocol has only a two-phase **request → confirm** transaction and no way to signal a transaction *didn't* complete. Yavorski (with [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dima Chudinov]] the prior week) decided to add a **"cancel transaction"** message, done the same way as the **SMIB protocol's existing cancel ("hensel") command**, so a returned bill ends the transaction cleanly. Yavorski wrote the draft; Pavel to check with Dima whether existing error-handling already covers it before adding it. [[raw/Flex-Port, Flex-Smib, Flex-POS sync and planing meeting.md]]
- **Task tree + timeline.** **HFM-340** = two-phase-confirm protocol (due ~Thu); **HFM-353** = the multi-denomination chain; **HFM-351** = **3 user screens** (progress "$50 of $95…", remaining-on-ticket, and a neutral **limit-reached** screen — Val: JVL can't know *why* the machine stopped, so the message stays neutral); **HFM-352** = reporting. Whole chain **~3 weeks = end of December**. **HFM-255** (machine docking/compatibility) closed complete. HFM-339 handed to the **FlexPay side (Dima/Pavel)** as a dependency; Dima-dependent work stays deferred to January (Dima loaded on Flex-POS). [[raw/Flex-Port, Flex-Smib, Flex-POS sync and planing meeting.md]]
- **Git-hygiene ruling + validator debug.** Val: **whoever works a branch commits its source** — Alekseev commits his EDA export, Genadi his production files. HFM-258 validator still fails on the real machine (works on the simulator, intermittent connect/drop); plan = give Alekseev a **diagnostic tool** + a new **MEI validator** to test independently of the software. FM-274 fixed, 272/275 fixed with 10 corrected boards ordered; interface board + 3 complete sets ship to Minsk. [[raw/Flex-Port, Flex-Smib, Flex-POS sync and planing meeting.md]]
- **Year-end scope (Val).** Finish **FlexPort — complete the WSS protocol — and connect it to the FlexPay kiosk**; Dima-dependent pieces won't make it; the Flex-cabinet repair/upgrade continues into the new year. A recent machine-connect failure traced by **Andrei** (network/infra — [[wiki/people/andrey-bosik.md|Bosik]] by context) to a **DNS misconfiguration** prompted Val to ask for proper end-to-end **network diagnostics** (a separate session). [[raw/Flex-Port, Flex-Smib, Flex-POS sync and planing meeting.md]]

## Feb 5 2026 — the FlexPay↔FlexPort WSS responsibility split (briefing for Aaron)

A JIRA-sourced briefing prepared for [[wiki/people/aaron.md|Aaron]] ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-05-flexpay-flexport-wss-responsibility-briefing.md]]) formalized who owns which end of the **WebSocket-Secure (WSS) protocol** — the shared "phone line" between the two systems. It continues the Dec-2025 thread where the WSS gained its **"cancel-transaction" command** (Dec-8). [[raw/Aaron_Briefing_FlexPay_FlexPort_WSS-February-5-2026.md]]

- **[[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Yavorski]] = Flex-Port (embedded) end + protocol architect.** He builds/maintains the STM32-based board inside the machine and **owns the WSS protocol specification and message formats**, plus bill-validator (ID-003) comms, EGM cash-out forwarding, firmware/bootloader, on-device logging, encrypted-WSS/TrustZone security, and TITO at the hardware layer via Flex-SMIB handoff. Embedded-only — not involved server-side. [[raw/Aaron_Briefing_FlexPay_FlexPort_WSS-February-5-2026.md]]
- **[[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Chudinov]] = Flex-Pay (kiosk) end + all backend.** He implements the same protocol on the kiosk (SAS Cashout/Ticket-In/logging), plus transaction confirm/cancel logic, payment-processor integration (InComm, Pyramid), remote-firmware-update triggering, kiosk-side log storage/export, full kiosk TITO, and the whole server/backend layer (Payment Hub API, registration, time sync, CMS/orchestration, InComm parameter management). **The WSS protocol is the contract between them.** [[raw/Aaron_Briefing_FlexPay_FlexPort_WSS-February-5-2026.md]]
- **Single-point-of-knowledge risk (the briefing's headline for Aaron):** because only Yavorski fully understands the protocol specification, Chudinov and the broader team are dependent on him — making the ongoing knowledge transfer critical. Per-person JIRA keys and open-item status are on the two person pages. [[raw/Aaron_Briefing_FlexPay_FlexPort_WSS-February-5-2026.md]]
- **Derived summary companion:** the raw file `responsibilitie_split_Yavorski_Chudinov-February-5-2026.md` is a short prose summary of this same briefing (same "Yavorski owns Flex-Port + protocol, Chudinov owns Flex-Pay kiosk + backend, WSS is the contract, single-point-of-knowledge risk" content, incl. Chudinov's "21 closed + 2 done" track record). Per §12 it is folded here as a derived-document companion and not separately cited (logged in `wiki/skipped.md`).

## Feb 9–10 2026 — Georgia go-to-market: through the master, credit-note device pricing, data safeguards

The device catalog gets its **commercial/go-to-market** model in a [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] ↔ [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-10-ga-coam-transactional-go-to-market-strategy.md|Feb-10 call]]), prompted by [[wiki/people/sean-fellows.md|Sean Fellows]]'s concern the previous day at the [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-09-diamond-amusement-partnership-meeting.md|Feb-9 Diamond Amusement meeting]]. A separate **written strategy document** synthesizes the two conversations. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]

- **The central principle — go through the master operator, not around them.** Val agreed with the unease of JVL, "a manufacturer of a slot machine, going directly to locations" — the locations are the operator's customers. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] Per the strategy doc, Sean was direct at the Feb-9 meeting that master operators won't accept a manufacturer engaging their retail accounts, but left one opening — a **turnkey deployment/management service for smaller operators who lack staff, provided the master operator approves from day one** — and noted that because these systems connect to **COAMS**, master-operator involvement is effectively mandatory for integration anyway. [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]
- **The InComm-perception contrast.** InComm went directly to 4,500+ locations with a handheld gift-card device and gets away with it because operators view InComm as a **financial-services company** ("like a bank"), not a gaming competitor. JVL, as a slot manufacturer, "does not have that luxury" — hence the through-the-master routing. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]
- **POS pricing = full price + a $1,000 credit note (not a discount).** The POS retails ~$950, "call it $1,000." The operator pays the full $1,000 (a real transaction — "it cannot be awashed"; they must actually pay), then **immediately receives a $1,000 credit note applied against the machine or any JVL purchase** — so it feels free but pulls them back into JVL's ecosystem. Val floated packaging the credit note as a **serialized, credit-card-size metal plate in a luxurious box** (one box per POS), each with a folio number entered against the operator's account; a plain paper note is the no-gimmick fallback. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]
- **Recurring share — $0.20 per card activation to the master.** Val also proposed **$0.20 per card activation** paid to the master operator, giving them a stake in promoting the system. He expects InComm would be "hard pressed to give them more than $0.20" — and offering it doubles as **price discovery**: if InComm pays more (e.g. $0.50 or $1), operators will say so immediately. The two firmed-up proposals (subject to Juan's and Felipe's comments): **100% credit for the POS** and **$0.20 per transaction**. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]
- **The lead sales pitch is security (Juan).** InComm's handheld "in no way interacts with Mother Goose or the gaming system" — it is manual, creating a **theft opportunity** at the location; JVL's integrated solution "takes that theft opportunity out of the loop." [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]
- **Product tiers offered as a menu.** Beyond the POS: **FlexPort** (Val thinks it "an amazing device," offered alongside POS toward an e-wallet solution) priced ~$1,000 retail with **$700 in credit → ~$300 out of pocket** — Val preferred the credit route over a straight $300 price so "the more credit they accumulate, they will come back and buy something from us." A **FlexPay Lite** kiosk — "very, very, very inexpensive," just a screen and maybe a validator, **no printer**, using players' **phones for QR-code transport** so there's no annoying paper. And the current expensive kiosk only makes sense once it grows into a **cash-dispensing ATM**. The message to operators: **printers and bill acceptors become optional convenience items, not requirements** ("you don't need it to operate… you want it, no problems"). [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]
- **Texas recycler-ATM progress (noted in passing).** Ahead of schedule on a **bill-to-bill recycler**, since the **Fujitsu dispenser** hasn't arrived — a combination taking one or two bill-to-bill dispensers side by side in a **~22-inch-wide (just under 2 ft)** unit that fits between two machines. A recycler ATM next to the machine also enables an **upcoming InComm feature converting gift cards to cash through the ATM**. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]
- **Accounting/service data — "JVL knows too much."** Because all machines carry **two SAS-compatible ports**, JVL could push real-time machine/performance data to operators' phones — far better than the current manual Intralot-report pull. But Val flagged the sensitivity: JVL would know more about operator locations/revenue than a manufacturer normally should — "something we need to be humble about… not to be cocky." Two mitigations discussed: keep the **data local to the operator** (JVL has no access), or build a **third-party-verified "Chinese wall"** — a formally isolated, certified data environment proving JVL can't see operator-specific performance. Juan noted that in Class-3 casinos manufacturer dial-in to view game performance is "frowned upon yet accepted," so the same could work in Georgia if positioned as being for the operator's benefit. [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]
- **Credit over discount is the loyalty engine.** A recurring theme: JVL credit beats a straight discount because "a $300 discount is a one-time event that ends the relationship. A $700 credit note is an open loop that brings the operator back." A **~20% recurring-revenue share** and credit against **Flex Sport/FlexPort** purchases were also floated. Val asked Juan to percolate and reconvene, calling it "a good model." [[raw/Strategy_transactional_GA-COAM-February-9-2026.md]] [[raw/JVL_Transactional_Strategy_Georgia-February-10-2026.md]]

## Feb 12 2026 — FlexBill-for-Texas economics; the "never use a memory adapter" design hedge; Flex-Port BOM

From the two same-day Val↔[[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] calls ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-12-genadi-flex-station-flexbill-texas.md]], [[wiki/meetings/2026-02-12-genadi-echo-printer-freeze-flexport-bom.md]]):

- **FlexBill as the Texas answer to the printer/dispenser service burden.** Val laid out the Texas cost model: today a Texas machine carries a **printer** (constant paper-roll/service, invisible only at 2,000-machine casinos, painful at a 20-machine location across a state where "you drive 10 hours end-to-end") plus a **Fujitsu dispenser/kiosk that charges ~$2,000**. His proposed Flex-S config — **one scanner + one printer + one FlexBill ("bil bil")** per machine, FlexPort only — should land at **~$5,000/kiosk** while removing the cash-handling service, which he called "revolutionary for Texas." Existing bill acceptors stay optional ("if they want, they can use them"). The **Flex station** cabinet is built around a removable in-door **insert** so the same cabinet takes Fujitsu now and FlexBill later. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-12-2026-1.md]]
- **Assemble the first 5 Flex-station prototype sets in Georgia, not Toronto** — Canada-built units pay duty, Toronto is short of components, and US assembly starts a US-fabricator relationship (see [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md]] Feb-12). [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-12-2026-1.md]]
- **"Never use a memory adapter" — a permanent hardware design decision.** Genadi reported the Georgia office's 3-machine **freeze** cleared after, on the affected machines, swapping SD cards, changing software, and — on one — replacing the memory and **removing the PCIe SD-card adapter** (a Sapphire card that runs adapter-less). Val had long distrusted intermediate memory adapters ("since the PacMan era… physically it shakes"); he ruled JVL should **never mount memory through a PCIe adapter** — instead mount the **SD card as SATA** (needs a small Linux/device-tree change by [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]]), calling it a **"design hedge" / architecture-level rule** ("~75% sure it's right either way; do it once and forever"). Because it can't be reliably reproduced, JVL won't chase the freeze as a bug — it fixes the architecture. Val to email the transcript to Pavel/Pyramid + Ivan to open a ticket, and issue a **bulletin for [[wiki/people/elizabeth.md|Elizabeth]]** to strip the adapter and re-seat memory whenever a ~$300-repair board comes through service. The next FPS-7 platform will fold this in natively. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-12-2026-2.md]]
- **Flex-Port bill-of-material.** The main board is **~$45 per 100** (Genadi had recorded $50), of which **flash memory ≈ $15 — nearly half** — because flash has roughly **tripled** in price. Genadi to ask [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]] whether the memory amount can be cut and to price **EPROM** as the alternative (Val: EPROM's write endurance is more than enough for JVL, and JVL doesn't need flash speed). Genadi noted the FlexPort currently uses two memory banks; left as his item to verify. A cosmetic **Flex-Port lighting/scanner** defect (black spot / flickering backlight) is a parallel "fix it properly" item. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-12-2026-2.md]]

## Feb 25 2026 — packaging into production; parts to China; Val's Texas FlexPort go-to-market strategy

A garbled Russian Val↔[[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] one-on-one ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-25-genadi-packaging-texas-flexport-market-strategy.md]], 11:01pm) continuing the Feb-12 thread. Hardware progress plus Val's fullest statement yet of how the FlexPort payout devices go to market across the three markets. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]

**Hardware progress:**
- **Packaging (shipping-box) prototype is now in production** ("находится в продакшн"). The packaging debate: monitors must ship **vertically, not screen-down** (like refrigerators — a machine laid screen-down under cargo pressure can crack the panel), and plain foam blocks don't absorb shock, so a shock absorber is needed. Genadi built a box with foam inserts that hold the packed machine firmly on all sides; a sample was already made and brought to the office. Val delegates packaging to Genadi but presses him to work closely with an industrial engineer he calls "Andrei" (whose alternative packaging Genadi had rejected) — "I can't afford to distract myself with packaging personally." (Andrei has no wiki page — identity unresolved; see Owner Decisions.) [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **Fujitsu cash-dispenser drawings updated; parts to be sent to China for reference pricing.** The Fujitsu cash dispenser is wired up and connected in the office (Pavel is aware); Genadi took the missing dimensions and passed **updated drawings with the corrected bezel** to [[wiki/people/mikhail-perelman.md|Misha (Mikhail Perelman)]]. The blocker: Misha has been unable (~2 weeks) to find someone in America to fabricate/send it. Resolution on the call: **send the parts to China (Hangzhou) for reference quotes and air-freight them back** — small parts made cheaply/quickly in China, maybe only ~4 large body/case parts locally; **23 sets** shipped (Val: "we don't need more than 23"); freight is cheap (~$10 per 100 kg) so "even if it's $20 it's worth it." Genadi confirmed the parts went out to Hangzhou that day. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **Flex Sport panel + lighting.** Genadi sent the **Flex Sport** panel back to the supplier to **texture the whole panel** (the sample looked bad/dirty) and fix several bosses with wrong sizes; after that he can finalize the **proof lighting**, which now reads as even/normal. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **POS to tooling; Pavel's unit powered up.** Genadi has switched to finishing the **POS drawings** (the device rendered "пёс"/"PES" in the transcript — the point-of-sale unit, garble) so they can go to **press-mold tooling** (Misha to release the tooling); "faster the better." [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] received the borders and monitor harnesses, connected everything, and got the unit working — **both monitors turned on** — so he can start; the remaining work is the **GUI so the touchscreen works**. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **Bill recycler deferred.** Grafting the bill **recycler** in is small work but held until the supplier responds; the approach (Fujitsu now vs another technology) is undecided. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]

**Val's Texas / Nebraska / Georgia FlexPort go-to-market strategy** (the durable substance of the call):
- **Three markets, Texas the freest.** Val frames JVL's three markets as **Georgia, Nebraska, and Texas**; Texas is "the freest market" — wide open, essentially unregulated. The scheme the Texas market has settled on: machines run a **Pyramid printer**, and the kiosk is supplied by **Fujitsu** (a cash dispenser). [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **Why Val rejects the Pyramid/Fujitsu route.** (1) To use the Fujitsu printer JVL would have to sign a **broad Pyramid agreement** Val fears would forbid JVL's own development — he refuses; (2) it would tie JVL in and hand **Pyramid a monopoly** — he refuses; (3) the Fujitsu dispenser must be **constantly refilled with bills** — expensive, service-heavy, and a security risk; the **same problem plagues Nebraska** (done "on their knees," refilling the dispenser is "a whole opera" and very costly); (4) Texas is so geographically huge ("10 hours to drive across") that **servicing Fujitsu dispensers is unrealistic**. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **The pitch Val made to a Texas operator.** JVL's solution (FlexPort + a bill recycler) lets the operator **eliminate the per-machine printer-paper handling and bill acceptor**, making the operation far more efficient — the operator's "eyes opened" when shown it works and that he could drop the paper/bill-gondola handling. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **Two go-to-market options.** (a) **Sell the kiosk box** — the operator signs the Pyramid agreement themselves, buys only a **dongle** to connect to JVL's kiosk, and JVL sells the kiosk at an aggressive **~$7,000** (from ~$9,000 less ~$2,000 they'll spend on install); downside Genadi raised — **no recurring revenue** on a straight kiosk sale. (b) JVL's **own dispenser device** at roughly **$800–$1,000** (Genadi saw ~$500/unit online). Cassette-denomination economics: dispensing **twenties** (nobody loads hundreds), you can allocate two cassettes of twenties (~$1,200 per cassette-load); a hundreds cassette makes no sense because operators don't stock hundreds. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **The two-variant Texas show pitch.** Offer operators a side-by-side at a small Texas show: **Variant A — keep your Pyramid** (you already know it: paper rolls, a bill acceptor in every machine, mechanical devices that break, and a kiosk you must constantly refill). **Variant B — strip the bill acceptor + printer** from each machine and install **FlexPort**; connect the JVL kiosk. Economics Val quoted: sell the kiosk at **~$9,000** (operators normally pay $12–15k); removing the bill acceptor + printer saves ~**$1,000 per machine** (× ~20 machines/location = **~$20,000**); the FlexPort itself costs ~$1,000 but Val **gives that $1,000 as credit against the machine** (e.g. a $15,000 machine "should" become $16,000 with FlexPort, but Val sells at the same $15,000). [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **FlexPort online promo-meter scheme (in development).** With FlexPort, JVL can run online player acquisition (e.g. **Facebook**): drive a player to a location with a **promo**, they scan it at the machine, and the credits land in a **promo meter**; promo credits convert to the **coin-in meter only via winnings** (promo cannot be cashed — it must be played through, and only winnings move to coins before the player inserts their own money). Lets JVL hand a player **promo credits** inside the machine. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **Sequence and Georgia.** Prove the scheme in **Texas first, then produce it in Nebraska** (Nebraska's existing installed base can't be swapped all at once; Texas has little/no base). **Georgia** = the redemption-center + smaller-machine plan from the Incom meeting. Open hardware question: whether the **bill acceptor fits the Flex machine by height** (Genadi must check); Val leans toward a **card-dispensing** approach rather than dispensing cash, for legal reasons ("the law sees us giving out money — not good; better to give out a card the player inserts into the machine"). Val runs the Fujitsu path and the recycler path **in parallel** so an operator can see both at once, "else they won't get it." [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]
- **Future FlexPay redesign (deferred).** Genadi noted FlexPay will later need the **vertical, narrow screen** redesign Val described earlier; parked for now. [[raw/Call_Genadi_Peari-February-25-2026.md]]

## Feb 26 2026 — the Fujitsu + bill-acceptor + recycler FlexPay carved into JIRA (two same-day calls)

Two distinct same-day [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]]/[[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]/[[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] recordings — a **2:35pm** tasks-planning session Val observed ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-26-tasks-planning-alignment.md]]) and a **3:18pm** JIRA-planning working call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-02-26-jira-planning-flex-dispenser.md]]) — turned the Feb-12/Feb-25 Texas Fujitsu thread into concrete hardware decisions and JIRA features. (The transcripts render *bill acceptor / bill validator* as "билокси / белых сортов / белок сектор", the *bill recycler* as "решает лир / леером", and the *e-wallet* as "его лет / оля" — read by context; heavily garbled Russian.)

- **Backlog housekeeping (2:35pm).** The team closed completed Flex tasks: **Flex Sport (IP 578)** plastic-houser / FlexPort products — Genadi has sent **2 more iterations to the manufacturer** with samples due in ~2 weeks for finalization, judged effectively done and closed; **FlexPay ES (IP 955)** — feature **HFM 277** (Flex-POS pulling) in progress, **plastic tooling design done**, **HFM 288 complete**, **HFM 289** the next story with pulling/manufacturing planned; **IP 1014 Flex Cash Dispenser** — BOM closed, the Fujitsu design closed, ranking done, in progress = hardware development and [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]]'s prototype. Genadi will order an initial **"run" of 15 units** (EMS-sourced, kept in stock); Val had the **harness ordered first**, then the run — a couple of days. [[raw/tasks_planning_alignment-February-26-2026.md]]
- **The e-wallet-generation problem (3:18pm).** FlexPay is used as the money-carrier; today the temporary e-wallet can only be generated **in FlexPay, not in the machine** — a machine prints its own TITO ticket and knows nothing of the e-wallet format, and putting a FlexPort in the machine does not teach it to print an e-wallet. Resolution: **generate the e-wallet in the JVL kiosk (FlexPay), then play at the machine** — but the kiosk currently has **no bill acceptor**, so cash can't enter there. Putting **one bill acceptor into the kiosk** makes the whole loop work (the recycler accepts the money). Leaving a printer on the machine was rejected as "a bad model." [[raw/JIRA_Planning-February-26-2026.md]]
- **The new FlexPay configuration.** FlexPay is to take a **bill validator + recycler + Fujitsu dispenser + printer** (the printer always lives on **FlexPort**). Adding the bill acceptor makes the unit wider — Genadi warned it becomes roughly the width of the current FlexPay. [[raw/JIRA_Planning-February-26-2026.md]]
- **Narrow the vertical FlexPay: ~2 ft → ~1 ft.** Val had planned FlexPay as a narrow vertical unit; he pushed to narrow it from today's **~2 feet (22 inches)** down to **~1 foot** — "a huge deal, very important." Genadi's two-stage plan (Val agreeing): **Stage 1** — build the two variants in the **same existing ~2-ft enclosure** and just drop the recycler into the spare space (one corpus, much faster to prototype); **Stage 2** — proper **industrial design**: rotate the monitor **vertical**, add height, and narrow the body (needs at least one new mold and a lighting decision). [[raw/JIRA_Planning-February-26-2026.md]]
- **JIRA feature carving under IP 1014.** **HFM 400** = the **Fujitsu variant** → add a **bill acceptor**; today it doesn't include a recycler (Val: also add store/recipe support automatically). **HFM 401** = the **recycler variant** — Val explicitly **stops at HFM 401 as already described** and refuses to plan a second recycler variant now. Genadi needs **two tasks** repeated for the new form: a mechanical **redesign** ("Design Flex station with Fujitsu dispenser + bill validator + printer, variant A") and a **BOM** on this Flex — plus prototypes to order, a harness design, and detailed BOM material; he may copy tasks **371/372** or (preferred) create them fresh. [[raw/JIRA_Planning-February-26-2026.md]]
- **Software side under IP 1007 (Flex dispenser software).** The dispenser-software features sit on [[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Alexander Biratka]] (transcribed "Александра пиратка" — a documented garble of Biratka/"Sasha Piratko"; resolved by the IP-1007 software context). The GUI features **FP 496** and **FP 505** were written expecting a **Pyramid printer**, so IP 1007 needs **updated logic for Flex-P supporting the cash dispenser + bill acceptor**. **FP 505** (the "pyramid style redemption" feature) is to have its **description and task name reworded to remove any reference to "pyramid style redemption,"** and re-specified to match the implementation set up for the market (a "Volodya," as transcribed — unresolved), **using FlexPort**. Val handed this rework to **Pavel**. [[raw/JIRA_Planning-February-26-2026.md]]
- **Printer strategy stays split.** JVL still offers machines with a **Pyramid printer** — the **JL Flex 327 / V43** with a printer, for locations that **already run a kiosk**. But in **JVL's own kiosk** they install **not a Pyramid printer but a Tito printer** — "significantly cheaper in large quantities." Net model: either a machine keeps a printer and works with its own kiosk, or the printer is removed and **only FlexPort** is installed. A "2 SAS per config" question Genadi raised was resolved as **not needed**. [[raw/JIRA_Planning-February-26-2026.md]]
- **Making the plan legible.** Pavel is to build a **JIRA filter on IP 1007 + IP 1014** so everything else drops away and only the Flex-dispenser project is visible; he'll have it ready the next morning for Val to review, and they'll meet again to walk the finished sketch. The Claude↔Jira tooling weakness behind the effort (Pavel found Claude "worked very badly with Jira") is on [[wiki/topics/ai-assisted-development.md]] (Feb-26). [[raw/JIRA_Planning-February-26-2026.md]]

A new set of **EM drawings for both HFM-400 and HFM-401** was to be released that week. ## Mar 11 2026 — the "Flex Recycler Slim": a narrow between-machines payout kiosk

A garbled Russian Val↔[[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] one-on-one ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-11-flex-recycler-slim.md]], recording header 08:31pm) working a new **slim payout kiosk — the "Flex Recycler Slim"** — designed to stand **between machines** on a gaming floor. It continues the [[#feb-25-2026--packaging-into-production-parts-to-china-vals-texas-flexport-go-to-market-strategy|Feb-25]] thread (where the bill recycler was deferred pending a supplier) and the [[#feb-12-2026--flexbill-for-texas-economics-the-never-use-a-memory-adapter-design-hedge-flex-port-bom|Feb-12]] FlexBill work, and revives the between-machines "dispensing tower" idea from the Apr–May 2025 FlexPay-Evolution sessions. [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]

- **The device concept + the competitor foil.** Val called this "**the most active device on the market**." The design was prompted by a competitor kiosk Val/Genadi call the **"Silver Kiosk"** (attributed to a maker transcribed "Данила / Danilo"; no wiki page): a single tall box with a **vertical printer + bill acceptor** and an elongated screen on top. Val's critique — the point of a narrow device is to **fit between machines**, but the competitor's **wide screen** and **wide pedestal** both defeat that, so making the writing/body narrow is pointless if the head and base don't fit. Genadi's own model **is** functional; the only fix needed is dropping its **wide stabiliser foot** (which Genadi added so a standalone unit doesn't tip). [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]
- **Width and form.** The unit must be **≤ 1 foot** wide — Genadi proposed **~11 inches**; the **head (where the screen sits) must be no wider than the whole pedestal including the leg**, on the condition it fits between machines. Screen size: **no more than 15 inches** ("what would you show on a bigger screen?"). Layout, top to bottom: a normal **title printer**, then a **scanner** (left protruding under the printer — Val explicitly did **not** want a separate scanner "nozzle"), then a **long dispenser/recycler** slot at the bottom. Touchscreen mounted **flush ("flash") on top**, as on Flex Space. [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]
- **Lighting + symmetry.** Keep the **perimeter/edge lighting** (Val likes it from Genadi's video render): visible even when placed between machines, attractive as a standalone. Genadi noted JVL already has it — **one running-LED line on a single driver**, same as FlexPay/"Flag spray." The face is to be **symmetric, with no buttons** on the front. [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]
- **Vandal-resistant, gapless front (Val's hard rule).** The **front will collect all the garbage** on a location — people spill/pour, throw napkins and sponges, try to light matches. So the front must be a **single smooth surface** with **no gaps, no shelves, no speakers, no visible locks/keys** — everything rolls/drains off. **Gorilla Glass is mandatory** on the touchscreen so it can't be easily smashed (anti-vandal). Electronics should **not** sit in the front (electronics rarely fail if well installed); the only reason to open the head is vandalism → replace the touchscreen/monitor via a **front door / front-access** service path (never a design that forces the technician to bend/reach behind). [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]
- **Cheap-BOM / recurring-revenue economics (§15.8 clear — market/BOM only).** Val's model: make the base material as **cheap as possible** but earn **recurring revenue on transactions** — the kiosk is a "bringer"/loss-leader placed **one per 2–3 machines** (a 6-machine location should buy 2 without hesitation), so it must be cheap. Numbers as said: a slim version could recoup ~**$1,500**; at ~1,000 units, ~**$500** [material]; the rest in "Andersen" metal (garble); a full build with **metal + touchscreen** costs **no more than ~$2,000** — at $2,000 build you can **sell for ~$5,000** ("easy sell, small money"). On the board, using **ARM + a cheap chip**, "up to 3 chips cost less than the memory we pay for FlexPort alone," and the memory is already present. [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]
- **Board choice — reuse JVL's board vs simplify (undecided).** Genadi favoured **reusing JVL's own board** (it has all the functionality + lighting) rather than doing a new design with [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]]; he argued the board's main cost is its **memory**, which this product doesn't need, roughly **halving the price**. Val pushed back that it is still an oversized board and wants everything **"simple and primitive"**; Genadi agreed but noted that means less functionality and that the **feature set must be recorded first**. Left for a separate features discussion — Genadi to send the render so Val can compose the features. [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]
- **Low-power door-monitoring mode.** Val confirmed the kiosk should support the machine's existing **low-power mode** — on power loss a battery keeps a **door-monitor running for ~3 months** — reusing the circuit already built for the machines, "even if the law doesn't require it." Lab **certification is not pursued** for these markets. [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]
- **Pedestal + anti-theft.** The metal **pedestal** (where the access doors are) must be **heavy metal like the machines**; make it in **two sizes**; it can carry a **laminate** finish. Consider **bolting it to the floor and/or the machines** so the units can't be stolen or carried off (there is a bridge/hook at the back already; add a hole and side fasteners). [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]
- **Prototype + tooling status.** The current **black-and-white plastic prototypes** (ordered one white, two black) assembled well and are **strong enough**, though Genadi had to correct/improve a lot; the texture is visibly "built-up," and a **properly-textured** plastic will look much better. New tooling forms take realistically **~7–8 weeks**; Genadi has started the drawings, will lay everything out and **send to a vendor ("Quaternion", garble) for pricing**, and expects a first batch of ~**10 units**. Sequence agreed: finish the current tooling release (~1–2 days) then focus on the **recycler slim** while the design is fresh; Genadi to **add a scanner** and send Val the render for the ad/features. [[raw/Flex-Recyler-Slim-Meeting-March-11-2026.md]]

## Mar 20 2026 — a dedicated product manager for transactional equipment

On a short Val↔[[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]] call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-03-20-andrei-klimovich-product-manager-role.md]], recording header 07:43pm), Val announced that as of **April 26** [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] moves **from "head of sales, amusement" to Product Manager, Transactional Equipment** — the first person dedicated to the commercial/product-management side of JVL's transactional gear (the FlexPort / FlexPay / POS / Tower / recycler line this topic covers). His brief: **gather market requirements and develop/manage the product capabilities** of transactional equipment from a customer/market perspective, across **Texas, Georgia and Nebraska**, with **dual reporting** to Juan and to Val. This complements the [[#feb-25-2026--packaging-into-production-parts-to-china-vals-texas-flexport-go-to-market-strategy|Feb-25]] / Feb-9–10 Georgia go-to-market work (which had defined the *selling model*) by putting an owner on the *product* side. On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-04-06-flex-pos-scheduling-gap-analysis.md|Apr-6 scheduling-gap session]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]] walked the full set of microservices that must converge for a Flex-POS release, each with its Jira "IP" number and owner (the release-schedule and Jira-dependency angle is on [[wiki/topics/github-release-management-pipeline.md]] (Apr-6)). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-POS_Scheduling_Gap_Analysis-1-April-6-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-POS_Scheduling_Gap_Analysis-April-6-2026.md]]

- **Services under the Orchestration Platform** (owners as stated by Pavel): **IP-10-01 Terminal Hub Service** ([[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dima]] & [[wiki/people/sergey-tsymanov.md|Sergei]]) — registers Flex-POS terminals and future terminals; the binding layer. **IP-10-02 Customer Management** (Dima & Sergei) — creating customers, linking plates, customer data. **IP-10-11 Location Management Service** (Dima & Sergei) — binds payment terminals to locations, provides a client GUI and notifications (e.g. payment failures). **IP-10-15 Location Billing & Timing** ([[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan]]). **IP-6-32 Payment Hub Web Service** (Nathan) — issues cards for a specific terminal/location. - **Flex-POS proper and hardware:** **IP-902 Flex-POS** ([[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dima]], primarily) — the Flex-POS application on the **RK3568** platform; **IP-977** the RK3568 development infrastructure; **IP-955 Flex-POS as a device** ([[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]]) — the hardware-side work on the physical device. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-POS_Scheduling_Gap_Analysis-1-April-6-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-POS_Scheduling_Gap_Analysis-April-6-2026.md]]

[[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-POS_Scheduling_Gap_Analysis-2-April-6-2026.md]]

## Apr 10 2026 — Flex-POS alpha build in Georgia: schedule, 3D-printed casings, vendor search (Session 3)

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-04-10-flex-pos-alpha-build-schedule.md|Apr-10 Session 3 (final schedule alignment)]] — the third session of the Apr-6 convergence week ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]) — the team planned the **alpha sample build** for Flex-POS, to be assembled on-site in the state of **Georgia**. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_Build_Schedule-April-10-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_POS_Convergence_Review_S3-1-April-10-2026.md]]

- **Alpha station + software timeline.** Alpha stations will be ready no earlier than **April 27, 2026**; that is the deadline for delivering the **alpha software build** for installation on them. April 20–21 are holidays (Mon–Tue) but April 25 (Fri) is a working day, giving a compressed window. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_Build_Schedule-April-10-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_POS_Convergence_Review_S3-2-April-10-2026.md]]
- **Hardware dependency chain + Genadi's role.** **HM 505 sits under HM 508, under IP 955** (Flex-POS as a device). [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] (not present) needs to order **3D-printed plastic casing components** for delivery to Georgia so the remaining parts can be shipped for on-site assembly. Val will speak to Genadi separately and create the ordering task in Jira linked to HM 505. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_Build_Schedule-April-10-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_POS_Convergence_Review_S3-1-April-10-2026.md]]
- **The alpha-assembly task.** [[wiki/people/elizabeth.md|Elizabeth]] (also heard as "Alisa" in the transcript) is assigned to **manufacture 2 alpha samples from 3D-printed ("contemporary/temporary") plastic between April 27–30** — a minimal production run — created in Jira during the meeting as a story under the existing "Flex-POS Transition to Production" feature (Jira/release-tracking detail on [[wiki/topics/github-release-management-pipeline.md]] (Apr-10)). A follow-up task for software installation on the assembled stations was noted as next. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_Build_Schedule-April-10-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_POS_Convergence_Review_S3-2-April-10-2026.md]]
- **3D-printing vendor search (device economics, §15.8-clear).** A Minsk colleague "Dima" contacted ~20+ 3D-print companies in Minsk. One quoted ~**$3,500 for 3 sets**; another offered a higher-quality variant at ~8,000 Belarusian rubles (~$2,500) and a simpler variant at ~2,000 rubles (~$700) for 3 sets (~$230/set; ~11 parts per set). Val steered toward **China** (cheapest; JVL has direct supplier relationships; ship by air), or Turkey/Poland. Pavel cautioned the material must be verified so casings don't disintegrate on first assembly. Decision: evaluate proposals + material specs before committing. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_Build_Schedule-April-10-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_POS_Convergence_Review_S3-2-April-10-2026.md]]

[[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]

[[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Production security work deferred from Alpha.** For production rollout Pavel flagged an encrypted-bootloader task using the CPU's built-in mechanisms — a multi-week job, owner candidates Pavel or [[wiki/people/aaron.md|Aaron]] (could go to Sasha Beratko but the Linux bootloader would be hard for him). Not needed for a pilot run. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **IO board — push for a Flex-POS-specific redesign instead of reusing the standard board.** Val believes JVL's standard IO board is expensive ("silly money") and argued for a new layout designed specifically for Flex POS, exploiting the modern ease of IO-board layout to cut cost; Pavel agreed this is a discussion for [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]]. (Val's rough alternative for the HAL-abstraction case: take the standard iBoard + a car-key connector, which would "already work.") [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Cellular modem + GPS — HFM-532 filed.** Pavel filed feature **HFM-532 (Wireless Interfaces Support)** today. Val's earlier written spec — a battery-backed cellular+GPS module able to run unpowered for a time to track the unit during transport — is not in the dev infrastructure yet; Pavel treats it as probably a separate IP/project. Val noted turnkey on-board modules (cellular + GPS + orientation sensors in one part) exist to buy off-the-shelf; bring up cellular first, then GPS, then the orientation algorithm. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Flex POS testing is blocked on the UI graphical layer — Anton Seregin must finish this week.** Backend services are deployed and running on AWS; the missing piece is the graphical-UI services. [[wiki/people/anton-seregin.md|Anton Seregin]] starts on those tomorrow (Apr 16). Pavel's plan had UI ready next week / full deploy by end of next week; [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina]] objected forcefully ("we need it now, we cannot test anything, we have a stopper"). Val backed her — no weekends in principle, but Anton will likely be asked to work Saturday so end-to-end testing can start next week. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **HAL → per-device microservices (Step 1 in C).** Today the Hardware Abstraction Layer is a monolith: when one device fails (e.g. the sound card) the whole HAL stack fails, the app crashes, and the machine reboots (black screen at worst). Pavel proposes splitting HAL into independent microservices, one per device class (IO board, printer, bill acceptor, accounter board…), each on its own communication channel — enabling independent reboot, independent update, and independent per-device end-to-end tests (e.g. printer ↔ LGS). **Step 1 is the split, still in C; Step 2 (later) rewrites individual services in Rust as needed** (Val first read it as "rewrite in Rust"; Pavel corrected). Val's mental model: the HAL has two ends — the side talking to peripheral devices (could be assigned to [[wiki/people/anton-leshkevich.md|Lishkevich]]) and the side talking to the data center (the part Pavel wants to modularize). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Dispenser / recycler — the Minsk logistics squeeze.** The **dispenser** is physically en route to Minsk (Genadi confirmed; the Fujitsu unit) — Sasha will pick up dispenser implementation after finishing "Repa" documents, working blind until it arrives (without the device you can only test happy-path, not negative scenarios). The **recycler** reaches Canada within a week but Minsk not for ~a month; Val says they don't have a month, so implementation proceeds remotely — an acknowledged-suboptimal compromise (Sasha can't insert/extract banknotes remotely, so he coordinates verbally with Sergey on-site while watching the debugger). Economics Val laid out: China-built recycler+dispenser samples weigh ~100 kg on a skid; air freight from China at **~$12/kg = ~$1,200 + taxes ≈ $2,000** total. Routing existing Japanese dispensers or Mexican recyclers *through* China is prohibitively expensive because Chinese import duties are non-recoverable on re-export. A Chinese supplier (transcript garble "MI"; notes read **MEI**) had two recycler units in stock and JVL took both; now sold out and won't reorder. Mexican recycler shipping is ~45 days best case. (Background Val gave: he personally originated the bill-recycler concept for this industry and built one of the first at his prior company "Tashko"/TSC, where Yavorski and "Aleksey" learned recyclers — history, not current state.) Much of the recycler code can be implemented against the known spec with Claude in ~2–3 days. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Flex Pay ICOM module → refactor to ICOM-in-cloud, aligning with Flex Pay Trans.** Flex Pay's existing software (on the Vivan Sousen motherboard platform) keeps its ICOM-communication module on the kiosk itself, whereas Flex POS runs ICOM in the cloud (AWS). Pavel wants Dima or Sergey, when they free up, to refactor the Flex Pay / Flex Pay Dispenser ICOM module to the same ICOM-in-cloud design — big shared-code reuse and it converges the Flex Pay family on the Flex Pay Trans architecture. This ties into the unsettled test-stack/repo/deployment questions (the ~$80K/yr stack delta on the OMNI 2 section). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]

## Apr 15 2026 — FlexPay UX review with the new Product Manager; alpha/beta scope; Georgia go-to-market

Two segments of one continuous meeting ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-15-flex-pay-functionality-review.md|Flex-Pay Functionality Review]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]; recording stopped after an 8-minute orientation and restarted for a ~56-minute working review) walking Andrei's first-hand FlexPay kiosk observations (as a novice user) into product decisions. Andrei is now the transactional-products **Product Manager** (a corporate, not sales, role — confirmed [[wiki/meetings/2026-03-30-black-horse-invoice-payment-review.md|Mar-30]]). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-1-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]

- **Process pipeline + tooling (Val's standard).** Observations must climb: comments → capabilities & requirements → ADRs → specifications → prose → code. The default conversion path is **capture the transcript → Aaron's MCP for Jira + Aaron's MCP for Confluence → Jira capabilities/requirements** (with descriptions and acceptance criteria). Val cautioned against persona-driven user stories for a small team (documentation load) — be selective. Val also acknowledged JVL's docs/tooling are far from perfect (tribal-knowledge problem). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-1-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
- **FlexPay UX decisions.** [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
  - **Allow immediate ticket scan on the idle screen; move EN/ES language selection to the next screen** (today a "Start Play" tap is required first because language is chosen there). Either presenting a barcode OR tapping the screen should initiate validation — a capability change of "a matter of days"; keep educational messaging/a scanner sticker since scanning-first isn't instinctive to casino players. **Agreed.**
  - **Central barcode stays as-is — hardware constraint, no change.** Only the thinner side barcodes read at the reader; the reader's beam isn't wide enough and the landing space won't fit the large central barcode. Accepted as a learnable quirk.
  - **"$0 playable" after cash insert is incorrect — bug vs scope gap unclear.** Scanning a freshly-printed ticket at the kiosk showed $0 playable while scanning at the machine correctly showed $40. [[wiki/people/sebastian.md|Sebastian]] to pull kiosk + machine logs to classify; Val assigned product ownership to Andrei on the spot.
  - **PIN-creation screen (ships in alpha):** move the "Create PIN Code"/"Confirm PIN Code" labels directly above the PIN input box, and change the action-button text between states ("Enter" → "Confirm") so the create→confirm transition is noticeable. Deeper redesign (shrinking/relocating the instruction panel) deferred to beta.
  - **Ticket-validation PIN screen:** remove the Confirm button — auto-validate on the fourth digit, error on mismatch, advance on success (the universal phone pattern).
  - **Email-entry keyboard → QWERTY (from alphabetical) + a ".com" shortcut** — backlog, not alpha; the kiosk also supports receipt by SMS.
  - **Card-preview graphic → all-zero number + far-future expiry** — asset comes from [[wiki/organizations/incom.md|InComm]] (who makes the cards), so JVL asks InComm to replace it.
  - **Idle-screen buttons ("Scan Ticket"/"Add Funds") → gentle looped animations** pointing at the physical scanner and bill acceptor (both already work in idle without a button press). Parked pending Andrei posting a photo.
  - **Player-A/Player-B persistent-hands scenario is NOT a fix item.** A player abandoning a machine with accumulated hands, letting the next player inherit and cash out, is industry-standard behavior — players ("fleas") hunt such machines and the market accepts it. Val's framing: the ticket is a **temporary wallet** ("it's the same as somebody stealing your wallet"); a non-secure cash environment.
- **Alpha-vs-beta scope principle (Val).** Small polish ships in alpha; larger redesigns wait for beta so full market feedback is gathered and implemented in one cycle — engineering must not be occupied with aesthetics pre-alpha, or the product falls into a never-ending polish loop. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Georgia go-to-market timing.** Alpha release is blocked on **temporary plastic casings arriving in Georgia (~2 weeks out)**, so realistic alpha start is **~May 1**, window ~May 1–15; ~2 weeks of setup labor. Customer meetings begin ~**May 15** at the Georgia office with a demo configuration placing **FlexPort across multiple machines alongside an e-wallet config and some TITO-only machines** for direct comparison — the first showing of **Flex-POS** to customers (most have seen FlexPay/FlexPort at the gaming shows). Andrei should be on the ground doing hands-on install ("nothing hits home like the actual installation"). [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]] starts May 1 lining up the right customers for a mid-May "wine and cheese" lunch demo; leave-behind sales materials don't exist yet and must be produced. Best competitive intel comes from the **Georgia gaming show in August** (~500 attendees, local family show; go day two, learn — don't sell). Georgia chose not to regulate the product category, so competition is intense and scattered. Cross-links [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]] (Apr-15). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Pricing model — not yet on paper.** Val has ideas but nothing written or tested; a dedicated pricing-model call was scheduled for **Tuesday, April 21, 3:00 PM Eastern** (Val, Juan, Felipe, Andrei). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Role division (corporate umbrella vs sales).** Andrei is a **corporate entity** providing a marketing/corporate/market-intelligence/product-depth umbrella; [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]] runs day-to-day Georgia sales and fields price/timing/availability questions while Andrei absorbs and steps in when a customer talks multi-location, multi-market or corporate-scale (package deals across purchase/leasing/payment terms, other geographies). Crucially, Felipe cannot cut deals; Andrei can ("the mightiest stick"). In Texas, "Rich" runs sales similarly (a dedicated salesperson to be hired later) with Andrei supporting; in Nebraska, "Denis" does not pitch transactional products but can loop Andrei into a meeting. Val's coaching doctrine for meetings: "less is more" — absorb, take notes, follow up by phone (covert recording devices ruled out as illegal). **§15.8:** compensation percentages were mentioned (Felipe and Andrei each keep their own, non-overlapping) but are **NOT ingested** — role/market/product economics only, no named-person compensation figures. ("Rich" the Texas salesperson and the Nebraska "Denis" are named only in passing and left un-matched, no dangling links — noted in the batch-145 overview journal, not force-matched.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
- **InComm reschedule.** Juan wants to reschedule an [[wiki/organizations/incom.md|InComm]] call so Andrei meets John and InComm updates JVL on its pipeline tech (ATM rollover, digital wallet) — no date committed. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-1-April-15-2026.md]]
