A ~1h 7m status/alignment call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-20-genadi-schedule-review-alignment.md|Genadi Schedule Review & Alignment]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]]) running the active Jira hardware backlog, then turning into a strategic product-direction discussion. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]

- **Backlog status (HFM tickets).** **HFM 507** (PayOS design molds/tooling) — tooling design complete; Genadi estimates **≥4 weeks** for samples from the vendor, +1 week if corrections needed. **HFM 514** ("FIVEN FOR"/critical parts for Georgia) — two catalysts/"cater" units ordered; Genadi believes the vendor confirmed but hadn't personally seen confirmation emails; Val to follow up with **Michael Perlman** ([[wiki/people/mikhail-perelman.md|Mikhail Perelman]]) on arrival timing so the Georgia/"Odessa" site knows what to do with the samples. **HFM 504** (fifth-country order parts) — blocked without the full BOM; Genadi to walk the BOM with Mikhail that day, plus re-order some small items. **HFM 519** (sample plan) — Genadi wants to release the specs + BOM that day so the recipient can assemble. An **audio/Rockchip bug** (no sound out of the Rockchip) is still with Pavel; ticket number uncertain (Genadi guessed IP 977). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **HFM 511 — FDM, not SLA, for the Minsk rapid prototype.** Pavel found a cheaper prototype variant — aesthetically poor ("wire-frame") but mechanically sufficient — so Val and Pavel agreed to use **FDM (fused deposition modeling) instead of SLA/stereolithography**. Val to confirm it's actually ordered and move the ticket from "To Do" to "In Progress." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **Jira hygiene — reporter ≠ assignee.** Val changed the reporter on **HFM 508** from himself to Pavel (a user can't be both reporter and assignee) and coached Genadi on how; **HFM 506** flagged the same way. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **Flex PAY (IP 1014) sample verification.** Pavel is verifying full functionality on "sample strips" — he took this on because [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]] can't; all software and hardware must be verified before production release; the first two working samples were already sent to Georgia. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **Dispenser logistics (HFM 367/370).** Metal parts were ordered from **Huizhou**; the vendor quoted ~**$1,800** for air-shipment. Val recalled agreeing but was unsure of the resolution (he'd told Mikhail "give me one day, you work with him" and may have dropped the follow-up); he'll check status. The Minsk dispenser was received (~Friday) and Pavel + Biratka are integrating it (configuring for "candles"/свечи payout mode); the Georgia dispenser was to ship Friday with a larger Georgia shipment [[wiki/people/arkady-batson.md|Arkady]] would add it to free of charge. Val leans toward **air-shipping the Georgia dispenser** given Minsk's alpha is already live. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **Strategic — FlexPay product definition + pricing paths.** Genadi described FlexPay as a side-by-side cabinet (narrower base, wider top, similar to the express-cabinet form) housing a printer card, cash dispenser, bill acceptor and scanner. Val laid out two go-to-market paths: (1) **support JVL's own FlexSport** directly with the product; or (2) **sell to distributors at a discount** (~$8,000) so they resell under their own brand at ~$11,000, with the distributor handling its own Pyramid-dongle arrangement. Rough economics (product/component only, no compensation): Val's COGS estimate ~$4,000 (Fujitsu printer ~$1,000–$1,080 + bill acceptor + a Vivo unit ~$2,000 + ~$1,500 in other components; Val computed $2,500 + $1,500). Genadi pushed back that distributor volumes are small (10–20–50 units) and questioned whether this helps JVL's core line or just pushes JVL into other OEMs' cabinets. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **Strategic pivot — recycler + FlexSport over the cash dispenser.** Val proposed bundling: sell FlexPay (with recycler) at ~$12,000 and give away several **FlexSport** units per sale (FlexSport nominal retail ~$1,200, wholesale ~$990; JVL cost only ~$80–$100, so giving 10 away is cheaper than putting a printer — ~$300 Pyramid, ~$500 "Operator Nami" — in each machine); Val mused pricing FlexSport at $300 to match Pyramid. The stronger product is the **slim recycler-based next-gen (IP 1055)** — slim form factor, recycler printer, no operator, no moving parts (no paper, no print head to clean); Val believes a BOM ~$2,000–$3,000 sells at ~$6,000–$9,000, plus a **$99/month subscription and a mobile app** for operators to monitor fleet performance. Val leaned toward **abandoning the cash dispenser entirely**; Genadi shared the concern that the dispenser targets an existing market where JVL offers no clear benefit ("lots of effort for selling 3 units and more headaches") — but the call was **not locked**, parked for continued discussion all week. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **Pyramid integration / dongle.** Existing gaming machines connect their Pyramid printers via **RS-232** with a proprietary Pyramid protocol; FlexSport sits on the **bill-validator line**, intercepting signals. To put a JVL dispenser into a machine that already has a Pyramid printer + bill acceptor, JVL's own printer and bill acceptor aren't needed — but the recycler or FlexPay must contain a **Pyramid dongle**, which requires a **contract with Pyramid**. Alternative: sell to Georgia wholesalers/distributors (Val cited 3–4 local manufacturers who'd want it but lack the technology) who handle their own integration. No decision. **Genadi's undertaking:** produce two schedules for **IP 1055** — a short ~2-week view and a longer-term roadmap — ideally that day or the next; Val confirmed IP 1055 exists with 3 features and that per schedule Genadi should start design. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]

## Apr 21 2026 — Flex-S cabinet direction: Flex C repurposed, OMNI 2, and the AMOA-2027 target

A ~13m evening alignment ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-21-flex-s-cabinet-direction.md|Flex-S Cabinet Direction]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/inna-levitan.md|Inna Levitan]] briefly at the end) covering a parked Georgia RIS issue and — the real subject — the cabinet direction for the refreshed **Flex S** line (IP 759, cf. the Apr-20/21 Genadi planning). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_S_Cabinet_Direction-April-21-2026.md]]

- **Flex C repurposed as the next-revision Flex S.** Val redirected the recently-developed **Flex C** cabinet design: because JVL is not focused on the casino market and the design "isn't good enough for Flex-C," it becomes the next evolution of **Flex S** instead of being held for a future Flex C launch. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_S_cabinet_direction-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_S_Cabinet_Direction-April-21-2026.md]]
- **Platform contents + the OMNI 2 decision.** The redirected cabinet carries a **new CPU, an embedded mobile/cellular modem, GPS, and OMNI 2** — **OMNI 1 is scrapped** ("I scrapped only one… I'm moving it to only two"). The cellular-modem-with-GPS element aligns with the existing `cell_GPS_capability` requirement. Platform decision cross-linked on [[wiki/topics/omni-core-advancement-and-game-templating.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_S_cabinet_direction-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_S_Cabinet_Direction-April-21-2026.md]]
- **Aesthetics + form-factor open questions.** Move away from chrome to **brushed aluminum and gunmetal**; push a **bleeding-edge V-deck** (find a screen for it) and **thin monitor bezels**. Juan added a market-driven preference (customers, except Light & Wonder, want adjacent main monitors to **physically touch** for a video-wall look — today the V-deck is wider than the monitor and prevents it; fix via larger monitors or a smaller/edge-bleeding V-deck). Slim buttons must stay, but replacing one currently needs a full V-deck disassembly (per Sebastian and Denis, Juan to confirm) — Juan wants an easy top-replaceable button; Val floated (and rejected as too expensive) integrating the button into custom glass. Perimeter lights on the monitors: keep (cheap) or drop (antiquated) — open. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_S_cabinet_direction-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_S_Cabinet_Direction-April-21-2026.md]]
- **Plan and tempo.** Juan to take **a week to re-discover the cabinet requirements** (Flex C as the starting point), then begin a regular meeting cadence — but only after **[[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] gets a week of uninterrupted focus on Flex Slim**. Target: the cabinet comes together by the **AMOA March 2027** show (alongside Texas/Nebraska/Georgia operations, the skill-game line, and transactional equipment). Val told Juan to start engaging **[[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]** on the requirements work — "a good asset" but "too much philosophy," needs to accelerate. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_S_cabinet_direction-April-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_S_Cabinet_Direction-April-21-2026.md]]
- **Georgia RIS issue (parked) + a closing AR item.** One machine of ten still won't connect after the serial-number cleanup; the site uses a single shared generic CMS login (can't trace to one device/employee), so the operator will use a **tablet only**. Val rejected the swap-to-next-machine workaround — keep the failing serial for a **reproducible repro** and root-cause it (Android-heavy Georgia vs iPhone-heavy Canada, and QR-source, floated as hypotheses); Juan to dig in with Minsk. Separately, **Denis** must drive back to **Rapid City** to reload the RIS after DEMA found the bad serial, so **[[wiki/organizations/mg-oil.md|MG Oil]]** can't install the Rapid City games yet (starting on the Aberdeen units); Juan to push for payment on the ready shipment by Friday. §15.8 clear (cabinet/component/market economics only, no named-person compensation). ## Apr 28 2026 — Flex-Recycler requirements/scope/acceptance: the vendor Java library that removed Alekseev, and the new-repo/GVL-API plan

The first of the Apr-28→30 Flex-Recycler REQ working sessions ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-28-recycler-architecture-planning.md|Recycler Architecture Planning]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Alexander Biratko]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]) — how to architect, scope, staff, and write acceptance criteria for the bill-recycler product on the Rockchip **RK3568** platform. Continues the recycler direction of the [[#apr-20-2026--genadi-schedule-review-hfm-backlog--the-dispenser-vs-recycler-strategy-debate|Apr 20]]/[[#apr-21-2026--genadi-continue-planning-sequencing-the-flex-slim-ip-1055-jira-gantt|Apr 21]] Genadi planning. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]

- **The discovery that flipped the staffing decision.** Val opened intending to give the recycler-protocol integration to [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Evgeniy Alekseev]] (who had implemented, but never tested, the same MEI protocol for FlexPay). Mid-meeting, Genadi observed the recycler vendor is the same firm behind JVL's MEI bill validator and almost certainly ships a ready-made Java library; the team navigated to the vendor's GitHub live and confirmed a **2025-vintage Java library** (API + demo + console demo + integration toolkit) whose classes look near-identical to JVL's existing MEI integration. Their own MEI library is a 2016 build they decompiled/repacked over the years to fix bugs. Because no byte-level protocol work is needed — only a higher-level integration on top of the vendor library — **Alekseev is no longer needed for this work** (Val was explicit it's nothing against him). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Architecture mirrors the existing kiosk stack.** Pavel: Linux on RK3568 → a Java backend that drives all workflow/operator/player logic → a browser-based GUI over a known IP. Alexander framed the change as "augment, not rewrite" — keep the architecture and existing screens, change only the recycler-specific operation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **New Flex_Recycler repo, assembled — not forked.** Val pushed back against forking FlexPOS because forks drag in dead code that pollutes and inflates Claude Code's context; Alexander agreed (dead Java code doesn't compile if unloaded but does clutter Claude). The plan: assemble the new product from selected parts of FlexPay/FlexDispenser, then use Claude Code to clean unneeded services/calls; the Linux base (RK3568 root) is consumed as a build artifact from its existing repo, not copied; a third repo may hold IO-board libraries. Pavel clarified the workflow being mirrored is **FlexDispenser's**, not FlexPOS's (customer/player flow almost one-for-one, only the bill acceptor is replaced). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Reuse the gaming-machine IO board with FRAM depopulated.** Val objected to the board's cost; Genadi explained the price is almost all in the 1.5MB of FRAM needed for transactions. For the recycler, no-populating six of the FRAM chips (leaving one, plus a cheap 16-kbit part) drops the cost by ~$30 and yields an already-designed, already-tested board. Adds LED-controller functionality for door/switch monitoring. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Firmware-language change deferred to FP7.** Val wants JVL to move "categorically away from C++" toward Rust; Pavel is skeptical about Rust for STM32 firmware specifically, though open to rewriting the OS-level library layer in Rust; Alexander defended Rust's cross-compilation. Genadi resolved the timing: the near-future **FP7** product needs the IO-board processor swapped to a high-speed-USB part (current one is becoming obsolete), so that is the natural moment to reconsider the firmware language and bring in a younger AI-routing engineer. Val explicitly did not want Alekseev on it. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **The GVL API — a Java interface with a not-implemented stub.** Alexander described the pattern: a Java interface file (methods like `dispenseBill`, `acceptBill`, `getStatus`, `loadCassette`) lives in the Flex_Recycler repo with a stub implementation returning "not implemented"; tests can be written against the interface before any implementation exists, and the work is done when every method is implemented. JVL provides the interface; the implementation (over the vendor library) is written in-house with Claude Code. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Hardware logistics — two samples, one to Minsk.** Val confirmed **two** recycler samples were paid for (Genadi thought one); one goes to Georgia, the second should ship to Minsk so Alexander can integrate against the physical device (many bugs only surface with real hardware, per the dispenser experience). Genadi estimates ~5 weeks China→Belarus; Chinese import taxes paid via intermediaries are non-recoverable. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **CI/CD and acceptance tests from day one; unit tests demoted.** The infrastructure must include CI/CD alongside acceptance-criteria definition, because branches will break main once they start landing. Val stated unit tests are now largely obsolete in the Claude-Code era; Alexander affirmed integration and acceptance tests are the primary quality gate. Requirements-first: feed Claude requirements, code generation follows. [[wiki/people/andrey-bosik.md|Andrei Bosik]] to build a base-metrics "checker" during the buildout. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Requirements plan around Pavel's vacation.** Pavel's last working day is Thu Apr 30; Pavel + Alexander build a Jira sketch (~2 weeks of described work for Alexander) on Apr 29, with a 10:00 AM Apr 29 walk-through (plus Pavel's Figma user-flow link). On Mon May 4 (Pavel away), Val meets Alexander, [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]] (Product Owner, to expand sparse requirements), and [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] (ramping as production requirements lead under Juan) to drill into requirements. UI: the new platform is slim with a **vertical screen**; [[wiki/people/ihar-hrybko.md|Igor Gribko]] will redo screens for the vertical layout, but not until requirements are locked. §15.8 clear (machine/component/logistics economics only; no named-person compensation). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]

## Apr 29 2026 — Flex-Recycler development planning: IP-1059 stood up as its own product, and the REQ gaps that still need authoring

A notes-only engineering-planning session ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-29-flex-recycler-development-planning.md|Flex-Recycler Development Planning]]; attendees not listed) that **established Flex-Recycler as a separate product, IP-1059**, split out from the IP-1014 Flex-Cash Dispenser (separate repository, infrastructure, and software stack; owner [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]

- **Product concept, as recorded:** "the same as Flex-Pay, but the Bill Validator + Card Dispenser pair is replaced by a single Cash Recycler, and the card payout is replaced by direct cash payout." Card-based redemption (payout onto a credit card) is excluded from MVP, to be confirmed with Val. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]
- **The requirements gap that framed the session.** The existing REQs (REQ-1019..1027, REQ-1037) cover form factor, display, GPS, GLI, and audit print, but **do not describe the recycler's core business behaviour** — cassettes, denominations, and the service/replenishment workflow. The team flagged ~11 new REQs still to author: recycler business logic (minimum two cassettes, two denominations per cassette; only configured denominations accepted; payout from cassettes; error/state tracking), accepted-denomination configuration + player disclosure, the operator replenishment/recount workflow, GPS purpose/data model, telemetry over LTE (priority errors → low-cash → full-cassette, to a new microservices stack, not the legacy CMS), diagnostics/log retrieval (network primary + USB fallback), LED and sound effects, monitor resolution/aspect ratio, and a universal repository-infrastructure standard. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Live Jira build-out under IP-1059.** The team drafted the Feature/Story list in Jira during the call — FP-527 repurposed ("Player GUI and Cash Recycler Workflow"), FP-530 ("Cash Recycler Workflow & GUI API"), and FP-534 (Cash Redemption/Acceptance, with FP-535/536/537) moved under IP-1059; FP-528 split into player-workflow vs operator/service-menu screens; a designer story for [[wiki/people/ihar-hrybko.md|Igor Gribko]]. Repository-infrastructure/CI-CD is owned by [[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Alexander Biratko]]; HAL-library (door/tamper/power-state) integration by [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Roadmap as discussed:** repository infrastructure starts the week of May 4; HAL integration ~2 days after; cash-recycler dispense work from the week of May 11; acceptance work + basic error handling late May; finalize positive/negative scenarios on the **real device in early June** (after delivery). Open items to raise with Val (audit-print MVP scope; card payout in/out; GPS purpose) and with [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] (a modem with GPS — feasibility and available modules; bumping platform RAM 2 GB → 4 GB; native monitor resolution/aspect ratio; GPS running independently of the modem). §15.8: product/component/logistics economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]

## Apr 29 2026 — Flex-Recycler pre-May4 REQ daily sync: four personas, and the e-wallet-not-TITO ruling

The evening "pre-May4 REQ" daily sync ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-29-flex-recycler-req-daily-sync.md|Flex-Recycler pre-May4 REQ daily sync]]; [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val Levitan]]) — a requirements sprint toward a **May 4** milestone — reviewed Andrei's draft Personas document live and produced Val's long tutorial on why the Recycler architecture exists. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]

- **Personas cut five → four.** Val told Andrei twice he was "drowning" on unconventional personas (compliance/regulator, bad actor, insurance/risk officer, ADA) and to "scrap them all." The four locked personas: **Player; Location Owner / Site Attendant (merged, owner listed first); Technician; Route Operator.** Player is priority #1. Each persona is structured as brief description / what they care about / how they interact with the Recycler / red flags they cannot tolerate. Val flagged the Operator persona's "what they care about" section as missing and the Player section as "a little bit skimpy." [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **e-wallet is the umbrella, not TITO.** Val and Juan walked Andrei off the TITO frame (a 30+-year IGT casino design needing a central server, kilometres of SAS wiring, redundant server rooms, and full-time staff; entry ~$900K for a 500-machine casino, most >$1.5M — "definitely not a gas station"). The credit-transfer abstraction is an **e-wallet** covering any concrete token — printed QR, barcode, four-digit PIN, plastic card, phone-photographed QR, or SMS-delivered PIN: "there's no standard, no rule." Auth = **QR paired with a PIN**. The token is **seized/confiscated on machine entry** and re-issued on cash-out (not transferable across machines without re-issue). An SMS-delivered PIN would drop the printer requirement entirely. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Why the Recycler exists (Val's deep-dive).** A win means the player walks away with cash, which needs a float; conventionally every machine has a bill acceptor + cash box, so cash-out needs a trusted transfer (a ticket) → hence TITO + server + drop teams sorting bills by denomination + armored refill + locked-up float. The Flex-Recycler eliminates almost all of it — remove bill acceptors/printers from floor machines (saving conservatively ~$1,000/machine), store incoming bills in cassettes that auto-refill the dispensing cassette; because RTP favours the house the Recycler stays filled; two recyclers can sit in one body for busy sites. Bills need "ATM-grade cash" (slightly worn, sorted; brand-new bills vacuum-stick). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Deployment rules.** **No co-installation with machine-side bill acceptors** (they would drain the Recycler) and **no co-installation with third-party redemption kiosks** (Count-R, distributed by Vanilla) — a Flex-Recycler location must be a **JVL-only ecosystem**, with player re-education/signage directing players to the Recycler first. Mixed-manufacturer floors are supported via **meter-pulse sniffing + bill-acceptor tap-in**, and JVL's **Flexport** window is verified compatible with the four main Georgia cabinet suppliers. Phase-one go-to-market = **new/greenfield locations** (warehouse-prepped without bill acceptors/printers); retrofits are phase two. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Revenue model + market frame.** Recurring revenue = a **connectivity fee** to the operator (server/analytics/telemetry/low-cash alerts) + a **processing fee** on transactions to the player (as in Georgia); Juan wants JVL to consider a **lease-with-deposit** model (matching Vanilla/Pace). Income (debit card) confirmed it will enter both Texas and Nebraska with JVL (~$2.50/transaction + ~$5 ATM fee), but US players strongly prefer cash. Market sizing: Georgia (24th, ~40,000 machines) vs Texas (2nd-largest state), which Juan projects "could easily have 150,000 machines"; the **launch target is the AMOA show, March 2027.** [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Texas compliance signal.** The only Texas compliance hook Juan could name: some manufacturers' kiosks do **driver's-license age verification** (under-21/no-ID forfeits the cash-out ticket), which JVL does not support; Juan to ask his Texas contact **Rich Winley** (currently testing JVL games machine-side) whether it is actually required, and whether transactional cash-out fees are charged in Texas today. §15.8: product/market/machine economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]

## May 1 2026 — Flex-Slim device layout: the front-panel stack order and the reuse-existing-molds speed call

A notes-only working session ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-01-flex-slim-device-layout.md|Flex-Slim Device Layout]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]) finalising the physical front-panel layout of the **Flex-Slim paystation** — a ~11.5"-wide single-touchscreen slim variant of the paystation, with scanner, printer and bill validator/recycler stacked vertically. This continues the slim-recycler product line from the [[#mar-11-2026--the-flex-recycler-slim-a-narrow-between-machines-payout-kiosk|Mar-11 "Flex Recycler Slim"]] and the [[#apr-21-2026--genadi-continue-planning-sequencing-the-flex-slim-ip-1055-jira-gantt|Apr-21 IP 1055 sequencing]]. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]

- **Front-panel stack order — decided.** Top-to-bottom: **scanner at the top → printer in the middle → bill validator/recycler raised as high as possible** (to the upper-middle area where the hand naturally rests). This departs from the competitor convention (printer-on-top) precisely because the slim newly includes a scanner: Andrei argued from ergonomics that the two most-frequent/critical actions — inserting cash and scanning a QR code — must sit at a comfortable height for an average ~1.75–1.78 m adult without bending, while receiving a printed ticket is a tolerable third-priority action. The printer has the largest envelope and slides forward for paper reload, so it dictates much of the geometry. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]
- **Single dispenser only; width defines "slim."** Total width is ~11.5" (<12"). Two cash dispensers won't fit (would need ~18", negating "slim"), so **two-dispenser configurations stay on the full-size paystation** and the Flex-Slim is single-dispenser only. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]
- **Reuse the existing paystation screen-angle molds — speed over profile.** Val dislikes the current side profile (rear hump from printer depth) but explicitly endorsed shipping it: reusing the existing molds saves **~2 months to market**, the profile is mostly invisible when units are sandwiched between machines, and "we can always make another model." Visible head-on differentiation stays: an edge-to-edge full-width touchscreen and programmable **RGB GoFlash perimeter light bands**. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]
- **Field-replaceable bezel glass.** The single-piece molded top holds Gorilla glass on a recessed plastic shelf via double-sided tape, decoupled from the LCD (no optical bonding) — so a scratched cover is a cheap drop-in sub-assembly swap (a margin-bearing replacement SKU) without disturbing the screen. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]
- **Open items (Genadi).** Add a **liquid/spill drainage channel** where the recessed glass meets the top housing (runoff destination TBD — Val flagged inevitable drink spills), and **verify both GSM (flat feed + top stacker) and MEI (downward feed) recyclers fit** the new vertical layout with enough clearance for either profile. §15.8: device/component/market economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]

## May 4 2026 — Flex-Recycler requirements working session: the May-4 REQ milestone and the Product/Capability JIRA restructure

The **May 4 milestone** that closes the Apr-28→30 Flex-Recycler REQ sprint ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-04-flex-recycler-requirements-working-session.md|Flex-Recycler Requirements Working Session]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Alexander Biratko]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]) — following the [[#apr-28-2026--flex-recycler-requirementsscopeacceptance-the-vendor-java-library-that-removed-alekseev-and-the-new-repogvl-api-plan|Apr-28]], [[#apr-29-2026--flex-recycler-development-planning-ip-1059-stood-up-as-its-own-product-and-the-req-gaps-that-still-need-authoring|Apr-29 dev planning]] and [[#apr-29-2026--flex-recycler-pre-may4-req-daily-sync-four-personas-and-the-e-wallet-not-tito-ruling|Apr-29 daily sync]] sessions. Only the ~8m wrap-up was recorded (the ~50m grooming ran off-tape). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_requirements_working_session-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Requirements_Working_Session-May-4-2026.md]]

- **JIRA hierarchy restructure.** Val set the direction to **remove the Initiative level** and replace it with a **Product** project type at the top, then wedge a new **Capability** level between Product and Feature. Net stack: Product → Capability → Feature → optional Story → Subtask. (Stated as "contemplating," not stamped Locked — confirm at the May 7 checkpoint.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_requirements_working_session-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Requirements_Working_Session-May-4-2026.md]]
- **Capability migration is Priority #1 (Andrei).** Groom current capabilities into a dedicated CAP project type and merge the **24 existing capabilities with the 8** already in the new model; capabilities already done are simply marked done ("no circle of fictitious development"); capabilities don't need acceptance criteria yet — only requirements do. Andrei catches up on the capability/requirement/use-case side while Alexander (Minsk reopening May 5) begins developing features in parallel. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_requirements_working_session-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Requirements_Working_Session-May-4-2026.md]]
- **Feature-branch engineering standards (Val, on record).** Each Feature = one **GitHub feature branch**, expected to last ~**one calendar week**, with multiple commits per day (~10 rough), PRs sized ~**200–400 LOC**, and a **Claude PR Reviewer** wedged into the flow as the first line of integration testing; acceptance criteria live with the PR-review process. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Requirements_Working_Session-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_requirements_working_session-May-4-2026.md]]
- **Parallel testing mandate (Irina, non-negotiable).** End-to-end tests run parallel to development, never sequentially; the E2E suite is written at the **capability** level (not per feature) and engaged incrementally as feature branches land. Val: "I will never accept sequential testing." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_requirements_working_session-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Requirements_Working_Session-May-4-2026.md]]
- **Next checkpoint:** Thursday **May 7, 8:00am** (moved from Friday May 8 to accommodate Andrei's absence); Val to book it and circulate the notes. §15.8: process/tooling/product economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_requirements_working_session-May-4-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Requirements_Working_Session-May-4-2026.md]]

## May 5 2026 — the unified operator web interface: build the spec internally first

A Russian-language [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] ↔ [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-05-flex-pos-recycler-web-interface-planning.md|Flex-POS / Recycler Web Interface Planning]]) opened the next workstream: a single JVL **operator web interface** that surfaces the Recycler, POS, Flex-Pay and any other JVL ecosystem hardware in one UI, with the **operator persona** (an operator running a Recycler + POS) as the primary lens. The Georgia-trip half of the call is on [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]] (May-5). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]]

- **Spec-before-resourcing (Val's ruling).** The prior external web-development relationship has been wound down (*"мы уже с ними завершили работу"*). Klimovich floated vibe-coding a new site with AI but flagged he does not know how it would handle **security** — needs special attention. Val declined to outsource further *or* to assign internal staff yet: **build the spec internally first, discuss it inside the team, then decide on resourcing** (external developer vs in-house). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]]
- **First deliverable (Klimovich, by EOD/lunch May 6).** A high-level document describing what the unified web interface must do — "at the capability level … for these roles" — covering Recycler + POS + Flex-Pay, dropped into the chat with Val and Juan for the three of them to review. Downstream goal: a specification detailed enough to hand the development team so they can begin the web-interface build. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]]
- **POS capabilities to be AI-generated from existing material (Val's pattern).** Klimovich flagged that **POS capability artifacts do not yet exist** — what Juan sent are Confluence pages, not capabilities. Val's call: feed the existing Recycler + POS material to AI to draft the POS capabilities rather than write them from scratch — he is "90% confident" the output will be reasonable and may even produce a 60–70%-quality HTML/wireframe variant. General working pattern: when capability artifacts are missing, draft them from existing documentation via AI, then refine. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]]
- **Backend stays fully in-house.** Val and Klimovich agreed the backend (which collects parameters from the peripheral devices and feeds the web interface) is JVL's own and is not opened to any outside party. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]]
- **Minsk expectation set as tangible output (Val's reframe).** Klimovich should write Minsk an explicit *expectations* note — that they process some percentage of **draft features** against the requirements already sent — not merely invite questions. The chain Val articulated: advance the **capability** to a state where Minsk can draft **features**, from which the team lays out **sketches / data objects**. A Friday **May 15 8 AM** Minsk call was set as the anchor (see the Georgia-trip logistics on the project page). §15.8: process/tooling/product economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_pos_recycler_web_interface_planning-May-5-2026.md]]

## May 22 2026 — transactional MVP strategy: three-pronged market entry, pricing vs Pax, cash-is-king, and the sales doctrine

A wide-ranging, notes-only strategic alignment ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-22-transactional-mvp-strategy.md|Transactional MVP Strategy]], ~1h 28m, 4:04pm ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]). It began as Andrei's request for a defined one-week priority focus before his Texas trip; Val pushed back that isolated, uninterrupted focus is unachievable in a small North-American company where departments can't be walled off — then laid out the transactional market-entry framework in full. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]

- **Three-pronged MVP path (Val, locked).** The product web spins out from three products — **Flex IQ + Operator's Dashboard + Tito Cloud Server**. Juan wanted the **Flex Card Dispenser** in phase one (the POS must output the card); Val agreed but placed the dispenser **shortly after** phase one, not in it. Together, IQ + dispenser + dashboard sharply reduce fraud and simplify operations; even operators who say "I have no fraud" or "I know everything myself" are, in Val's view, wrong — an "Apple-ish" minimal, flawless IQ + dashboard is a powerful entry point, and the discipline is "taking our time now and doing it right." [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **How Flex IQ works.** IQ sits **between the slot machine and the printer**, intercepting the Moo the Goose ticket cash-out signal. The attendant validates and activates the gift card on IQ; if the customer wants a receipt, the attendant presses a button and IQ **commands the existing Moo the Goose thermal printer** to print on its existing roll — **no new printer needed** (today Moo the Goose prints only the ticket). IQ has **two screens** (one customer-facing, one attendant-facing) plus a hidden internal connector for a **third remote screen**, used in Georgia locations where protection glass separates attendant from customer and the customer-facing screen must be mirrored outside the glass. Gift-card detail does **not** live in IQ itself — IQ issues the card and pushes everything to the **Payment Hub** (JVL's in-house service that interfaces to Microsoft Business Central); the Payment Hub is a hub, **not** an accounting station (it issues no invoices and reconciles nothing). The attendant can also **edit the cash-out value** IQ receives (a design feature and a security advantage, supporting trusted-player negotiations). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Operator-dashboard philosophy — ship a "skeleton" first.** Val wants the dashboard to launch showing the **full future layout with fields visible even when empty**, then populate real features weekly; operators are starved for information and even a correctly-structured wireframe will "make them salivate." Val deferred to Andrei as the dashboard expert (Andrei's HTML draft validated as the right direction). A minimal IQ-only dashboard (no SMIB, no Flexport) can already show everything Moo the Goose outputs (the existing accounting/data source), plus IQ service-state health and gift-card issuance detail. Juan's own view: most dashboards he's seen "sucked," so his method is to export data into SQL and build the reports he actually wants — not what the vendor wants shown. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Pricing vs Pax (program-level economics).** The standard operator objection: Pax costs ~$300, JVL IQ will sell for ~$1,000 — why pay 3x? Val's answer: Pax enables theft (a cash transaction on a register is "another way of stealing money"); IQ + dashboard delivers theft prevention and **account reconciliation** (which Pax lacks), plus the upgrade path to **SMIB** (~$10/machine) and **Flexport** (~$1,000 retail) that eventually replaces Moo the Goose entirely. Upsell sequence: IQ → dashboard → SMIB ("with every IQ I'll give you 6 free SMIBs") → Flexport → loyalty cards/promotions/mail-out card validation. A marketing lever Val floated: **"JVL dollars"** — convert part of a port's cost into JVL store credit redeemable for spare parts, machines, IQ units, or more ports. §15.8: product/market pricing only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **"Cash is king" thesis.** Val: "Fuck the automation … cash is king." The moment a card is issued, the player converts it to cash — either at an ATM (~$5 ATM fee on top of a ~$2 card fee) or via the attendant, who can buy the $50-face card back for **$48** (cheaper than the ATM for the player, keeps them at the machine, builds the relationship, and the attendant earns the spread). Tito and e-wallet matter precisely because they **prevent a cash-out event**; Moo the Goose **forces** one (you get a ticket — "the worst event in a casino's life"), which structurally creates the conditions for illegal cash-redemption. Buying a **debit card with value is legal**, which legalises the whole transaction chain. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Texas / Nebraska market context.** Nebraska uses the same **Intralot** system as Georgia, but its Department of Revenue gives manufacturers no operator reports, so operators there are starved for data; **Texas** has no regulator and no reporting obligation at all — operators know cash collected but not losses, machine overpayments, or performance. Both markets are "dying for information." Gift cards are sold everywhere (Walgreens, CVS, pharmacies), are usable in Texas, and are not blocked by Nebraska's regs; the pitch in these states is **cost savings** — switching cash to cards cuts refill labor, kiosk maintenance and cash exposure, and a new Texas location can launch **card-only** with no cash at all. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Operator-vs-location dynamic.** Operators and locations distrust each other: locations see operators as "idiots making money in spite of themselves," operators see locations as "extortionists" for owning gas stations. Operators want fast bank-account transfer and the location's role minimised; locations want to keep cash exposure and their personal player relationships. This plays especially well in Nebraska and Texas, where operators are trying to **own their own locations** (as in Pennsylvania). On automation, Andrei proposed a fully-automated IQ (customer scans the ticket, takes a card from a dispenser, swipes it himself); Juan noted that breaks the **trusted human relationship** Georgia operators specifically want (only two humans allow "sell me your $50 card for $45"). Val's ruling: it depends on the market (German spielhalle locations are fully automated) — JVL stays **adaptive** (automated / attended / hybrid) per operator preference, and the near-term priority is to **break as few existing cycles as possible**: Moo the Goose is in ~6,500 locations at ~75% market share for 20+ years, and operators won't switch unless forced. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Sales doctrine — "don't sell, listen."** Val's rule for operator visits: don't arrive with a pre-programmed pitch; open with "we have a transactional product family — what are your pain points?" and let the operator describe problems while the salesperson maps which JVL product fits. Coming in with "you need an IQ" can fail because it may demand an operational-model change the operator hasn't accepted. He approved Andrei's Texas expo flyer as a **family-of-products introduction** (no prices, no specific solutions), and was emphatic: "If I see you coming in with a PowerPoint presentation, I'm going to fire you." Be yourself — "either beef or pork, not both." Andrei (not Felipe) is the Texas booth contact for transactional products; the flyer uses AI-rendered machine images (built from blueprints plus a FlexPay reference photo). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **PrizePay and the skill-games/casino line.** Andrei asked about the PrizePay card video Felipe shared; Val confirmed PrizePay is **Incom's reloadable second card**, redeemable at an ATM for a service fee (~$5 in the video). Juan flagged that converting gift cards directly to cash sounds illegal — it crosses the line separating skill games from casinos, which **Mike Patel (GOMOA president)** is challenging because the industry doesn't want federal heat. Val explained the video's operator can do it because he **owns his own ATM** and uses a **non-Incom processor**, putting him outside the regulated chain — and the regulator publicly said it enforces nothing at the kiosk, only at the machine. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **US legal/cultural framework (Val's extended aside to Andrei).** The US is a **constitutional republic**, not a top-down federal-law system: federal and state law, many legislators, interest groups, and courts interpreting the constitution, so the regulatory "pendulum" swings widely over long stretches. "In the US, laws are recommendations" (Juan) — "as long as you pay your taxes" (Val). Two illustrations: Canadian gambling law was **unwritten until 1996** because the 1935 criminal code never defined screens or slot machines ("and we made a shitload of money"); and Seminole tribal gaming began in **1978** in tents and tiki huts with ~300 pull-tab machines — federally illegal but legal on tribal land (tribes are foreign governments inside the US) — growing to **$2.5B/year** by the time Juan left as VP of operations. The lesson for the skill-games loophole: it's clever, it generates state tax revenue (**Georgia takes $1 of every $5 service fee**), and operators will lobby, fund politicians and pay for new legislation if anyone threatens it. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Next capability grooming + near-term sessions.** Val named **Tito Cloud Server** (capability #60, already ~5–6 requirements attached) as the next capability to groom, with **Remote Initial Setup** the "next big deal" after (currently "chaotic and spotty," needs major grooming for elegance). Juan committed to weekend **sample dashboard reports** in two phases (phase 1 = Moo the Goose + Incom card-transaction data only; phase 2 = with SMIB + port). Near-term calls set: Monday **May 25 8am** Minsk call on IQ-MVP completeness (Val + Pavel also invited), and Tuesday **May 26 10am** Payment Hub deep-dive (Andrei + Juan + Nathan) to work out which accounting data flows where. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]

## May 25 2026 — Flex-IQ product walkthrough: POS→IQ rename, the Moo the Goose intercept, gift-card-vs-lottery redemption, and the protective-glass screen

A ~1h40m transcript walkthrough ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-25-transactional-products-review-flexiq.md|Transactional Products Review — Flex-IQ, services]], 12:05pm ET, Russian/garbled; [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]] walking [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] through the product, with [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]] and [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]]) — filling in, at engineering depth, the IQ mechanics the [[wiki/meetings/2026-05-22-transactional-mvp-strategy.md|May-22 MVP strategy]] set at the business level. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]

- **The product is being renamed Flex-POS → Flex-IQ.** Val asked [[wiki/people/dmitri-kovalchuk.md|Dima]] to rename everything to "IQ" (Dima said he finished, though Val still sees tasks under "POS"; Irina noted some regulations still say POS, e.g. items 925–928). The rename is at the product level; the design of the IQ screens carries forward to the Recycler UI ("logical continuation of the terminal product line"). Andrei's call: **stay in the current visual style** for now and later develop a **style book for the whole product line**, then roll it out across all products. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]
- **Device-registration + encrypted-channel security model.** IQ runs on a set of **cloud services** supporting the terminals. Every device must be in a **common device database**; unregistered/foreign devices are not allowed to connect. Only an **internal JVL employee** (with the right role) can add a device — via a mobile app called **Scanner**: log in, then a **double-scan** — first the **QR code** shown by a "naked" unregistered device (empty passport), then the **barcode with the serial number** — which binds the serial to the internal identifier (passport). Operators cannot register devices. Security capability: before registration the device talks over **default connection keys**; on registration JVL generates **per-device secret keys**, so one device's key won't work for another. (This carried over from the old machines to the new cloud platform.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]
- **The Moo the Goose printer-intercept + "confidence number" validation.** IQ splices in-line between **Moo the Goose (Mutha Goose)** and its printer: the Moo↔printer link is severed, the printer is connected to IQ, and IQ tells the printer what to print. IQ catches the signals Moo sends toward the printer, can print the same tickets Moo does, and adds printing of card-issuance receipts. IQ gets the **amount + machine info from Moo the Goose** (not from the printer). A regulation requires validating the ticket's **"confidence number"** — a very basic ticket protection: an encrypted integer field, unique per ticket, checkable against Moo's manufacturer algorithm to confirm the amount. IQ records the number and can cut off a fake ticket with a wrong confidence number — but Moo the Goose is **weakly protected** (the ticket is forgeable if you know the algorithm + ticket sequence, and the in-machine board that catches Moo's signal could, with physical access, be fed any amount), so **Moo the Goose is the point of truth** — if a hack happens before Moo, IQ cannot verify it. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]
- **Redemption paths — gift card vs lottery ticket, and the $5-void case.** On the cashier screen, cashed-out tickets appear with statuses (**Pending** → cashier selects → "issue card" → swipe a blank card on the magnetic reader → player dialog to accept **Incom** terms + service fee → Incom communication activates the card → amount minus service fee credited → player asked for receipt by email or print). Incom's per-series minimum is likely **$7** (~**$2 service fee**, **$5** onto the card). Because **Georgia cash-out is in multiples of $5**, a $5 cash-out can't go to a card (after the $2 fee it falls below the minimum) — so it is handled by **voiding** the ticket on IQ and paying the player **$5 in lottery tickets** (the "not-gift-card" path; from July 1 those locations dispense lottery tickets rather than cigarettes/lighters). Any transaction can thus be redeemed **two ways — gift card OR lottery tickets**. A **split** (part card, part lottery) is architecturally possible but **not** built for the July-5 target. Val was uneasy about the whole void mechanism ("something I don't like, don't know why yet") but confirmed void is necessary to avoid getting stuck in a cycle. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]
- **The copy-ticket printing question — Val doesn't want to print the Moo ticket.** Today IQ prints a **copy** of the Moo the Goose ticket marked "no cash value" (stage-1, to see it working). Andrei's UX objection: the cashier works off IQ's on-screen electronic ticket and doesn't need a per-transaction paper copy — better to make it an **end-of-shift report** operation or a config parameter. Val's stronger line: don't offer it as a menu toggle — treat it as "we don't have it, but if you want it we'll ship you new software" (an administrator-dashboard download when a specific operator asks), and he questioned whether "ticket" is even the right word (probably **audit tickets** / a transaction receipt). **Decision for July 5: block the automatic Moo-ticket print** (printer prints nothing automatically once IQ intercepts the signal); revisit after the Texas trip and Felipe input. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]
- **The protective-glass player-screen problem (parked).** IQ has two screens — **player-facing** and **cashier-facing** — and the player must physically accept the Incom terms/fee on the player screen. But Georgia locations have **protective glass** with only a small slot, so the built-in player screen isn't reachable. Options debated: a **detachable/remote player screen** run outside the glass (Andrei's preference; Val confirmed an internal channel exists for a remote screen), or letting the **cashier accept on the player's behalf** (a compliance grey area — the player could later deny consent). Pavel noted the current USB ports are only for a flash drive (logs/software update), not a second screen, and the built-in screen can't simply be relocated without exposing the board cavity — must confirm with [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]]. **Val parked it: not solving for July 5**; instead he added a **test-location selection criterion — pick locations without protective glass** — and floated moving the second screen onto the **dispenser** (an attaching appliance at the back, with a small screen where the card is taken) since Incom strongly recommends a **card dispenser** anyway (hide cards from open circulation; part of supervisor manipulation-control). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]
- **Operator dashboard, PIN model, roles.** PIN codes (master + cashier) are generated by JVL on request and are viewable/regeneratable in the operator (private) dashboard — already implemented and working: the operator logs in, sees the registered terminal number, and can reveal/regenerate the master and cashier PINs (can't hand-enter, only randomly regenerate). Two IQ roles exist: **cashier/operator** and **master** (a more engineering-oriented menu with device info, errors/telemetry). Dashboard fields are largely read-only for the operator. No IQ-side reporting exists yet (Val: JVL wants **no reports in IQ** — reporting belongs to the application/operator dashboard). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]
- **No emulator; devs mock on their own machines.** There is no IQ emulator; Andrei wanted one to test the feel, and asked to add a general rule to budget time for emulators on future devices. Pavel clarified developers don't use a device simulator — they run the same backend/frontend on their own computers with **mocks** for the devices they can't attach (a partial simulation). An emulator is out of scope for the deadline. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]
- **Val's "zero new features before July 5" ruling.** Val was emphatic that **nothing new** ships for the July-5 (~5,000-location) target: "zero" new features — only close the copy-print question (and maybe void handling). His method: after beta sites are operating (he wants ~10–20 test sites, some free), collect a **big list of everything needed** and address it **all at once**, then release the finished product ~**September** to begin marketing. He batted down mid-call scope-adds (balance-check by scanning the card's barcode on the currently-unused **optical reader**; a self-service card-balance kiosk; QR-based Wi-Fi/engineering hooks) as "record it, develop later, not for the deadline." His framing on the protective-glass worry: "6,500 lottery [locations], we need 10 — don't over-complicate." Next: a **Tue May 26 demo** (6pm Minsk / 11am ET; Andrei travels Wed; Val invited) walking the real device screens, with Irina to screen-share/screenshot if the live remote-screen demo can't be assembled. §15.8: transactional-product/service economics only (service-fee/card-minimum figures); no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Transactional_Products_Review_FlexIQ-May-25-2026.md]]

## May 25 2026 — GSM/GPS modem and event recorder: exploratory hardware/telemetry planning (no decisions)

A ~43m exploratory call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-25-gsm-gps-modem-and-event-recorder.md|GSM/GPS Modem and Event Recorder]], 3:30pm ET, Russian/garbled; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]]) on two components for the Flex devices and machines. Val opened it to "play ping-pong" and reach one direction because "everything is up in the air"; it reached **no decisions** — only research assignments. This advances the open Flex-Recycler hardware question (a cellular modem with GPS — feasibility + modules) parked in the Open commitments below, and rides on the ARM/Linux board of [[wiki/decisions/2025-05-26-arm-linux-platform-flex-devices.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GSM_GPS_Modem_and_Event_Recorder-May-25-2026.md]]

- **The LTE modem is the easy part.** An off-the-shelf **LTE modem** (~**$10** retail) plugs into the motherboard (a full slot on the **FP7/"PS7"** board; a smaller one on **FP1000**; the **Flex** boards have a different form factor — possibly needing a **USB** modem), takes a **SIM**, and with a small software wrapper makes the board a wireless device over GSM. Pavel already tested one modem on **FlexPOS** and it worked remotely over cellular — but that is the simplest use: **no location tracking and no autonomous (battery) operation**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GSM_GPS_Modem_and_Event_Recorder-May-25-2026.md]]
- **Positioning: cell-tower vs a GPS antenna.** An LTE modem gives only cell-tower-based positioning — Pavel put it around **half a kilometre** within a city (imprecise; not all towers publish their location, so accuracy varies and can be unusable in places). Some LTE modems expose a **separate GPS-antenna output** and may cover both functions in one device — needs study. Standalone **GPS trackers** on the market are closed **cloud services** (often Chinese, "can't rely on it") with no USB/serial connector to tap into, so they're unusable; JVL doesn't want to build its own tracker and prefers a ready module. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GSM_GPS_Modem_and_Event_Recorder-May-25-2026.md]]
- **Antenna routing + cost/data economics.** A modem inside a **metal machine enclosure** needs its antenna routed to a plastic/transparent spot (a plafon); the Flex POS/IQ plastic housings are fine. A **USB** modem is its own antenna but is less reliable and hard to fit an external antenna to (Genadi countered it's more universal). Monthly cost is a **carrier data plan per state/city** (any mobile operator; **no voice**, data only). Val floated a **25 MB/month** budget; Pavel said that's too low — realistically ~**200 MB/month** (JVL wants to *increase* the data, e.g. every spin/game result), optimizable via compression, night traffic, or narrow bands. One modem per **bank of machines** is cheaper but complicates things (needs a local wired/Wi-Fi mesh, breaks when machines move, and some **skill-games clusters won't allow wireless** at all) — Val wants each machine **autonomous**, and floated **cloned SIMs / one account for several machines** (a fixed prepaid allotment, e.g. 1 GB included then per-MB) to cut cost. Purpose is **data collection only, not progressive-controller comms** ("only for gathering data for now"). Val's request: get **carrier quotes for multipoint communication at ~200 MB/month/point, experiment ~100 points**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GSM_GPS_Modem_and_Event_Recorder-May-25-2026.md]]
- **The always-on-when-powered-off limit.** If the machine is off, the modem is off. Keeping the modem alive would need a **separate always-on processor + battery** (battery life ~a year to 18 months), which is bulky and complicates service — Val judged it "not a solution" for now and deferred it. Genadi to check manufacturers for a **GSM/GPS + battery module with an open interface** next week (only a superficial ~5-minute search so far). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GSM_GPS_Modem_and_Event_Recorder-May-25-2026.md]]
- **The event recorder ("black box").** The idea: record all events (down to the millisecond before a crash) so hard-to-reproduce crashes can be pulled off the machine via GSM once it recovers. Pavel's read: a **truly external** black box duplicates the machine's own hardware (an autonomous subsystem with a fast link to the main CPU, and still no guarantee of capturing every internal event) — that's a lab/JTAG solution, not a commercial one. More realistic: use the machine's **own hardware** (SSD, RAM, the modem) to monitor continuously without loading the games — with finite recording depth. Storage needs a **real fast disk, not flash**: options weighed were a dedicated **SSD over SATA**, a **PCIe** module, or **USB** (slower, and a third-party USB stick can be pulled — fine in casinos, unsure in skill games). Genadi/Pavel liked a **FRAM / battery-backed-RAM module** (directly on the bus, no overhead, ring-buffer writes, and with a **power-warning** giving ~150 ms to finish writing on power loss) — buildable as a small custom module (Genadi: "not hard"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GSM_GPS_Modem_and_Event_Recorder-May-25-2026.md]]
- **GSM largely solves the log-delivery gap; the hard part is *what* to record.** Today, if a machine isn't on the internet, the only way to retrieve logs/events is manual USB collection — so the GSM modem would itself solve much of the log/event-delivery problem (Val: do it via GSM, or get casino permission to insert a USB collector for a period). With the hardware understood, the open question is **what to write** — well-designed, purposeful data, not the undisciplined "let me log this too" logs that turn out useless during a real crash. Val's simple wants: know whether machines power on each day, plus diagnostics. **Actions:** search ready GSM/GPS+battery modules with an open interface (Genadi, next week) and fast battery-backed **SATA/PCIe** storage modules; Val to formulate the carrier-quote request. §15.8: hardware/telemetry/carrier-cost economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GSM_GPS_Modem_and_Event_Recorder-May-25-2026.md]]

## May 26 2026 — Flex-IQ MVP end-to-end demo: install→setup→InComm→fund→payout, the card-not-issuing blocker, and the counter cable-run problem

A ~1h9m live demo ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-26-flex-iq-mvp-cashier-player-flow-demo.md|Flex-IQ MVP Cashier & Player Flow Demo]], 3:02pm recording start, Russian; [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]] and [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]] walking [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] and [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] through the full MVP, with [[wiki/people/alexei-khamin.md|Lesha Khamin]] and [[wiki/people/ivan.md|Vanya Shpak]] on the Minsk hardware) — the working proof of the IQ mechanics the [[wiki/meetings/2026-05-22-transactional-mvp-strategy.md|May-22 MVP strategy]] and [[wiki/meetings/2026-05-25-transactional-products-review-flexiq.md|May-25 walkthrough]] described. Two halves: the on-screen software flow, then the assembled physical hardware on camera. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FLEX_IQ_MVP_Cashier_Player_Flow_Demo-May-26-2026.md]]

- **Device install + customer/location setup.** Playing the JVL technician, Andrei logged in under his own JVL account, scanned the device QR code and the barcode sticker, and the FLEX-IQ device registered (FLEX-POS came up registered). He then opened Pavel's registration link, entered and verified an email via activation code, validated a phone, and filled in the location. Two Georgia license types are supported: operate under your own license, or under someone else's as a **"Master License Holder"** (which reveals an extra field). **Roles are not built yet** — everyone at JVL currently has full access to the site, and roles are required before real operation; the flow expects a JVL person (Juan, Andrei, or whoever covers Georgia) to check and approve the license-holder data the customer enters (i.e. confirm the customer actually holds a license). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FLEX_IQ_MVP_Cashier_Player_Flow_Demo-May-26-2026.md]]
- **InComm approval is manual copy-paste.** Once a location is approved, its data is sent to **InComm** — today by a separate email. InComm approves, then JVL approves and enters the InComm fields back in; InComm generates these fields per location, per customer, and right now they must be **copied and pasted by hand** each time. The values used in the demo were **sandbox test cards**, and the exact InComm workflow (email vs. an alternative InComm may offer) is still undefined. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FLEX_IQ_MVP_Cashier_Player_Flow_Demo-May-26-2026.md]]
- **Funding and go-live via the Payment Hub.** A license expiry date was set (how long the device may operate), a funding source was attached through the **Payment Hub** using sandbox values, and the location flipped to "operational"; the terminal then showed idle time. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FLEX_IQ_MVP_Cashier_Player_Flow_Demo-May-26-2026.md]]
- **Cashier/player flow — and the card-not-issuing blocker.** The cashier enters a PIN on a keypad whose layout **shuffles every time** on purpose (so nobody can watch which keys are pressed). Last tickets printed by the Mutha Goose are listed, and any ticket can be redeemed or voided; on a redeem the player screen shows the withdrawal plus a fee and asks to confirm. Andrei flagged the wording as unclear — it **doesn't show the final amount the player actually receives** — and noted "accept terms" can be pressed without scrolling to the bottom. The flow then offers a receipt by email or print and the cashier swipes the player's magnetic card — but **the card did not issue: the InComm side failed and a machine/voucher-number field is wrong** (the main blocker to a working payout). Pending transactions have **no expiry** and stay in IQ indefinitely. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FLEX_IQ_MVP_Cashier_Player_Flow_Demo-May-26-2026.md]]
- **Keycloak has no working password recovery.** Both Andrei and Pavel were locked out of their accounts — the shared **Keycloak** auth service had no working password recovery; Pavel said recovery work needs scheduling soon. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FLEX_IQ_MVP_Cashier_Player_Flow_Demo-May-26-2026.md]]
- **Hardware — the in-line splice and the counter cable-run problem.** Lesha showed the assembled unit on camera. **FLEX-POS splices in-line on a single cable:** the Mutha Goose normally runs a DB9 cable to the printer (DB25 at the printer end), and FLEX-POS (which has two DB9 ports) sits in the break between the Mutha Goose and the printer; a separate green **RS-485** line daisy-chains the machines to the Mutha Goose. Andrei raised a constraint nobody had considered: in real shops the Mutha Goose sits well behind the counter, but FLEX-IQ is meant to sit **on** the counter facing the customer — so the connecting cable may have to run across a **staff walkway**. Today's cable is ~1.6 m and can be lengthened; options floated were a longer off-the-shelf **RS-232 extender** (tested for reliability) or, per Val, possibly **wireless** later. Val called it a good point they hadn't thought about. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FLEX_IQ_MVP_Cashier_Player_Flow_Demo-May-26-2026.md]]
- **Legal placeholder must be replaced.** The terms-and-conditions PDF in the flow is a **placeholder** and must be replaced with the real JVL legal document before go-live — [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] is drafting it and Andrei will sync with him. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FLEX_IQ_MVP_Cashier_Player_Flow_Demo-May-26-2026.md]]
- **Decisions.** (1) **Proceed to MVP** — Andrei is satisfied the system is ready to enter MVP, subject to a fix list (endorsed by Andrei and Val). (2) **No abbreviations anywhere — make it house style** (triggered by confusion over the "DBA" field): spell things out on all screens and correspondence, always (Val). (3) **Defer UX nice-to-haves** (e.g. showing the three receipt options at once) to a later release — "we can do whatever we need, just not in this release" (Val). Named fixes going into Jira: find why the card isn't issuing (InComm side / voucher-machine-number field — high); clearer cashout display showing withdrawal + fee + final amount (by July 5); switch the on-screen keypad to a standard QWERTY layout (July 5, OK to slip); schedule the Keycloak password-recovery work; fix the missing/incorrect machine (voucher) number on the printed ticket; fix the Help screen's phone number and email; and test a longer RS-232 extender cable for reliability. §15.8: transactional-product/service economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FLEX_IQ_MVP_Cashier_Player_Flow_Demo-May-26-2026.md]]

## May 29 2026 — transactional device platform: the whole category moves V-1000 → Rockchip

A ~9-minute alignment ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-29-transactional-hardware-rockchip-transition.md|Transactional Hardware Rockchip Transition]], 11:33am ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]) settling which compute platform the transactional devices — **recycler, dispenser, paystation** — run on. These were first built on the **V-1000** (an aging AMD-based processor almost out of circulation). Val said staying on it is pointless — it was always just a starting point and keeping it only wastes money — and the room agreed: the **whole transactional category moves to Rockchip**. This extends the platform choice already locked for the Flex Tower / POS / TITO devices ([[wiki/decisions/2025-05-26-arm-linux-platform-flex-devices.md|Rockchip / ARM-Linux decision]], May-26-2025) to the transactional-device line. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_Hardware_Rockchip_Transition-May-29-2026.md]]

- **Per-device migration path.** Genadi and Pavel had already reached the same view the day before — no point building an in-between version. **Recycler:** build on Rockchip from the start, no temporary version. **Dispenser:** can't switch quickly, so leave the current V-1000 sample as-is for now and move it to Rockchip as the next step (unscheduled). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_Hardware_Rockchip_Transition-May-29-2026.md]]
- **Rockchip build blocked today.** Genadi can't finish the Rockchip build yet: the wiring he needs (the same wiring planned for Flex) isn't done, and he's waiting on an **interface adapter board that Alexeev is making**. He'll assemble what he can, build it so it's easy to retrofit later, and write a short punch list of what's left so others can finish connecting it. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_Hardware_Rockchip_Transition-May-29-2026.md]]
- **Paystation form factor left undecided on purpose.** On bulky-versus-slim, Val chose not to decide: some buyers want a wider unit, some want slim, and JVL is comfortable enough to let the product exist in more than one form factor and let the market decide. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_Hardware_Rockchip_Transition-May-29-2026.md]]
- **Georgia assembly-floor quality flagged.** Genadi raised a gap between the technology the team designs and how things are actually built on the floor in Georgia — the assembly is makeshift and the place needs better working culture. Val agreed; no action assigned. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_Hardware_Rockchip_Transition-May-29-2026.md]]

§15.8: device/component/platform economics only; no named-person compensation.
