## Jun 5 2026 — Texas transactional read from AMOT: a cash market, no debit cards, the per-transaction-fee opening, and Flex-IQ as more than a debit product

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-05-amot-texas-market-scouting.md|Jun-5 AMOT Texas report-back]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe Miranda]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]) the trip's transactional-product findings were reported and Val reframed how the Flex devices should enter Texas. The market-structure / distribution substance is on [[wiki/projects/texas-used-machine-distribution.md]] (Jun-5). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_AMOT_Texas_Market_Scouting-June-5-2026.md]]

- **How Texas players pay today.** Almost everything is **cash**. Very few TITO setups; most locations print a ticket from a **pyramid printer** that the player takes to a cashier or a cash-out kiosk. Oddities seen: a **"silver pellet"** scheme (one owner, redeemed at an attached jewelry shop, no fee); a **"match"** mechanic (the location matches your bet but raises the minimum cash-out); and **"sweepstakes"-style PC stations** with electronic wallets. **No location charges any per-transaction fee.** Kiosks run about **$7K**; cash **dispensers** are common, **recyclers** rare. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_AMOT_Texas_Market_Scouting-June-5-2026.md]]
- **Felipe / Andrei's product read.** A **cash dispenser is welcome and needed**; **debit-card products (FlexPay, and Flex-IQ as currently built) are not**, because it is a cash market and players won't trade real cash for a card. The biggest operational gap they saw: **players can't move between machines** — they must cash out at each one individually. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_AMOT_Texas_Market_Scouting-June-5-2026.md]]
- **Val's per-transaction-fee opening.** Val agreed debit cards are wrong for Texas but pressed that **no one charges a fee today only because the market is new** and operators just chase demand; as stable operators move in they look for efficiency. If JVL offers a **menu option to add a small ATM-style charge (~$2–5)**, smart operators will adopt it to cover costs and the market will copy fast. Juan's benchmark: one ATM at the **Tampa Hard Rock** earns **Seminole Gaming ~$400K/month** in fees. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_AMOT_Texas_Market_Scouting-June-5-2026.md]]
- **Flex-IQ is not debit-card-only (Val corrected Andrei).** Flex-IQ can issue a **QR ticket or a plastic card as an e-wallet**, let players **roam machine to machine**, and act as a **central accounting / reconciliation point** — so a location stops opening every machine by hand to tally cash. Paired with **FlexPort**, a single ticket issued once at the counter can move around all machines. For TITO environments Val would pair a standard TITO printer + bill acceptor with **IQ as the central accounting layer**. Decisions: debit cards are wrong for Texas (cash market beats cards); **Flex-IQ is to be treated as more than a debit-card product** (QR / plastic-card e-wallet, machine-to-machine roaming, centralized accounting). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_AMOT_Texas_Market_Scouting-June-5-2026.md]]
- **Val's ask.** He was frustrated the team returned with observations but **no concrete product proposals** and asked Andrei to bring tangible proposals to the Monday, June 8 follow-up. §15.8: device / market / fee economics only; no named-person compensation recorded (the ~$400K/month figure is Seminole Gaming's revenue, not a person's pay). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_AMOT_Texas_Market_Scouting-June-5-2026.md]]

## Jun 8 2026 — the low-cost "street" machine: sell cheap, then charge a recurring connection fee

On a ~30-minute (mostly Russian) call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-06-08-low-cost-street-machine-design.md|Low-Cost Street Machine Design]], 9:48pm ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]]) Val opened a new low-cost street-device direction driven by dropping street prices — the cabinet/hardware design substance is on [[wiki/decisions/2025-02-27-flex-street-cabinet-design-direction.md]] (Jun-8); the business-model and device-cost angle is captured here. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Low_Cost_Street_Machine_Design-June-8-2026.md]]

- **The device-cost target and the recurring-fee model.** Target a whole device for **under $2,000 all-in** (~$1,200 machine + ~$800 bill acceptor). Pair the low price with a **recurring connection/subscription fee — about $3/day, or ~10% of revenue** for operators who prefer that — justified by constant software updates and "intelligence" (service data, statistical/accounting data). Val floated a sale price of **$5,000–6,000** but called those numbers rough guesses; the aim is to sell far more machines at a lower margin plus recurring fees. §15.8: device/program cost and fee economics only (the sub-$2,000 build target and $3/day fee are product economics); no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Low_Cost_Street_Machine_Design-June-8-2026.md]]

## Jun 9 2026 — TX transactional product diagram: the Flex-IQ + FlexPort player flow for Texas

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-09-tx-transactional-product-diagram.md|Jun-9 TX transactional product diagram]] session (6:17 PM ET, ~1h42m; [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] presenting, with [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]] and [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]]) Andrei walked a swim-lane diagram of the whole **Texas transactional ecosystem** — **Flex IQ** at the location, every machine upgraded with a **FlexPort**, a cloud **host / "ticket server"** as the point of truth, and printers, recyclers and dispensers as the cash endpoints. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_TX_Transactional_Product_Diagram-June-9-2026.md]]

- **The redeem flow, simplified to mirror TITO.** Player cash-ins at the bill validator, plays, hits redeem; the FlexPort prints a **QR receipt** carrying the e-wallet reference (not the value). Andrei had proposed offering a "create-new vs update-existing wallet" choice, but Val and Juan **killed the choice** — it introduces hesitation and reconciliation risk. Instead: when a QR is scanned at the next machine, its value is **fully consumed and the QR is killed** (drained, invalid, cannot circulate), exactly like a TITO ticket; a fresh QR is printed on the next cash-out. FlexPort is **stateless** — the host holds the wallet record. **No partial cash-in.** At the counter the cashier scans the QR on the IQ, the host returns the balance, the cashier pays and presses **paid/void** (a single process). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_TX_Transactional_Product_Diagram-June-9-2026.md]]
- **The wiring problem — the biggest open obstacle.** FlexPort connects to IQ by **Ethernet**, so every location must be **wired up** (a large install expense on 20–100-machine rooms); who buys/installs the network cabling is unresolved, and Val called it "a swamp." Two mitigations floated: (a) a **wireless FlexPort↔IQ link** (works in gray markets like Texas/Nebraska, not in highly-regulated states); (b) Val's **pre-validated encrypted-QR "nozzle"** idea — a cheap RFID-paired nozzle (borrowed from how the "pyramid" printer works) that reads an **encrypted printed QR** only paired nozzles at that location can decrypt, with the printer re-routed through the port, removing the need to wire the room. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_TX_Transactional_Product_Diagram-June-9-2026.md]]
- **Target segment and revenue model.** The **low-hanging fruit is fully-manual game rooms with no dispenser/kiosk** (many exist); third-party dispensers (competitor "Vanilla Pace") won't open up. JVL's income is **hardware plus flat fees for optional reporting** (basic money-in/out reconciliation up to per-game slot performance) — **never a percentage of cash in/out** ("stay out of their business"). Val's blocker list before any pilot: **no host, IQ doesn't yet talk to ports, no reconciliation/accounting piece.** Andrei to write a short two-language brief; Val to take it to the engineers, cautioning Andrei not to pull Pavel into the encryption idea yet. §15.8: device/market/fee economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_TX_Transactional_Product_Diagram-June-9-2026.md]]

## Jun 11 2026 — passport/Dallas registration: the "dsKey not registered" block, and how to verify a chip against the record

A short Georgia field-incident call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-06-11-passport-scan-dskey-malfunction.md|Passport Scan / dsKey Malfunction]], ~10:49am ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]], [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dzmitry Chudzinau]]) exposed a concrete detail of how JVL registers a device against its **Dallas** security chip — the mechanism behind the "passport re-registration on power-up" requirement recorded above. The field/incident narrative is on [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]] (Jun-11); the durable architecture point is here. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Passport_Scan_dsKey_Malfunction-June-11-2026.md]]

- **The registration record encodes the Dallas number, and a mismatch blocks the machine.** After a software upgrade, scanning the machine passport returns **"dsKey not registered"** and blocks the machine — the block fires when the chip presented doesn't match the registration on file (Minsk's reading: the Dallas was replaced). The verification method the team settled on makes the mechanism explicit: the **Dallas number is encoded inside the system-generated registration record**, so Minsk can **decode** it and compare it against the **actual Dallas number read from the machine's logs**; if the two don't match, the fault is on the software side. This is the payout-device counterpart to the Aug-2025 hot-swap/provisioning pattern (a device auto-configures on power-up but its identity is keyed to a chip/serial in a master record). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Passport_Scan_dsKey_Malfunction-June-11-2026.md]]
- **Open — whether field reuse of Dallas chips/passports actually happens.** Dzmitry maintains reuse of old passports and Dallas chips does happen in the field (reason unknown, and he lacks the data on which chips are involved); Val doubts reuse is the cause and leans to a software-upgrade bug. Unresolved pending the log-vs-record comparison on machines **229918 and 230217**. §15.8: device-registration engineering only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Passport_Scan_dsKey_Malfunction-June-11-2026.md]]

## Jun 12 2026 — Port-IQ pairing for Texas: the encrypted-QR "nozzle", WiFi-mesh validation, and the bill-acceptor tilt safeguard

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-12-port-iq-pairing-for-texas-market.md|Jun-12 Port-IQ pairing session]] (4:45 PM ET, ~1h15m; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]; Russian/EN, transcript garbled) the team worked out how to pair the **FlexPort** with the **Flex-IQ** so JVL can enter Texas street locations without wiring them — the concrete continuation of the "encrypted-QR nozzle" idea Val floated on Jun-9. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Port_IQ_Pairing_for_Texas_Market-June-12-2026.md]]

- **The problem being solved — copyable paper tickets.** Texas locations today run machines with a **Pyramid QR printer + bill acceptor and no dispenser**: the player cash-ins, plays, hits cashout, gets a **printed QR ticket** showing the win, and carries it to a cashier who reads it and pays cash. The ticket can be **copied or re-printed**, so it is unsafe; the market is new enough that this has not yet caused problems. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Port_IQ_Pairing_for_Texas_Market-June-12-2026.md]]
- **The solution — FlexPort prints an encrypted QR that only a paired Flex-IQ can validate.** JVL puts a **FlexPort in-line with the Pyramid printer**; winning tickets are printed with JVL's own **encrypted QR**, and the player scans the ticket at the **Flex-IQ** (which decrypts/validates it). Pairing every FlexPort to the Flex-IQ **in advance, as a process** (pairing can be done by NFC, by phone, before install, or over WiFi) gives duplicate-proof tickets and "decent security" for a street location — and lets operators eventually drop the printers entirely (cashier hands a QR ticket used like an e-wallet). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Port_IQ_Pairing_for_Texas_Market-June-12-2026.md]]
- **Machine-to-machine play needs a central brain over WiFi mesh.** To let a player carry a QR from one machine to another (rather than only cashing out), some device must know whether that ticket was **already redeemed** — so the **Flex-IQ is the single point of truth** and the FlexPorts talk to it (and each other) over a **WiFi mesh**. Pavel proposed a cheap **ESP32** processor (built-in WiFi, an "ESP" mesh protocol that phones can't see, ~km range) as the radio. There is **no internet at these locations**; Flex-IQ can carry an **LTE modem** (preliminary pricing ~$3–6/month, so not worth running wired internet). Wires cannot be run at street locations, so a **secure-WiFi auto-config** is the elegant target Val most wants. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Port_IQ_Pairing_for_Texas_Market-June-12-2026.md]]
- **The device — a small "nozzle" (носик) mounted outside the machine.** It is a mini-FlexPort carrying WiFi-mesh, a **small info-only display** (Val wants an elongated ~3×1 screen that just reports "reading QR… crediting $80… transaction complete" — no touchscreen, no player choices, since JVL is deliberately killing the "select an amount" behavior and always credits/pays the full sum), a **printer connection**, and **SAS**. Pavel's insight: instead of the unreliable one-directional electromechanical-counter cashout tap, connect **in-line at the printer output** ("print-in / print-out": catch what the machine prints on one port, drive the real printer from another) — every machine can print, so this is a **unified method that also helps Georgia**. Connection can be via **SAS, printer port, bill acceptor, or counters** — Val wants all kept as options. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Port_IQ_Pairing_for_Texas_Market-June-12-2026.md]]
- **The bill-acceptor tilt safeguard.** Pavel added putting the **bill acceptor in-line** too: if the device has a problem and cannot credit money, it can signal an error over the **bill-acceptor protocol** so the machine sees the acceptor as disconnected, goes to **tilt, and stops** — an elegant kill-switch Val liked (Pavel had discussed it with Yavorski before). **Genadi pushed back** that in-lining the bill acceptor bloats the project: each machine's motherboard connector differs, so a technician must **cut and solder** wires rather than plug in-between, and the device is not really "mini" — every FlexPort interface (opto/serial/bill-validator/SAS/printer) stays, giving only ~⅓ size reduction plus the added WiFi. (A small-display precedent exists: Evgeniy Alekseev built bill acceptors with a small screen and knows the part.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Port_IQ_Pairing_for_Texas_Market-June-12-2026.md]]
- **Graceful degradation if the WiFi is jammed.** Any wireless link can be jammed (a ~$30 Chinese jammer floods all WiFi). If that happens, **machine-to-machine transfer stops but the base system keeps working**: the player takes the ticket to the **Flex-IQ at the counter** to cash out (no WiFi needed there), machines still print tickets and accept bills, and because the QRs are encrypted they can't be forged or double-spent — the Flex-IQ also detects the lost port connection as a jam signal, and a port that can't see the IQ can put its machine into tilt. Val parked the deeper machine-to-machine design at the end as still open. §15.8: device architecture and market economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Port_IQ_Pairing_for_Texas_Market-June-12-2026.md]]

## Jun 22 2026 — three transactional concepts pitched to NTN operators (electronic wallet, promo credits, recycler kiosk)

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-22-ntn-operator-introduction.md|Jun-22 NTN introductory call]] (~22 min; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] + two anonymized **NTN** operators — a Nebraska operator, single mention, recorded in prose and queued, no org page) Val pitched three transactional/payout-device concepts. The Nebraska-market context is on [[wiki/projects/nebraska-skill-market.md]] (Jun-22). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_NTN_Operator_Introduction-June-22-2026.md]]

- **1. Electronic wallet / split-payout.** Because Nebraska hand pays run through the state wager system with **1099 reporting**, operators disable hand pays and pay large wins (e.g. **$2,500**) in cash. Val proposed **splitting large wins into sub-$1,200 payouts plus an electronic-wallet balance**, or structuring player "partnerships" to divide winnings below the threshold. Operators saw theoretical appeal but expected it to be **abused by players gaming the system** and to need **state regulatory review**. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_NTN_Operator_Introduction-June-22-2026.md]]
- **2. Promotional credits.** For every **$20 wagered, the machine adds $2 in non-cashable promo credits** (Val ran this on 1980s horse-racing machines). Both operators were receptive — "every player wants free credits" — though one noted the **accounting/back-end integration** with their current system would need working out. (Same mechanic Val scoped for Nebraska games; design side on [[wiki/topics/slot-game-design-principles.md]] (Jun-22).) [[raw/Meeting_Notes_NTN_Operator_Introduction-June-22-2026.md]]
- **3. Recycler kiosk (Flex Recycler) + free SMIB boards.** A **single kiosk per location** replaces the bill acceptors and dispensers in every machine: the player inserts cash, gets a **QR code**, scans it at any machine to play, and cashes out at the kiosk from the same bills it already collected — eliminating bill jams, paper, cash-collection crews, and kiosk refills. Val also offered **free SMIB boards** for per-machine reporting, claiming it would undercut what operators currently pay for that data. Operators called it "a good idea" and "interesting." (This is the Flex Recycler cash-recycling concept applied to a Nebraska street operator.) [[raw/Meeting_Notes_NTN_Operator_Introduction-June-22-2026.md]]
- **Val's credibility framing.** Val cited founding **CashCode** (sold to **Crane** in 2006, which later acquired MEI/Mars Electronics) and sitting on the **Gaming Standards Association board 2000–2006** developing peripheral-device standards. Val set a follow-up for July 6; the operators agreed to consider the concepts, no commitment. §15.8 clear — payout-device and operator/market economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_NTN_Operator_Introduction-June-22-2026.md]]

## Jun 23 2026 — the Recycler pitched to Texas operators: a standalone mini-ATM that removes printers and bill acceptors

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-23-texas-operators-reach-out.md|June-23 Texas operators reach-out call]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val Levitan]] + Texas operator prospects Jordan and Joe) Val pitched the **Recycler** as the centerpiece of the September transactional set. The operator/market side of this call is on [[wiki/projects/texas-used-machine-distribution.md]] (Jun-23); the device economics are here. [[raw/Transcript_Texas_Operators_Reach_Out-June-23-2026.md]]

- **Standalone, not in-machine.** Jordan asked whether the Recycler is the device where a player inserts money into the bill acceptor and can be paid out from the same machine on a win. Val confirmed the mechanic but said JVL keeps it a **standalone device, not built into the machine** — building it in raises machine cost sharply and the Recycler is too large for many cabinets. As a standalone it can be **treated as a mini-ATM**. [[raw/Transcript_Texas_Operators_Reach_Out-June-23-2026.md]]
- **Why it fits the Texas cash market — remove printers and bill acceptors.** In a market running **pyramid printers** that trigger a payout ticket on every cash-out, the Recycler lets operators **remove printers and bill acceptors from the machines** — killing paper, bill collection, and dispenser refills, for "remarkable" operational savings (Val set theft/shortage savings aside as extra). It also addresses the **fraud exposure of copyable printed tickets** (a photocopied ticket could be presented twice): with the Recycler, one ticket travels **machine to machine**, and the player redeems it back at the Recycler for the money originally inserted, so nothing is being refilled. [[raw/Transcript_Texas_Operators_Reach_Out-June-23-2026.md]]
- **Val's history with the concept.** Val noted his former bill-acceptor company was **first to come out with the bill-recycler concept, shown in the early 2000s** when nobody else had it, and said the current product is well-manufactured — so he is familiar with the technology. Presentation of the transactional set to these operators is planned for ~September. §15.8 clear — payout-device and operator/market economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Transcript_Texas_Operators_Reach_Out-June-23-2026.md]]

## Jun 25 2026 — Flex-Recycler decomposed into per-repo services, and the emulator-vs-physical-device test gap

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-06-25-jira-hierarchy-flex-recycler-in-plans.md|June-25 11:06am ET JIRA-hierarchy session]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/dmitri-kovalchuk.md|Dmitri Kovalchuk]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/andrey-bosik.md|Andrei Bosik]]) the Flex-Recycler program was placed into the new JIRA hierarchy, which exposed how the build itself decomposes. The hierarchy/CI-CD and integration-test-ownership debate is on [[wiki/topics/github-release-management-pipeline.md]] (Jun-25); the device/build breakdown is here. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Jira_Hierarchy_Flex_Recycler_in_Plans-June-25-2026.md]]

- **Flex-Recycler = several services, one repo each, different owners.** To ship the Flex Recycler and "give it to people," JVL must build the **Flex Recycler terminal** plus supporting services — Irina named a **location service** (one or two variants) and a **packaged ticket/billing service** ("packaged billet service"), each living in its own repository and each with a different owner (Biratka, Dima Chudinov, and others; **Vladimir** on front-end). Because each repo knows nothing about the others, verifying that (for example) the terminal works with the location service is a cross-repo concern that lands in the shared integration repository. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Jira_Hierarchy_Flex_Recycler_in_Plans-June-25-2026.md]]
- **The emulator-vs-physical-device gap (Irina's live concern).** Compatibility checks can run against **emulated/simulated** platforms "without problems," but the Flex Recycler installs as a **real physical device at the customer** — not an emulator — and Irina flagged that yesterday's missed **bug** ("we did the work poorly, didn't check") was only catchable on the actual machine. Pavel acknowledged he had "gone dark for a moment" on how to test the physical terminal, then noted he'd found a way to run the tests on an emulated platform; Val added that today AI-emulation testing works poorly against **C++ hardware code** (better on **Rust**), and that hardware **tolerances** must be treated as negative/marginal cases baked into acceptance criteria so emulation can catch them — all left as **open theory**, no decision. §15.8 clear — device/build/testing architecture only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Jira_Hierarchy_Flex_Recycler_in_Plans-June-25-2026.md]]

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-02-product-state-of-readiness.md|July-2 9:19 PM readiness review]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]]) the team went product by product on where each transactional device stands. The **August Georgia Expo** is the driving deadline. The requirements/persona method and the Payment Hub operators dashboard from the same meeting are on [[wiki/topics/persona-driven-design-and-orchestration-platform.md]] (July-2). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Product_State_of_Readiness-July-2-2026.md]]

- **Recycler — best-covered product.** Player experience is fully covered; the **root-operator** role is ~80% (missing **settlement and alerting**); the technician / JVL-master / JVL-technician roles are covered for use cases and capabilities but have **no written requirements**. The **Georgia version** — hidden card-to-card conversion, then cash — is **not covered at all** and is flagged high priority. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Product_State_of_Readiness-July-2-2026.md]]
- **Flex IQ — covered only at the player-experience level.** Its basic MVP issues a gift/debit card, pulls the transaction from **Mother Goose**, and prints a receipt; there is **no operator or technician coverage**. It is in **release-candidate** phase, expected to be delivered the following week. It was built **without properly collected written requirements** (files exist but aren't groomed). It was **formerly called Flex POS**, which did have documentation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Product_State_of_Readiness-July-2-2026.md]]
- **Port, SMIB, Dispenser — not August-show priorities.** Port and SMIB are **functional products with initial requirements** but **no personas/use cases**; the Dispenser has very little but can be spun up quickly. None are August priorities. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Product_State_of_Readiness-July-2-2026.md]]
- **August Georgia Expo priorities (decision).** A **beautifully polished Flex IQ with an operator interface** (showing operators their machine intelligence/accounting) is the **minimum**; the **Georgia-version Recycler** is added if capacity allows — which would make it a "killer show." Texas (which needs recycler/dispenser) is secondary to Georgia. (Val, endorsed by Juan and Andrei.) [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Product_State_of_Readiness-July-2-2026.md]]
- **Georgia trip + first hands-on test.** Andrei's Georgia trip was approved (budget a "go," apartment booked for two months, in the Georgia office **Monday July 13**) to connect the IQ release candidate to **Mother Goose + machines** and test the full cycle for gaps/defects — the first real hands-on test of the live product. Juan is to contact **John at InCom** (with Alan) to verify the second/rollover card conversion (ATM-to-instant-cash) needed for the Georgia recycler, and report whether to push for integration — this determines whether the Georgia recycler is feasible for the show. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Product_State_of_Readiness-July-2-2026.md]]
- **Open question — show Flex Pay too?** Flex IQ currently talks only to **Mother Goose**, not Flex Port yet, whereas **Flex Pay ↔ Flex Port already works** — possibly an easier lift to demonstrate. Left open. **§15.8:** clear — device-readiness / show / product economics only, no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Product_State_of_Readiness-July-2-2026.md]]

## Jul 7 2026 — Flex IQ build/delivery state and the move from USB sticks to over-the-air updates

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-07-flexiq-cicd-requirements-pipeline.md|Jul-7 3:20 PM CI/CD review]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]) Pavel described how a Flex IQ build reaches a device today, and the group set the direction for remote updates. The pipeline/requirements-engineering side (deployment tool, approval gate, reverse-requirements method) is on [[wiki/topics/github-release-management-pipeline.md]] (Jul-7). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FlexIQ_CICD_Requirements_Pipeline-July-7-2026.md]]

- **How a build reaches a Flex IQ today — three GitHub actions.** One **builds the binary artifact** for the device; one **makes the installer** (binary + a prebuilt OS packaged into an SD-card image, used at the factory for the first install with the case open); one **builds an encrypted binary for USB-stick field updates** with no case opening. The **USB update path**: the machine finds the file on the stick, pulls it in, and on the next power cycle **decrypts it, checks the version is newer, installs, and tries to boot the new software** — if boot and database init succeed it **commits** the new version, otherwise it **rolls back** to the old one. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FlexIQ_CICD_Requirements_Pipeline-July-7-2026.md]]
- **Move past USB sticks to over-the-air ("download") updates — targeted Sept/Oct, not August.** Val wants remote updates rather than physical USB sticks. Pavel said the infrastructure is **basically built** and the code **probably already exists** (Sasha wrote it) but **nobody has tested it**; with planning and bug-fixing it is doable in a short, reasonable time — but **not for the August exhibition**. Target is **September/October**. Delivery must go through a management step with an approval gate and a small trial batch first (never to all machines at once) — see [[wiki/topics/github-release-management-pipeline.md]] (Jul-7). §15.8 clear — device build/delivery economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FlexIQ_CICD_Requirements_Pipeline-July-7-2026.md]]

[[raw/Meeting_Notes_Bill_Recycler_ATM_Registration-July-9-2026.md]]

- **Two paths Val understands to ATM registration.** (1) **Get sponsored by your own bank** — costs about **$10,000–$15,000** and is a lengthy process; (2) **ride on a company that is already registered** (piggyback on an existing registration) to jumpstart faster. Val has **already approached his own bank** to register independently (path 1), but wants to know whether path 2 is viable to move quicker. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Bill_Recycler_ATM_Registration-July-9-2026.md]]
- **InComm cannot sponsor it.** John said InComm works with ATM providers but has **no ATM registrations of its own** and **does not touch the money-services business (MSB)**, so it cannot sponsor JVL directly. Instead he offered to introduce **Maya Fuentes** — a board member of the ATM Association of the US and a personal friend — as the ATM-registration expert (she later surfaces as JVL's [[wiki/organizations/herring-bank.md|Herring Bank]] cash-management contact). §15.8 clear — ATM-registration/sponsorship economics only; no named-person compensation. ## Jul 15 2026 — the Flex-IQ alpha test: the cashout flow demoed, void→ticket + print-confirmation, and the runway to a real-location beta

On a [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-15-flexiq-alpha-test-walkthrough.md|Jul-15 3:15pm ET walkthrough]] ([[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] → [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]]) Andrei virtually walked Val through the standing **Flex-IQ alpha-test rig** at the Georgia office showroom and the two settled several product changes. The GA-showroom/beta-timeline side is on [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]] (Jul-15). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]]

- **The alpha rig — one table, sandbox environment.** The demo sits on one small table: a **printer, the Mutha Goose, and the Flex-IQ unit**, positioned so you can see both the player side and the cashier side. It runs on a **sandbox test environment**; a **production location** will be added once InCom validates them, to test how invoices are issued and read end-to-end. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]]
- **The full cashout flow, demoed.** The cashier logs in with a **PIN** and lands on a **pending-transactions desktop** (the home tab). Andrei played a machine (a **$64** balance), cashed out **$15**, and the transaction appeared on the cashier side with a **timestamp, a through-system voucher number, the machine number, and the correct amount** in pending state. From pending the cashier can **redeem to a card** (player gets **$13 of $15**, a **$2 fee**) or **void**. The redeem-to-card demo couldn't finish because the single test card was already used — cards must be cleared through an **online app [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dmitry Chudinov]] is still setting up**; Andrei will clear the card and send Val a video of the full redeem flow. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]]
- **Two product changes Val required — print a confirmation ticket, and rename "Void."** Currently nothing prints when a cashier settles a transaction; Val ruled the printer must **print a confirmation ticket on settlement** so the player has physical proof the money was processed. He also wants the harsh **"Void" status renamed** to something like **"Ticket"** (or "Card") — Andrei agreed the flow is settle-as-ticket → print confirmation, and separately plans to redo the on-screen cashout wording so the **$15 / $13 / $2-fee** split reads clearly. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]]
- **Demo layout for prospects — hide the Mutha Goose.** They agreed a clean prospect-demo layout: the **printer next to the Flex-IQ on its own**, with the **Mutha Goose tucked away on a separate table**. The Mutha Goose is still required (it intercepts the machine-to-printer signal), but the point is to show a location owner a clean setup that looks like their standard location minus the box. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]]
- **Two-week runway, and the over-the-air-over-modem block.** Per the agreement Andrei's team has **~2 weeks (to ~July 29)** to stand up the **production server**, verify **remote software updates over modem**, and find a first beta location. The modem update is **blocked — the modems haven't arrived**; Andrei asked [[wiki/people/mikhail-perelman.md|Mikhail]] for a tracking link and got no reply. This is the same OTA-remote-update goal set on [[#jul-7-2026--flex-iq-build-delivery-state-and-the-move-from-usb-sticks-to-over-the-air-updates|Jul-7]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_FlexIQ_Alpha_Test_Walkthrough-July-15-2026.md]]
- **Trial commercial terms (device free, operator pays cashouts + fees).** For the trial the **device and setup are free** (Val suggested **90 days**), but the operator still pays **all cashout money including the $2-per-transaction fees** — it is not free, only the hardware/setup is. Val wants the commercial terms worked out during the next two weeks and needs to consult before finalizing; he explicitly parked the terms discussion for now. §15.8 clear — Flex-IQ alpha-test and device/trial economics only; no named-person compensation. ## Jul 17 2026 — the "IQ down / printer bypass" fail-safe: one card-only flow, drop the Mutha-Goose history, keep the location running when IQ dies

On a [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-17-iq-down-printer-bypass.md|Jul-17 9:00 AM ET session]] (~2h13m, Russian; [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]], with [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] joining ~23 min in) the team designed what happens at a location when the **Flex-IQ** — which sits **in-line between the Mutha Goose game controller and the ticket printer** — goes down or unavailable, so the location keeps operating. The launch on Mutha Goose locations is **August 1, 2026 — 5 devices at 5 locations**, about two weeks out. This is the fail-safe / final-flow layer under the [[#jul-15-2026--the-flex-iq-alpha-test-the-cashout-flow-demoed-void-ticket--print-confirmation-and-the-runway-to-a-real-location-beta|Jul-15 alpha test]]. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]

- **The dead-IQ (power-off) bypass — the fail-safe itself.** If Flex-IQ fully dies, Mutha Goose starts beeping within seconds ("printer lost"), because IQ is a passive device Mutha Goose polls; the cashier unplugs IQ and **connects the printer directly to Mutha Goose**, and the location keeps working the old way. Mutha Goose **caches unprinted tickets** and prints all of them once the printer is reconnected, so cashouts made during the gap are not lost — and the first player arriving at the counter makes the problem visible right away. Val called this "practically not a scary thing." A break-the-chain wiring design (IQ interrupts the Mutha-Goose→printer line) is what lets IQ print its own receipts; Val floated a parallel wiring harness instead, but Pavel explained breaking the chain was an early project decision. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]
- **JVL will not instruct location staff on the wire swap.** Val ruled JVL will not teach cashiers to unplug IQ and reconnect the printer even if it could — "even if we had the possibility, I wouldn't do it" (no monitor manual tells you to plug the laptop back in if the screen breaks). A replacement IQ device **starts from zero — no history is pulled from Mutha Goose.** JVL must instead **see failures remotely**: the IQ tilt / out-of-service screen is to be made **red and blinking/pulsing**, and Dima and Sergei's extended terminal statuses should surface "tilt / Mutha-Goose-disconnected" on the dashboard. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]
- **The final single flow (decided, for the Aug-1 launch).** The long dispute ended on one flow: **every cashout defaults to card issuance, with no player-facing alternative** — only if card issuance **fails** does the cashier get two options, retry or press a button that prints a ticket. The printed ticket is the **standard Mutha Goose ticket**, identical to what Mutha Goose itself would send, and the location then settles it the old way (no longer JVL's concern). The **"Void" button is renamed** (e.g. "validation failed — try again or print ticket") and transaction statuses become **pending / redeemed / ticket**. Andrei recapped it; Pavel committed "no problem, we'll do it by August 1." Val stressed this is **only** for the Aug-1 launch — capability expansions come later, once the market is understood. The earlier **deferred-redemption bearer-ticket proposal** (print a voucher, player returns later to activate the card) was **dropped** in favour of this PAX-like "transaction failed → print a ticket → hand back to the old system" model. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]
- **IQ stops ingesting Mutha Goose's 25 stored historical tickets — real-time only.** Pavel disclosed the requirement to ingest Mutha Goose's 25 cached tickets came from him (IQ had to parse that command anyway, and issuing cards for them looked like a way to earn the activation percentage). Val called that a critical mistake: those 25 tickets are **already cashed** by the time IQ reconnects (players run to the cashier immediately), so no money is made on them, and **building on a competitor's memory helps the Mutha Goose company push JVL out of the market later.** Agreed: **IQ processes only real-time transactions; everything historical from Mutha Goose is ignored and nothing is stored**; the only history a location is offered is what sits in JVL's own hub. Dropping the history also makes behavior uniform whether 1, 20, or 1000 tickets were missed, removing the reprint double-payout path. Val's strategic alternative: give locations **free SMIBs** to expand capabilities through JVL's own hub. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]
- **Boundary amounts where a card can't be issued (new, end of meeting).** **Under ~$10** (e.g. a $5 cashout minus the $2 activation fee leaves $3, too little for a card): Andrei proposed auto-routing to the print-ticket scenario with an on-screen message that the amount can't go on a card; Val liked it pending Juan's opinion. **Over $1000:** INCOMM's market limit is **$1000 per card**, so a jackpot could exceed it and the case is currently unhandled — splitting into several cards tied to one voucher is technically easy, but Val flagged legality, know-your-customer and tax obligations (wins above $1200 are taxable; Georgia treats gambling as illegal, so which tax applies?). All of it goes to **Juan**, who is to discuss multiple-card issuance one-on-one with INCOMM. §15.8 clear — Flex-IQ / printer-bypass / device / field economics only (INCOMM card limits and the $2 activation fee are product economics); no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]
- **Val's market doubt (stated, not resolved).** Val personally doubts the market will accept card issuance — players want cash, a black market will persist (cards bought for cash on the spot), and some locations may keep working with PAX or the bare printer; until a cash-out card exists he thinks the business system (not IQ itself) won't work. Andrei considers that part outside scope. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Down_Printer_Bypass_Complete-July-17-2026.md]]

## Jul 20 2026 — adding LTE modem support to the Flex (V1000) devices via an x86 platform SDK

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-20-lte-modem-jira-structure.md|July-20 6:06 AM session]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/dmitri-kovalchuk.md|Dmitri Kovalchuk]]) Val set the goal of adding **LTE modem support to the Flex (V1000) machines** and framed how the work builds. The Jira-planning side is on [[wiki/topics/github-release-management-pipeline.md]] (Jul-20); the device/build side is here. This continues the [[wiki/meetings/2026-05-25-gsm-gps-modem-and-event-recorder.md|May-25 GSM/GPS modem]] thread. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_LTE_Modem_Jira_Structure-July-20-2026.md]]

- **No source-based build for the V1000 OS today.** Pavel explained there are **no source files for the V1000 operating system** — only a **prebuilt binary package** (Linux plus OS files) sitting on the builder, reused for every product build; a new package was created each time the kernel changed. Two options: (1) the old way — **rebuild the OS with modem support** (~3–5 days); or (2) build a proper **git-based build system** like the existing **RK3568 platform SDK** repository (~2–3 weeks, "3–4× longer"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_LTE_Modem_Jira_Structure-July-20-2026.md]]
- **Decision — take the git-based route (a new x86 platform SDK).** Val agreed with Pavel's recommendation of the git-based approach even though it takes longer: it **unifies the SDK style across platforms** (V1000, FP7, ARM), the team already has a **Linux kernel mirror in its own repository** (made by Andrei, so one kernel version can serve all projects), and the work runs **in parallel with other projects** (an isolated task Claude can grind on with occasional check-ins). It also fits Friday's direction of possibly moving **RAGE to Nova**, which would pull repository reorganization and GitHub Actions builds along with it. A **new repository is needed — an x86 platform SDK** (analog of the RK3568 platform SDK), named something like "Platform SDK x86 Gaming." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_LTE_Modem_Jira_Structure-July-20-2026.md]]
- **Kernel question deferred.** V1000 is on kernel **5.15**, RK3568 is on **6.12** — whether to spread 6.12 to the Nova transition needs a decision later (maybe for Nova; not for V1000 in Georgia for now), not at this meeting. **§15.8:** clear — device/build-system/platform economics only, no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_LTE_Modem_Jira_Structure-July-20-2026.md]]
