## Jul 21 2026 — unbind vs freeze, and what to do with transactions on an offline unit

On the earlier [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-21-unbind-vs-freeze-and-offline-transactions.md|Jul-21 5:15 PM ET call]] (~43m, Russian; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] and [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]) they worked out how a unit is **detached from a location** and how an **offline unit's stored transactions** should be handled — the discussion that then continued into the [[#jul-21-2026--the-escrow-model-for-other-network-transactions-and-vals-owned-network-product-vision|7:05 PM escrow-model call]] below. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Unbind_vs_Freeze_and_Offline_Transactions-July-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Unbind_vs_Freeze_and_Offline_Transactions-July-21-2026.md]]

- **Unbind and Freeze are two separate, independent functions (Val's direction; Andrei agreed).** Andrei described the operations dashboard as forcing a sequence — a unit must be **frozen first** and only then taken online or unbound. Val rejected routing one function through the other. **Freeze** is an operational, reversible action: stop a unit for a business reason (e.g. the location did not pay), reactivate it later, possibly **automatically once payment arrives**. **Unbind** is a different event: detaching a unit from a location. There is no reason to force a freeze-before-unbind chain. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Unbind_vs_Freeze_and_Offline_Transactions-July-21-2026.md]]
- **A unit never silently rebinds or returns to a previous location (Val's core rule).** A unit bound to a location is **always at that location**. If it goes offline and later reconnects at a **different** location, that is an alarm / possible fraud — "red light, siren." A removed unit can **never return to its old location** either; any reuse always requires a **fresh connection at a new location**, which happens automatically on connection. Val argued this simplicity removes the need for a manual "unbind then rebind" dashboard procedure (unnecessary labour) and solves the scaling problem. On replacement: physically unplug the old unit, box it, ship it — the reappearance somewhere else is the trigger to act, not a manual step. A unit can be **re-used**, but only via a new connection; it does not automatically "die." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Unbind_vs_Freeze_and_Offline_Transactions-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Offline units issue no cards — only printed tickets.** Per old requirements an offline unit still **receives transactions from MuthaGoose and stores them locally in a pending state**; while offline the only completion is a **printed ticket** (which closes the transaction), never a card. Val's rule: a card can be issued **only at the millisecond the button is pressed while online**; if offline at that moment, print a ticket only. Andrei's harder case — a transaction received while offline but issued as a card once connectivity returns a millisecond later — Val rejected: only the **state at the moment of the button press** matters. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Unbind_vs_Freeze_and_Offline_Transactions-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Invalid pending transactions on a detached/decommissioned unit are deleted (hard reset) — both agreed.** Pending transactions can pile up locally (Andrei said **could be ~10**, unpredictable). Once a unit is unbound / frozen / decommissioned those transactions can **never be finalized** (no print, no card redemption), so they are **invalid** and should be **deleted from memory (hard reset)** at some point in the unit's life cycle, so they cannot cause problems. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Unbind_vs_Freeze_and_Offline_Transactions-July-21-2026.md]]
- **The harder question → escrow (carried into the 7:05 PM call).** To avoid storing anything at all, the unit must be able to tell MuthaGoose it **did not process** a transaction. Val floated an **escrow-style idea** — MuthaGoose **holds** the transaction (not delivered as processed) until IQ validates and accepts it — but this depends on MuthaGoose's **command protocol**, which Val does not have in front of him; that is a question for **Pavel**. Val then left for an America meeting and continued in the [[#jul-21-2026--the-escrow-model-for-other-network-transactions-and-vals-owned-network-product-vision|7:05 PM escrow-model call]]. **§15.8:** clear — unbind/freeze/offline-transaction/device life-cycle economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Unbind_vs_Freeze_and_Offline_Transactions-July-21-2026.md]]

## Jul 21 2026 — the escrow model for other-network transactions, and Val's owned-network product vision

On a [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-21-escrow-model-and-owned-network-vision.md|Jul-21 7:05 PM ET call]] (~43m, Russian; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] and [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]) they continued the earlier discussion of what to do with **local transactions stored on a machine when a device is unbound** — the narrow case first raised by **Dmitry** (the backend architect [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dmitri Chudinov]], by context). This is the escrow/transaction-flow layer alongside the [[#jul-17-2026--the-iq-down--printer-bypass-fail-safe-one-card-only-flow-drop-the-mutha-goose-history-keep-the-location-running-when-iq-dies|Jul-17 IQ-down printer-bypass]] design; the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-21-unbind-vs-freeze-and-offline-transactions.md|separate, earlier Jul-21 5:15 PM "unbind vs freeze / offline transactions" call]] (whose escrow idea this call continued). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]

- **The escrow principle (Val's direction).** A transaction is created on the machine when received from **MuthaGoose** (status "pending"), then moves to a terminal state — ticket printed or card activated. Val's position is stronger than "maybe we don't need to store it": JVL **should not, and it is wrong to**, store local transactions — because **JVL does not own the local network**. When JVL taps into someone else's network to bind a machine to its IQ, the purpose is to emit **PAX (JVL's competitor)**; not owning the whole network, JVL **cannot take responsibility for storing information — only push a transaction away or process it**. While processing it holds the transaction in **escrow**: if processing succeeds, accept it (transaction passed); if not, reject it. If JVL owned its own network the logic would be radically different. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **The flow — IQ is a "pocket," not the brain.** Pull MuthaGoose data **only to display on the IQ screen** (machine number, cashout amount) — **do not create the transaction in Payment Hub yet**. Only when moving to **card issuance** does JVL's protocol engage: create the transaction in **Payment Hub as finalized**, and tell MuthaGoose the transaction is processed. Cloud services (Payment Hub) talk to **INCOM** directly, bypassing IQ; IQ just relays which transaction to issue a card for. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Fallback if MuthaGoose has no escrow.** Always **print a ticket, then validate that ticket separately, and never pass the same ticket number twice** (e.g. "ticket 777 for $555" validated once, then that number is never honoured again) — the same escrow effect **without a handshake**. Val stressed he is **guessing** about MuthaGoose's command protocol; Pavel can confirm in one sentence. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Not a full redesign (Val vs Andrei's worry).** Andrei worried this substantially changes how the product was conceived over ~2 years; Val disagreed — the base product mostly works as-is, connection and business flow stay roughly the same, and even if Pavel or Dmitry need up to **3 months**, they will still put the test on a location because nobody fully understands today where things are stored or how the flow runs. The **August 1** target is fine. On why the original design blocked the transaction from going straight to the printer: when they designed it they lacked the full requirements and facts (they did not know about PAX then); with all the facts now, they can change their minds. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]

**The owned-network product vision** (a system fully controlled by JVL on IQ, without MuthaGoose): [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]

- **SMIB/SMI** — a module inserted into the machine, connected to the **2nd SAS port**, communicating over **WSS** (as FlexPay and FlexPort do today) or ideally **hardwired**. Val warned **WiFi can be jammed cheaply (~$300 device)**, so run wires. SMIB transmits **everything** through the SAS port — all account and service info **plus bill-acceptor statistics** (e.g. flagging a bill acceptor rejecting **20% of $20 bills**) — captured to the cloud hub and turned into **desktop / HTML reports**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Machine fit** — one SAS port is taken by **Intralot**; the 2nd port can carry SMIB. About **30% of Georgia machines are single-SAS** and aging out. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **FlexPort instead of SMIB** — Val would use **FlexPort** rather than SMIB because it enables **player mobility between machines** instead of forcing a cashout each time. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Location types.** Small locations (~5–6 machines) put a **recycler within arm's reach**; a few very high-volume "punching bag" locations (~10 machines) might **keep bill acceptors and printers in the machines and skip a kiosk**. Val guessed those are ~**3% of the market (~100 of ~3,000 machines)** — a **gut feel, explicitly not data**. He expects most to move to **recycler + secure arcade ticket + FlexPort/FlexIQ on JVL's own network, no MuthaGoose**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Config (firm rule).** FlexPort **hardwired to SAS-2 → FlexIQ → Flex Recycler**; **no bill acceptors or printers in machines where a recycler is present** ("bill acceptors cannot sit in machines if a recycler is present"). IQ is still kept **alongside** a recycler for a full modular set and **redundancy** (either can pick up cloud-hub connectivity) at low incremental cost, and lets the location run consoles directly on IQ. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Player / skill-pay flow.** Player loads money at the **recycler**, scans a **QR** at the machine via the **FlexPort optical reader**, and credit loads. On cashout, once **INCOM launches skill-pay-card conversion in the fall**: money → gift card → buys skill card → skill card cashed out → site dispenses cash. If not cashing out, the same happens at **IQ**: player scans the ticket at IQ, the cashier sees the transaction and can **manually activate a skill-pay card from a stack** and hand it over. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Skill-pay card.** Can be **read at the recycler and converted to game credits**, or act like an **ATM**. It can work **cross-location** (issued at one operator, used at another with a GL system) — Val thinks the GL system may **not even be needed** if the location validates by its own principle. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Constraint — no ordinary credit cards.** This does **not** let the recycler accept ordinary credit cards; that is a separate track (Visa/Mastercard need verification). Skill-pay works precisely **because JVL does not take credit cards**. On registration, Val is unsure what type JVL needs; the INCOM call raised that such terminals must register as **ITM**, but Val doubts it fully applies since JVL's terminals do not take credit cards (unlike the TEM/ITM terminals that do). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]
- **Business-plan emphasis.** (1) **IQ**, (2) **put in JVL's own network**. Once JVL captures ~**30% of the market via IQ**, immediately try to move all locations onto JVL's own network, offering services impossible via MuthaGoose. **§15.8:** clear — escrow / transaction-model / device / market economics only (the ~$300 jammer, ~30%/~3% market figures, INCOM/ITM registration are product/market economics); no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]

They agreed to bring in **Pavel and Dmitry** the next day (Jul-22, 9:30 AM ET) to confirm whether MuthaGoose supports the escrow mode; Andrei to send Val the two call recordings and share excerpts to pre-load Pavel and Dmitry. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Escrow_Model_and_Owned_Network_Vision-July-21-2026.md]]

## Jul 22 2026 — the V1000 LTE-modem work goes persona-driven: don't look at the code, start from ~50 personas

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-22-lte-modem-claude-prompt-review.md|Jul-22 7:03 AM call]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] and [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], ~25m, Russian), reviewing the AI prompt/agent approach applied to the **V1000 LTE modem**, Val settled how the modem work should be reasoned about at the device level. The AI-method side (prompt/agents/skill, source-of-truth) is on [[wiki/topics/ai-assisted-development.md]] (Jul-22); the device/engineering side is here. Continues the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-20-lte-modem-jira-structure.md|July-20 LTE-modem Jira-structure]] work (LTE modem for the Flex/V1000 via an x86 platform SDK). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_LTE_Modem_Claude_Prompt_Review-July-22-2026.md]]

- **Do not point the AI at the V1000 code (decision, Val + Pavel).** The V1000 code lives in **SVN**, is not structured for AI use, and the AI would find nothing useful there — code-based grounding is only for **new projects where everything is in Git and tied to Jira**. Trying to fit the code to the workflow "for the thousandth" (V1000) is a dead end. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_LTE_Modem_Claude_Prompt_Review-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Persona-driven flow for the RAGE-platform work (Val's direction).** The current work is the **Flex cabinet for the V1000, built for the RAGE platform**; because it targets RAGE, the flow must start from **personas**, not code. JVL has ~**50 personas** — e.g. the **street-location operator** who buys the machines (JVL sells to street locations, not casinos), the operator's **service people**, an **accounting department**, and JVL's own **creative staff who develop new games** — and asks which personas could **benefit from the modem**. The chain, agreed step by step: **personas → use cases → capabilities → requirements → features** (features written from the **developer's point of view**). Requirements record how to implement the capabilities; only once a set of requirements exists do you start writing features. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_LTE_Modem_Claude_Prompt_Review-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Pavel's agent test found existing Jira capabilities.** Running the newly-generated skill/agents with almost no input ("I need an LTE modem for the V1000" + the July-20 meeting record + the modem requirements), the subagents (one on Jira, one on Git) reported that **capabilities for this already exist in JVL's Jira structure, with linkages** — evidence the prior Jira setup is being picked up automatically. §15.8 clear — device / persona-flow / build economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_LTE_Modem_Claude_Prompt_Review-July-22-2026.md]]

## Jul 22 2026 — MG↔IQ escrow transaction flow: escrow answered no, print-then-validate-in-parallel, and store-nothing liability

The [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-22-mg-iq-escrow-transaction-flow.md|Jul-22 ~9:00 AM ET call]] (~40m, Russian; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], and [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dzmitry Chudzinau]] / Dmitri Chudinov) is the promised next-day session that **answered the Jul-21 escrow question** and designed the MG↔IQ transaction flow without escrow. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]

- **Escrow answered no (evolution — resolves the Jul-21 open question).** Andrei laid out the escrow idea — MG holds a cash-out and passes IQ only the **voucher number, machine number, and win amount**; IQ shows it to the cashier and reports back on completion. **Pavel's answer was an immediate no** — MG has no such capability. Val said he expected that answer, and raised the discussion "one level up." So the [[#open-questions|Jul-21 open question]] ("does MuthaGoose support an escrow (hold-then-validate) mode") is now **answered: no** — JVL uses the print-ticket-then-validate fallback. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Val's framing — JVL is a guest.** JVL competes with the **manual PAX** process but is a **"guest" on an established business** that has run on MG for years; JVL cannot impose its own rules, so every transaction process must start from that reality. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **The agreed flow — MG ticket always prints, IQ runs in parallel.** The MG ticket **always prints** (returning to how it originally worked); the transaction **also appears in parallel on IQ**. IQ validates the transaction through **cloud services**, and only when validation **fully passes** does it issue a card. A completed card transaction is recorded in the cloud and becomes part of JVL's reporting. If a card cannot be issued, the customer still has the printed ticket and proceeds by the location's standard path — **JVL is not involved, and does not print a second ticket** on card failure. (Confirms and refines the Jul-17 IQ-down printer-bypass single-flow.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Store nothing on IQ, know nothing beyond completed transactions.** IQ **never stores** transactions (transient memory of seconds/minutes at most). All transactions belong to the location and live in the **cloud hub keyed to the location's identifier**, regardless of which IQ unit is installed; a broken IQ is simply replaced and a **replacement IQ "starts a new life"** with no prior history. JVL records **only** transactions that completed with an issued card; everything else JVL deliberately "doesn't know" — Val stressed this is liability protection ("we are in the banking business," US litigation risk). His example report to a location: *"You issued $100,000 in payouts; we made card transactions for $25,000; we have no idea about the rest."* Pavel's suggestion of keeping pending transactions **"under the hood"** for seamless IQ-replacement sync was **rejected** (both Andrei and Val: a replacement IQ knows nothing of what came before). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Commissioning — one location forever, no decommissioning.** There is **no decommissioning process**; an IQ is commissioned to **exactly one location** and can never move — if it ever connects from another location it is simply **no longer commissioned**. Today commissioning is a **manual dashboard procedure** done on JVL's behalf (e.g. by Andrei or Filipp); it will likely be automated to some degree later. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Master-operator dashboard shows only card-issued transactions.** The **master operator (business owner)** sees only validated transactions with an issued card, against a specific IQ device number — **no pending/live state**. Val's hook strategy: when operators ask for more visibility, JVL offers **free SMIB/port infrastructure**, which enables extra capabilities on JVL's own network ("that's the hook"; "appetite comes with eating"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **MG reports and the printer — JVL sits in parallel.** With tickets always printing, JVL **passes the signals through as if it doesn't exist**; the printer prints all MG transactions and MG can **reconcile its own tickets**, and cashier-initiated MG reports are unaffected. Andrei and Pavel noted JVL's IQ is **technically in-line** between the MG controller and the printer, but **process-wise and legally it is parallel** — Val insisted on the parallel framing. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Two MG + two IQ per location** (Dzmitry): theoretically possible — MG supports up to **~128 machines** per spec — but nobody sees a real use case; one unit is cheaper. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Above-limit cash-outs — resolved by not taking them (supersedes the split-into-cards idea).** The card cap is **$1,000** (InComm limit). Splitting a payout into multiple cards (e.g. $2,500 = $1,000 + $1,000 + $500 against one MG voucher, "#77") was discussed but **partial failure kills it**: if 2 of 3 cards issue and the 3rd fails (cards run out, InComm service down), MG's printer still shows the full $2,500. **Resolution: a cash-out exceeding the one-card limit is not processed at all** — IQ does not show it and does not light the card button. Dzmitry: better not to get entangled — it makes JVL dependent on InComm. **The same applies below the card minimum** (Andrei cited $5; Val said anything under **$7**): passed through, **not shown on IQ**, no card button lit. (Val: interesting problem, "I don't know how to handle it" — then chose not to take the transaction.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Word-choice discipline for a card-less end.** For a transaction that ends without a card, the team must pick a precise term — **"void" was rejected** (implies JVL acted); "cancel"/"abort" are candidates — being **pedantic that the wording implies no JVL responsibility** ("transaction passed or did not pass; if it did not, you keep your ticket, we have nothing to do with it"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Requirements are living.** Val fixed the agreement as of **today's date (July 22 2026)**: if JVL's competitive position advances, capabilities change or requirements are added — requirements are "a living creature." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **August-1 launch — LTE only.** All IQs at all locations connect via **LTE**; no LAN cabling. SIMs: buy **retail prepaid cards** (avoids contract-approval steps); Val accepted after initially questioning why not a regular plan. On tariffs, Val shut down the optimization discussion — ~**$50/month unlimited** is immaterial at beta scale (~**10 locations**, then 20–50); engineering time costs more than the savings, so **no log/traffic optimization now** — revisit at ~1,000 locations. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **SMIB resume — go-ahead given.** SMIB development was **stopped at some point** and only needs a go-ahead to resume; Val hoped for delivery in ~1 month, **Pavel estimates ~3 months**. Val was enthusiastic about restarting it. (Same SMIB the Jul-21 owned-network vision leans on.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **New capability for the current stage (not a rework).** Rather than reworking the capabilities created earlier, **Dzmitry will write a new capability** reflecting today's agreed flow for the current stage; Val cautioned against rushing into a mess ("take a day, clean up, think it through"). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Val's standing instruction:** do not hold meetings of this kind **without him present** — he wants to be at every one. **§15.8:** clear — escrow/transaction-flow/device/market economics only (the $1,000/$7 card thresholds, ~$50/mo LTE, ~10-location beta scale are product/market economics); no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_MG_IQ_Escrow_Transaction_Flow_Complete-July-22-2026.md]]

## Jul 22 2026 — Flex-IQ process-flow review with Juan: edge cases, the manual-entry / keep-memory reopening, and PAX as the true competitor

That evening on the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-22-iq-process-flow-review.md|Jul-22 5:00 PM ET call]] (~58m; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]) Juan walked through the morning's [[#jul-22-2026--mgiq-escrow-transaction-flow-escrow-answered-no-print-then-validate-in-parallel-and-store-nothing-liability|MG↔IQ process flow]] (which Val architected) point by point and raised his concerns — the review pass, with a Part 2 booked for July 23. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Process_Flow_Review-July-22-2026.md]]

- **Points Juan accepted.** MG always prints the ticket and IQ does **not suppress or intercept** it, so the location keeps its existing paper process; a **failed** card does nothing to the location's normal workflow. The team confirmed **"failed" (not "voided")** — void already abandoned. **Business Central posting is unaffected**: Nathan's money flow only ever depended on **successful** transactions (he tracks validated balances, not rejections), so recording only successful ones changes nothing. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Review-July-22-2026.md]]
- **The main dispute — the no-memory design (Juan pushed back).** The morning design records **only successful (validated)** transactions in the cloud and keeps no memory of anything else. Juan raised real edge cases: (1) a **large win** the location can't pay on the spot, so it asks the player to return later (a real "route" scenario, as in South Dakota); (2) an **IQ dying with pending transactions** on it — they exist only locally, so a **replacement unit loses them**; (3) **partial redemptions** — a $2,500 ticket only partly paid before cards run out. His worries: **no way to pay the player later in the JVL ecosystem** (manual entry is not allowed), **theft risk** on unresolved fractional balances (a $500 remainder disputed as $400), and locations demanding a **PAX backup device** — which costs JVL its **$2 per-transaction fee**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Review-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Juan's proposal vs Andrei's objection.** Juan proposed **temporarily storing open transactions in the cloud** (e.g. 3–5 days, then wiped) so they survive a dead IQ, plus **manual ticket entry under an administrator PIN controlled by the master** (cashier → her boss → the master, who authorizes with a PIN — for high-dollar jackpots). Andrei objected that **manual entry destroys the "fully automated" advantage** that makes IQ better than PAX (automation is the killer feature; masters love not worrying about theft); Juan countered that JVL doesn't dictate operator policy — a **master can simply refuse to support the feature** ("case closed"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Review-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Val's reasoning for killing memory, and his own doubt.** Val's case: with IQ **wedged as a guest into someone else's network**, keeping partial data invites scenarios JVL can't resolve, while a **memory-less design pushes operators toward JVL's future end-to-end network** (free SMIBs replacing Mother Goose), where full memory and rich capabilities become possible ("appetite comes with eating"). He also argued the **prepaid MasterCard concept is unrealistic on the street** — players want cash, and cards enable a **gray market** (buy the card at a discount for cash) — and that Georgia's weak lottery enforcement means the street will operate the way the street wants. But Val conceded he has swung **"like a pendulum"** across always-print/never-print and keep/don't-keep, that there is **no technical barrier** to keeping data, and openly questioned whether his no-memory assumption was wrong ("I don't mind being wrong"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Review-July-22-2026.md]]
- **PAX is the true competitor.** Val framed the real competition as **PAX** (owned by InComm, who explicitly won't develop it further) — JVL competes not on the sale but on the **recurring $2-per-transaction revenue**. Juan agreed and noted there is **value in keeping cloud data at least until the bugs are ironed out** (as a backup). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Review-July-22-2026.md]]
- **Points settled in passing.** **Sub-$7 intercepts are ignored, but a UI/UX cashier notification is required** (tell her that ticket must be handled another way — same for over-$1,000). On wins **over $1,000**, card-splitting works until cards run out, leaving an **unresolved fractional remainder**. **Device commissioning is automatic**: one IQ per location; commissioning a new device **automatically decommissions** the old one (no manual decommission step), with **InComm notified automatically**. Juan's technician-swap worry was resolved because commissioning always goes **through InComm**; Val floated a possible **multi-device-per-location cap (3–5)** for later thought. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Review-July-22-2026.md]]
- **No decision forced — deferred overnight.** Val was tired and asked to think the core dispute (keep-pending-memory + manual entry) through overnight; the group booked a **follow-up brainstorming session for July 23 at 8:30 AM** (Juan sent the invite during the call), and Juan will drill down with the team into the original three-to-five scenarios that led Val to eliminate memory. **§15.8:** clear — Flex-IQ process-flow / device / transaction / market economics only (the $7/$1,000 card thresholds, $2 per-transaction fee, PAX competition are product economics); no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Review-July-22-2026.md]]

## Jul 23 2026 — IQ Process Flow Part 2: no-memory settled, the "recorder" audit device, and hand-pay for wins over $1,000

Part 2 of the process-flow review ([[wiki/meetings/2026-07-23-iq-process-flow-part-2.md|Jul-23 8:38 AM ET call]], ~1h22m; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]]) closed out the Jul-22 edge cases. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Process_Flow_Part_2-July-23-2026.md]]

- **IQ keeps no local memory — settled.** Val held his line: IQ is a "dumb" transactional hop — a screen that automates card issuance, nothing more. It holds a transaction only in **transitional memory during the ~30-second validation cycle**; the transaction ends **validated or failed**, and **failed is a terminal state**. Only **card-issued (approved)** transactions are written to the Payment Hub cloud, tied to the location and retrievable by a replacement IQ; a dead IQ's un-issued pending transactions are gone **by design** — for security, so the replacement can't create a duplicate payout when the location may already have paid the player another way (Juan's point). Mother Goose tickets always print; IQ never blocks that path. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Part_2-July-23-2026.md]]
- **No manual entry, and no photo/scanner entry.** Manual ticket entry stays banned (it is the fraud vector operators and InComm complain about in PAX); Andrei's idea of **scanning a photo** of the Mother Goose ticket was rejected as unreliable — low ink / smudged digits can misread the amount. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Part_2-July-23-2026.md]]
- **The flow changes for Aug 1.** Implement Mother-Goose **ticket pass-through**; **remove the Void button**; the only action is **issue-a-card with 2–3 automatic retries**, after which the transaction is **automatically marked failed — no button** (failed = terminal). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Part_2-July-23-2026.md]]
- **The "recorder" — a new audit device.** Val proposed a small box like a **Flex Port board with no display**, sitting between Mother Goose and the printer, needing only **power and line in/out**, that **permanently records every transaction at the location** and never shuts off (persistent memory — "could remember things for a year"). It syncs to the cloud only through another JVL device (an IQ, recycler or dispenser). After an IQ outage a replacement IQ plugs into it, pulls the history, and JVL hands the operator a **reconciliation report** — "these transactions happened while we were out of service; you, operator, decide how to treat each one" — so IQ still keeps no memory. It could be **adapted from the existing Flex Port hardware** (which already has ample memory), possibly delivered in **~30 days**, and **rented (~99¢/day)**. Naming: the box is the **"recorder"** (a dumb recorder); the **"CFO agent"** is the software agent that reads from it (persona-clarity discussion → [[wiki/topics/persona-driven-design-and-orchestration-platform.md]]). Added to the product roster; it does **not** delay the Aug-1 pilot. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Part_2-July-23-2026.md]]
- **Pending-transaction timeout — unsettled.** How long an untouched pending transaction stays on the IQ screen before auto-erase: Juan suggested ~1 hour; Val floated (explicitly "shooting from the hip") **24 hours plus a 48-hour operator-resolution window (~72h total)** with escalating warnings, then erase and notify. Not decided. The screen should display the **countdown** and **highlight "dying" transactions**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Part_2-July-23-2026.md]]
- **Hand-pay for wins over $1,000.** Val's proposal (Juan endorsed it from casino experience): any win **over $1,000** leaves the regular automation cycle and becomes a **"hand pay."** It shows in a distinct colour (yellow); the cashier is trained **never to touch it** and to fetch the person in charge ("call Bob"). The **Payment Hub** (not IQ) texts the master operator's decision-maker an **SMS one-time code — one code per jackpot, expiring**, carrying the amount, ticket/voucher number and location; entering the code **unlocks only that jackpot**. The amount is split across multiple InComm cards (**$1,000 load max each, $2 fee per card**), and the split must keep the **last card at ≥$7** ($5 card minimum + $2 fee) — a simple programmatic rule. InComm has **no pre-approval step** (Dmitri confirmed the prior day) — a card either loads the exact amount or errors; a card that fails after retries is **failed/terminal** (a recorder could re-automate it later). A **hand-pay report** returns to the approver when the cycle finishes. The CFO can weigh the location's **$5,000 daily InComm limit** when approving (or ask InComm for a temporary increase). Val prefers **SMS over email** and wants the **market consulted** on the approval mechanism. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Part_2-July-23-2026.md]]

Andrei undertook to send Val and Juan a high-level summary of the agreed changes (with a basic mention of the recorder) for sign-off before it is unwound into caps/reqs updates for the dev team. **§15.8:** clear — Flex-IQ flow / device / transaction economics only ($7 / $1,000 / $5,000 thresholds, $2 per-card fee); no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Process_Flow_Part_2-July-23-2026.md]]

## Jul 24 2026 — recycler redemption: full at the wallet stage, partial from the card; and the cassette-skimming red alert

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-24-recycler-redemption-partial-vs-full.md|Jul-24 8:07 AM ET call]] (~48m; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Alexander Biratko]]) the team settled Alexander's development question — does the Flex-Recycler support **partial** redemption or only full? The answer **splits by stage**, scoped to the **Georgia recycler** under the assumption that cash payout becomes permitted there. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]

- **Terminology fixed for the recording.** The printed QR paper a player carries between machines at one location is the **"JVL temporary wallet"** (the "e-" was dropped) — it lives only inside one location, for a short time. Two cards exist: **Prize Pay** (InComm's current gift card, which replaced the old yellow Georgia lottery card; **$2** issue fee, not reloadable) and **Skill Play** (the new card, spelled "play"; not live yet; not reloadable). Skill Play's purpose: play at any machine, or get cash at an ATM. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]
- **Step 1 — the JVL temporary wallet is always redeemed 100% at the recycler; no split.** All money moves to the card; the paper QR wallet empties and is thrown away. Val expects **Prize Pay to become a virtual background step** with the player physically ending up holding a **Skill Play** card. Total player fees to get a Skill Play card in hand: **$5** ($2 Prize Pay + $3 Skill Play, the $3 charged only at the point of redemption). Val said InComm takes no share of the $3 (per John), worth re-confirming. Andrei noted FlexPay allows split redemption, but Val ruled it out as a precedent — **FlexPay was frozen a year to a year-and-a-half ago** and did not progress. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]
- **The recycler always dispenses a physical Skill Play card — no cash-or-card choice at that step.** Operators and locations want the card dispensed; it preserves the counter's final relationship with the player (the location can still hand cash for the card, the "unauthorized" but common Georgia practice, without the player going to an ATM). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]
- **Step 2 — partial cash-out from a Skill Play card at the recycler IS allowed.** The player inputs the amount he wants; the recycler verifies it against the bills it actually holds and, if it can't pay the exact figure, offers the **nearest feasible amounts** (e.g. "80 or 90"). The player never chooses denominations — the machine balances its own bill collection. A redemption fee is deducted on top of the requested amount (e.g. $85 + $2 = $87 off the card). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]
- **Cash-out fee is fixed per location, set at installation** (same model as ATM fees, $2–5 range, competing with ATMs; "strip joints charge more") — not dynamic, but the on-screen message must reflect whatever value is configured. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]
- **Per-transaction payout maximum is a location-level configuration** (perhaps $400–500; experience will decide), driven by **mechanical capacity, not legislation**. No jackpots are paid from the recycler; large amounts (e.g. $1,500) route to the window/attendant — Val expects every recycler location to also have a **Flex IQ**, welcomed by location owners who want big players in front of them. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]
- **Cards are location-bound.** A card issued at location A cannot be redeemed at location B — the **location owner's** rule (industry standard, like casino tickets in Las Vegas), not a JVL limitation; the recycler validates the card number against cards issued at that same location. Regular bank/debit cards and outside gift cards are not accepted. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]
- **Service and anti-theft requirements (for Alexander).** The recycler needs an **administrator mode** for filling and removing money (it has both; some models add a stock-feed add-on holding ~30–50 bills). The system must always know the **cash-box count** (cash box is separate from the recycling cassettes, ~1,000-bill capacity) and trigger a **collection service call at ~800 bills**, since locations are remote and every device carries a modem. **Any cassette removal (recycling or cash, power on or off) raises an alert**; because bills can't be recounted after reinsertion, an **unlawful cassette removal is a red-alert condition** — stop operation, empty the cassette to zero, recount, and refill before it returns to service. Val called cassette skimming (removing one $20 bill a day) "the perfect theft" this procedure must block. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]
- **Scope and timing.** Georgia is the focus only because it has the most clarity now; direction can shift if Texas or Nebraska becomes clearer (Val: "that's the beauty of a small private company"). Skill Play timing per InComm: originally Q4, latest word late August–early September; its API is called **"Alder"** and may not have been formally requested yet. Open questions raised: PCI / ATM-network rules (InComm didn't reply about JVL joining their ATM network, which could force recycler adaptations), and whether Skill Play has a PIN (unclear). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Recycler_Redemption_Partial_vs_Full-July-24-2026.md]]

**§15.8:** clear — recycler redemption / card-fee / device-service economics only (the $2/$3/$5 card fees, $2–5 cash-out fee, ~$400–500 per-transaction cap, ~800/1,000-bill cash-box thresholds); no named-person compensation.

## Jul 24 2026 — the Payment Hub "daily service fee": a per-location configuration carried to a GL account

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-24-payment-hub-daily-service-fee.md|Jul-24 10:00 AM ET call]] (~13m; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]) Val asked the group to explain the **"daily fee"** to close a knowledge gap. Nathan explained it is a **configuration in Payment Hub** assigned to a customer location — whatever fee is set there flows onto the invoice when billing runs; Juan added it was envisioned as an upcharge for extra services (enhanced performance, reporting). There is no live use of the fee today, but the group wants it ready end-to-end: a fee set on a location's setup screen must carry through to a **GL account in Business Central (MSBC)** and be traceable (e.g. a $5 daily fee showing up in the GL). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Payment_Hub_Daily_Service_Fee-July-24-2026.md]] The daily-fee field (GL **41421**) already exists in Payment Hub location settings; the group agreed it should **default to zero for new locations** and be activated only when a fee applies (Nathan to make sure Pavel's engineers design it that way). Juan flagged that account **41421 is mislabeled "ACH service fee revenue"** (Nathan to relabel), and that other known fees (wallet, insufficient-funds, rejection/cancellation) are missing from the chart — Val's instruction: **add codes for every known fee plus two reserved fields with proper GL accounts assigned**, to avoid another redesign later. The group also agreed a **consolidated daily invoice should be emailed for as long as an account is open, even when every line is zero** (fee awareness + reconciliation for both sides; it posts through MSBC as a zero invoice). The finance-system side of this (dimension codes / GL / COD terms) continues on [[wiki/topics/business-central-erp-implementation.md]] (Jul-24). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Payment_Hub_Daily_Service_Fee-July-24-2026.md]] **§15.8:** allowed — the daily-fee amount/structure and billing configuration are product/service economics; no named-person compensation.

## Jul 28 2026 — IQ pilot release priorities: remote update becomes priority 1, staged delivery, firmware-hosting still open

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-28-iq-pilot-release-priorities.md|Jul-28 10:30 AM ET call]] (~41m, Russian; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dzmitry Chudzinau]]) Val talked through the **Flex-IQ pilot** directly and re-set the release-priority list for the **Aug-1 pilot**, because too much detail was coming to him in writing to follow. Andrei had formalized the prior week's changes with Val and Juan, written them as short plain-language descriptions any non-engineer can read, updated the matching Jira requirements (changing ones that contradicted the new logic, adding missing ones), and shared them with the team that day. This continues the [[#jul-17-2026--the-iq-down--printer-bypass-fail-safe-one-card-only-flow-drop-the-mutha-goose-history-keep-the-location-running-when-iq-dies|Jul-17 IQ-down/printer-bypass]] and [[#jul-22-2026--mgiq-escrow-transaction-flow-escrow-answered-no-print-then-validate-in-parallel-and-store-nothing-liability|Jul-22 MG↔IQ escrow]] flow work. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]]

- **Remote software update becomes priority 1, ahead of ticket pass-through.** Andrei's original list had ticket pass-through first; Val and Andrei agreed to move **remote software update to first place**. Val was firm: he **will not deploy a unit to a location if remote update does not work** — he will accept slipping Aug-1 or Aug-2, but not this requirement. Val also asked that pending-transaction logic (priority 5) be done **before or alongside** priority 2. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]]
- **A remote update must not wipe pending transactions.** Pending transactions live **only locally on the machine** until they finalize with an issued card — nothing is written to any database beforehand — and transactions **belong to the location, not the terminal**. This creates the hard requirement that a remote software update must not overwrite them. Pavel confirmed a remote **application** update leaves the database alone (it only tries to apply changes inside the software); only a **full SD-card reimage** erases everything. Accepted risk: if a kiosk itself fails before its pending transactions reach the cloud, they are lost — those un-carded transactions simply disappear. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]]
- **Ticket printing is unconditional.** Dzmitry walked the flow to confirm it: the terminal sits between the game machine (Mutha Goose) and the printer, passes the message through, and the ticket **always prints** — offline, payment hub unreachable, licence expired, or the terminal not functional for the operator. The terminal intercepts and stores the passed-through data and later shows it to the cashier. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]]
- **Delivery split into stages 1a / 1b / 1c.** Rather than force everything into Aug-1, Val split priority 1 into **1a (Aug 1)**, **1b (Aug 20)** and **1c (end of September)**. For **1a** the accepted mechanism is manual: push the build to each unit, then ask the location to **power the terminal off and on** so the update installs on next boot (a background download triggered by the location manager / payment hub telling the terminal a download link, installed on the next reboot; no remote reboot control yet). Val said this is fine even at 5 or 20 units — "inconvenient, not a problem." **1b** would be a real system that tracks every terminal's version and pushes to a chosen terminal, ideally on a location working schedule so updates land outside business hours. A full **SD-card reimage** (whole OS from a physical card — e.g. for a kernel security patch) is expected to be needed rarely; ~90%+ of needs are covered by the remote application update. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]]
- **Where firmware builds are hosted is unresolved.** Pavel builds in **GitHub Actions** and hands Andrei a direct release link. Val asked why GitHub cannot host the firmware; Pavel said it would mean putting **credentials on every terminal**, which is insecure. Pavel floated **Dropbox** as fastest; Val rejected it outright as wrong in principle, saying he would sooner do it over the SIM card. Pavel and Dzmitry are to settle the hosting location with Sergei (and Andrei Bosik, back from vacation). Pavel also noted ready open-source update systems exist that JVL may pick from; Andrei won't object to reusing one if it costs no extra money and adds no security exposure. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]]
- **InComm is the pilot blocker.** Registering a production location still needs **master and location licence numbers**, which JVL does not have; InComm is working on a workaround to let JVL register a location without them. Andrei wants the full path proven end-to-end — register location, issue cards, receive invoice. This is the same blocker tracked from the [[wiki/meetings/2026-07-27-incomm-prod-location-registration.md|Jul-27 InComm call]]. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]]
- **The yellow-flag / GenPay interface improvement is deferred to releases after the pilot** — Andrei called it separate logic that improves the product feel but "we are not chasing it now." Process discipline was reinforced: Val pushed back on discussing solutions ahead of written requirements and asked the team not to write requirements ahead of need — changes must rest on requirements and acceptance criteria, and engineers should not act without them. **§15.8:** clear — Flex-IQ / release-priority / update-mechanism / firmware-hosting economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_IQ_Pilot_Release_Priorities-July-28-2026.md]]
